# Backlog

The living handoff and next-steps doc for the kit. It carries active items only. When an item is done, it moves out to a dated snapshot in `archive/` (`backlog-YYYY-QN.md`) rather than being struck through in place, so this file stays lean and readable.

Per-plan history does not live here. A plan's Chapters are its own append-only record and travel with the plan into `archive/` when it closes. This file is for cross-effort next-steps that do not belong to any single open plan.

## Active

- **Install the capacity reader and watch its first line (2026-09-24, operator-pending, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_capacity-gate_spec_v1.md`'s `## Operator Verification`).** Once the plan's pull request merges, pull main into D:\claude-kit and update the installed plugin, so sessions carry `hooks/capacity-read.js`. The first fable dispatch after that records a `fable capacity:` line. What reopens the work: a line naming an email, organization identifier or path, or a reader run that takes seconds.
- **Verify the interactive-only arm gate and the worktree boundary once installed (2026-09-25, operator-pending, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_kit-goal-interactive-only_spec_v1.md`'s `## Operator Verification`).** Once the plan's pull request merges, pull main into D:\claude-kit and update the installed plugin. In an interactive session, type `/kit-goal <a plan path>` and confirm it arms; in a persona session, have the model run `kit-goal.js arm <plan path>` with no typed `/kit-goal` and confirm it refuses naming the rule. In an unleashed persona working in a linked worktree, confirm a Chapter close runs `kit-compact-checkpoint.js boundary` and that `status` from the worktree shows the session's own marker with a position. What reopens the work: an arm that proceeds without a typed invocation (section 1), or an unpositioned marker or a compaction still riding to the safety ceiling after a declared Chapter close (section 4).
- **`test/memory-index.test.js:606` (a sweep writes only its own sidecar) fails intermittently under whole-gate load, twice now (2026-09-25 from `docs/archive/claude-kit_kit-goal-interactive-only_spec_v1.md`'s handoff gate, 2026-09-26 from corpus-compression section 5's gate at 79b7fb14), and passes in isolation (3 of 3 on 2026-09-26).** The assertion finds no change under the fixture's project memory directory where it expects the test's own hand edit (0 against 1). The likely cause is in the test: `snapshot` (:205) records each file's mtime and size only, and the edit at :620 rewrites the :121 body with a 45-character body of the same length. So the edit is visible only when the clock ticks between the two writes. The same-length bodies are confirmed; the clock-tick reading is inferred. Remedy to check before applying: give the :620 body a different length, or snapshot content rather than mtime and size.
- **The capacity reader downgrades on a window whose reset has already passed until claude-swap re-polls (2026-09-24, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_capacity-gate_spec_v1.md`, section 1's consult).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/capacity-read.js` compares no `resets_at`, so a 100 percent reading whose window reset after the last poll still prints `-> downgrade`. claude-swap re-polls within about 60 seconds of a reset in steady state, so the window is bounded by its backoff and dead-process cases, up to 30 minutes normally and 90 under a post-429 poll plan. The fix is one clause inside the two-file rule: where the binding window's `resets_at` is in the past, the verdict is `no reading (stale)`. Signal: a stall raise at an implementer site, or a compensated review, recorded on a downgrade line whose window had already reset.
- **The capacity reader can stall on a mapped or dead network-drive home (2026-09-24, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_capacity-gate_spec_v1.md`, the finishing performance review).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/capacity-read.js` screens a home spelled as a UNC share through `namesNetworkShare`, which a drive letter mapped to a share passes, so the synchronous open blocks for the SMB timeout before reading `absent file`. The fix is a drive-type check before the open, or a sentence in `docs/security-model.md`'s reader paragraph naming the bound. Signal: a capacity reading taking seconds rather than milliseconds.
- **The capacity reader's staleness bound may undercut claude-swap's post-429 poll plan (2026-09-24, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_capacity-gate_spec_v1.md`, the finishing performance review).** The reader calls a reading stale past three times the account's `pollIntervalS`, and never reads claude-swap's own `nextPollAt`. If `pollIntervalS` keeps its base value while a post-429 plan holds polling off for up to 90 minutes, every fable dispatch in that stretch reads `no reading (stale)` and pays the ladder. Confirming it needs claude-swap's writer of `pollIntervalS` under a 429 plan. The fix is to key staleness on `nextPollAt` plus one interval, with the 30-minute fallback. Signal: a `no reading (stale)` line within 90 minutes of a 429 on the active seat.

- **Make the sidecar's `unproven` verdict live and read its first week (2026-09-24, operator-pending, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_sidecar-verdict-split_spec_v1.md`'s `## Operator Verification`).** Once the plan's pull request merges and is pulled into the clone `~/.claude/claude-kit.local.json` names: end the sidecar daemon process by its process id (the scheduled task re-launches it within fifteen minutes) and confirm the next verdict record carries `promptId` `judgment-v5`; rebuild and reinstall the plugin so the installed capture hook renders the two sentences. After a week of v5 verdicts, read the rollup's per-prompt split for `judgment-v5` and re-sample a dozen `unproven` and a dozen `diverged` alerts for fairness. What reopens the work: a verdict record after the restart still carrying `judgment-v4`, an `unproven` item rendered under the old sentence after the reinstall, or `unproven`'s fair share reading no better than `diverged`'s did under v4.

- **judgment-v5 labels a pipe-exit route defect `diverged` rather than `unproven` (2026-09-24, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_sidecar-verdict-split_spec_v1.md`, section 1).** Battery judgment-v2 case 14, a check whose reported exit code belongs to a trailing `head` in the pipe, took `diverged` on all six v5 runs, each reason naming the pipe-exit defect in the words of v5's own `unproven` sentence. The operator ruled on 2026-09-24 to keep the case in the floor as a recorded miss and leave the fix to a later wording plan. Signal: that plan is written when the week-long v5 rollup reading the sidecar plan's Operator Verification names shows the pipe-exit shape still landing under `diverged`. The fix is a judgment-v6 whose `unproven` boundary draws the pipe-exit shape more sharply, measured on a battery version where case 14 must pass.

- **Read the leash's status surfaces on a real armed goal (2026-09-24, operator-pending, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_leash-status-truth_spec_v1.md`'s `## Operator Verification`).** After the pull request merges and the plugin update is installed, arm a goal with a queue of more than five plans in a checkout, then read `kit-goal.js status` and the next session-start notice. The notice should name every queued path up to fifty, and no binding line should print for a session that is not leashed. A notice folding past five, or a binding printed for an unleashed session, reopens the plan as a new round.
- **The checkpoint owner check restates the bind rule by hand (2026-09-24, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_leash-status-truth_spec_v1.md`, section 1's security review).** `isBindableSessionId` in `hooks/kit-goal-lib.js` is now the one exported form of the rule `bindSession` enforces (a non-empty string of at most 128 characters with no control character), and `normalizeState` calls it. `storableCheckpointOwner` in `hooks/kit-compact-lib.js` repeats the same test inline, and its comments call it "bindSession's own storage rules". The two agree today only because the copies match, so a change to one would let the checkpoint gate and the goal normalizer accept different ids with nothing to flag it. The fix is for `storableCheckpointOwner` to call the exported predicate. `kit-goal-lib.js` already requires `kit-compact-lib.js`, so the call needs a deferred require, or the predicate moves to `kit-compact-lib.js` and `kit-goal-lib.js` re-exports it.
- **memq's lock helper can recreate a project's `.kit/` without its ignore marker (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_kit-scratch-self-ignore_spec_v1.md`, the finishing goal read).** Every kit writer that creates `.kit/` goes through `ensureScratchDirIgnored`, which writes `.kit/.gitignore` containing `*`. `acquireLock` in `scripts/memq.js` creates its lock file's parent with a plain recursive create, and `jev-judge.js`'s `rewriteShown` hands it `.kit/jev-shown.json.lock`. On the `updateShown` path a `.kit/` deleted between the shown-file check and the lock comes back unmarked until the next write through the helper. `docs/security-model.md` names the route. The fix is for `rewriteShown` to call the helper on the lock's parent before `acquireLock`, with a test that deletes `.kit/` after the check.

- **The doctor loads installed-copy code from the shim resolver's root with no containment check (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, section 1's security review).** On a clone run the doctor asks the checkout's shim resolver where the installed plugin sits, then runs code from that root: it dot-sources `doctor\install-memory-sync.ps1` and `doctor\install-memq-shim.ps1`, runs `scripts\memq-shim.js`, and the embedder check loads `memory-index.js` from it. The only check is that the root differs from the checkout. With `KIT_PLUGINS_ROOT` and `KIT_PLUGINS_ROOT_ALLOW_CODE` both set, that root can be anywhere. `docs/security-model.md`'s `KIT_PLUGINS_ROOT` entry states the gap and the accident-removal framing that made it a deferral rather than a fix. The fix would be one guard, shared by the three callers, refusing a root outside `~/.claude/plugins/` unless the pair is honored, with a test per caller.

- **The doctor's Memory sync trailing judgment is narrower than the rest of the three-way reading (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, section 1 review rounds 1 and 3).** On a clone run, `doctor.ps1` calls the store's sync allowlist "trailing" only when a managed file reads `Drift`. The judgment hard-codes the two managed files, `.gitignore` and `.gitattributes`. It runs its leak probes under the checkout's rules even when the verdict is trailing, which is the installed copy's behavior being judged by the checkout's rules. And no fixture pins a `.gitattributes`-only drift. None of this diverges today, since both copies manage the same two files. The first change to the managed set, or to the attributes text alone, would misreport. The fix reads the managed set and the probe rules from the installed copy, the way the text comparison already does, and adds the attributes-only fixture.

- **A store file written by an older installed kit reads Foreign on a clone run when the marker line changed (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, section 1 fix round 2).** `Get-MemorySyncFileState` in `install-memory-sync.ps1` classifies a managed file by the checkout's `$script:MemorySyncMarker`. A kit version that changes the marker would make every store written by the previous version read `Foreign`. The doctor then reports the allowlist as untrusted and refuses to adopt it, rather than reading it as trailing an older install. No current version changed the marker, so this is latent. The fix classifies the file against the installed copy's marker as well, and tests a marker change.

- **Experiment: does a same-version plugin reinstall replace a damaged cache folder? (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, section 1 consultant ruling.)** When the doctor finds the installed memq payload damaged, its FAIL tells the operator to uninstall and reinstall the claude-kit plugin with `/plugin`. If the harness reuses an existing same-version cache folder on reinstall, the damage survives and the remedy loops. The doctor names no folder to delete because that behavior is unknown. Signal: one run on a real install, damaging a file in the version's cache folder, then uninstalling and reinstalling at the same version, and reading whether the file came back whole. Decision protocol: where it comes back whole, retire this item. Where it does not, the FAIL gains the folder to delete before the reinstall, with a test pinning the path.

- **A symlinked `.kit/goal-state.json` reads absent to the hooks but not to the doctor (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, section 4 review round 1).** The goal hooks refuse a symlinked goal state and treat the leash as absent. The doctor's goal-state step follows the link and reports whatever the target holds, so it can report a leash no hook enforces. This predates the plan. The fix mirrors the hooks' link check in the doctor's clone-run block and reports the state as one the hooks refuse, with a test on a symlinked fixture where the platform allows one.

- **The doctor reads an oversized goal state whole before reporting it as over the cap (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, section 4 review round 1).** Past the 65,536-byte cap, the hooks read `.kit/goal-state.json` as absent, and the doctor now says so. It still reads the whole file, because Decision 6 asks it to print the queued plans. A file of hundreds of megabytes would stall the doctor. The fix bounds the read to a multiple of the cap and prints the plans only where the file parses within that bound.

- **The security model's `KIT_PLUGINS_ROOT` entry does not name the status-line launcher as a consumer (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, the finishing docs curation).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-statusline.js` loads the widget from the root `memq-shim.js`'s resolver returns (lines 65 and 357), and it runs on every status-line refresh. `docs/security-model.md`'s gated-overrides entry for `KIT_PLUGINS_ROOT` names the memq shim and the doctor and not the launcher, so an auditor counting what that override steers misses it. The gap predates the doctor honesty plan. Remedy: add the launcher to the entry in one sentence.

- **The security model's paragraph on the sync report's printed lines overstates how few there are (2026-09-23, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_doctor-honesty_spec_v1.md`, the finishing docs curation).** `docs/security-model.md`'s sync paragraph (the one opening "The sync repository's allowlist is the boundary") says the FIXED branch is the only one reachable after the leak probes read clean and a destination resolved, and that two lines in the report are commands an operator pastes. The PASS branch, and now the trailing INFO branch, are also reached there, and the report prints more pasteable commands (`remote add`, `fetch`, `push -u`, and the trailing `<installed root>\doctor\doctor.cmd -Fix` line, built from a resolver path passed through `Get-SanitizedLine`). `docs/architecture.md`'s "every other reachable branch is a WARN or a FAIL" shares the first claim. Both claims were false before the doctor honesty plan; that plan added the trailing command line. Remedy: rewrite the paragraph to name every reachable branch and every pasteable line, and state which gate covers the resolver path in the new one.

- **The memory-database promote test reads the real clock across a deadline, so it fails by one millisecond under load (2026-09-23, from the memory record provenance plan's section 2 lanes).** `test/memory-database.test.js` "a promote runs under the curator login, names the record by its host identity, and hands back the row" asserts `call.budgetMs === 4000`. `promoteRecord` in `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memory-database.js` sets `deadline = now() + budgetMs` and the transport is later handed what remains, and the test passes no `deps.now`, so a clock tick between the two reads yields 3999. On the dev-plugin seat's machine on 2026-09-23 it failed 2 of 6 whole-lane runs of the six-file lane beside it on the provenance branch and 0 of 3 on an `origin/main` export, and 2 of 13 isolated runs on the branch against 0 of 6 on main; the counts are too small to separate the sides. Remedy: inject a fixed `now` through `deps` in that test, as its siblings that assert clock-derived values do, or assert a range.

- **The coordinator seat still reads only the contract board path, while the registry stamp audit follows a relocated board through the `board:` key (2026-09-22, from the kaizen code batch's finishing docs curation).** Decision 5 of `claude-kit_kaizen-code-batch_spec_v1.md` chose one keyed operator-tier record so that a seat and a hook read the same board. Section 10 built the audit's half in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-registry-stamp.js`. The seat's half was section 2 of `docs/archive/claude-kit_liveness-by-session-identity_spec_v1.md`, which was abandoned, so `plugins/claude-kit/skills/coordinator/SKILL.md` still says the board's path is the same on every machine and `plugins/claude-kit/skills/role/SKILL.md` still says nothing per-machine needs recording. On a machine whose board has moved, the audit and the seat read different files. Repair shape: the coordinator runbook reads the keyed record the audit reads, and the two skill sentences are amended to match. Signal: the first machine to record a `board:` key.

- **The curating-docs backlog template says an item ages from the first date on its line (2026-09-22, from the kaizen code batch's section 4 review).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/curating-docs/references/templates.md` says "The surfacing layers age an item from the first date on its line". Since section 4 of `claude-kit_kaizen-code-batch_spec_v1.md`, the session-start scanner reads the first ISO token in the item, which includes its continuation lines. The ordering rule the sentence supports still holds, because the title line comes first. The plan's Out of Scope list held its prose to one skill sentence, so the template edit, "on its line" to "in its item", is left here.

- **A reader given the output style and `executing-work` alone cannot tell which governs a message sent after an authorized push (2026-09-22, surfaced by the prose register plan's finishing probe leg).** The probe moment `pre-send-checklist-after-an-authorized-push` is ruled `send-without-asking`. On the `output-style-plus-executing-work` shape it read that answer at the plan's base and read CONTESTED twice after it, with the reader reporting that no owner is named between two directives: the output style's pre-send line asking whether an irreversible act was taken "without naming the rollback and stopping", and `executing-work`'s Commit-and-Push paragraph directing that push as the model's ordinary mechanism. The reader also noted that the blocker set excludes an act "no proceed-ahead covers" while neither document defines a proceed-ahead. The plan aimed at neither passage, so the intent test in `skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md` makes this a finding rather than a re-ruling. Two other shapes of the same moment, `full` and `doctrine-plus-output-style`, still read the ruled answer, so the gap shows only where the doctrine itself is absent from the shape. Repair shape: the proceed-ahead term is defined where a session reading those two documents alone will meet it, or the pre-send line points at the doctrine bullet that closes the list of ungated channels. Signal: the next run of this moment on that shape.

- **The operator reads the prose register's new doctrine bullets and confirms their shape (2026-09-22, operator-pending, from the prose register plan).** The plan (`docs/archive/claude-kit_prose-register_spec_v1.md`) added seven bullets after the doctrine's plain-prose bullet, one naming the register's three layers and six stating its structure, pinned byte-identical into the output style. The register states the operator's own reading preference, so no test can check it. The operator reads the bullets in the merged pull request against the shape asked for on 2026-09-18. A bullet that misstates it reopens the work as a new plan. Signal: the operator's read.

- **Read the ASR before-and-after against the shipped prose register (2026-09-22, operator-pending, from the prose register plan).** Where the operator brings the ASR before-and-after over from ASR-CLAUDE, the Expert seat reads it against the register the prose register plan shipped and records any move the rewrite made that the register does not state. A move the register lacks is a candidate structure bullet for a new plan. Signal: the operator bringing the pair over.

- **The sentence layer of the prose register is scoped to what the operator reads, while the register around it is scoped to every reader (2026-09-22, from section 1 of the prose register plan)**. The doctrine's plain-prose bullet opens "This governs everything I read except literal code", and the new scope bullet two lines below it says the register covers anything written for a reader, naming the plain-prose bullet as its sentence layer. So a session drafting a client document has one bullet saying the sentence rules reach it and one saying they do not. Section 1 left the seam standing because the plan's Goal fixes the sentence layer as "the doctrine's plain-prose bullet, unchanged", which forecloses the fix inside that plan. The remedy is one sentence: widen the plain-prose bullet's scope clause, or say in the scope bullet that the sentence layer is widened there. It sits inside the pinned set, so the edit lands in three files and the parity test covers it.

- **Count the overlap-red kaizen notes on or after 2026-10-05 and report the count to the operator (2026-09-21, owner: the architect persona, from the heavy-process claim retirement plan).** A session starts a suite on a clean process poll, and the testing-discipline red protocol sends a red seen with another suite, build or embedding pass on the box that passes on a solo re-run to the kaizen inbox, as a note naming which suite cannot share the box with what. On or after 2026-10-05 the architect counts those notes in the kaizen inbox and its archive and reports the count. Zero closes the question the count was opened for, and the red-protocol sentence stays. A count above zero reopens it, and reinstating any claim is a new plan the count informs.

- **Relaunch the fleet's steward on the installed kit and confirm it holds the coordinator seat under its roster name (2026-09-21, from the fleet coordinator seat plan's close-out; operator-pending).** The fleet's personas load the kit from the installed plugin, so the fleet coordinator seat plan (`archive/claude-kit_fleet-coordinator-seat_spec_v1.md`) reaches them only once the kit update is installed on SCOTT-CLAUDE and each persona is relaunched at a quiet point. Then check three things. First, `~/.claude/coordinator/SCOTT-CLAUDE/registry/` gains an entry whose `Name:` is `STEWARD`, whose `Role:` is `Coordinator` and whose `Session:` is the new steward session. No entry, or a relaunch instruction in the steward's first turn, reopens that plan's section 2. Second, tell the stand-in session registered as `Role: Expert` under `Name: COORDINATOR` to stop writing the board once the steward's entry exists. Third, launch a second session under the `SCOTT-CLAUDE: Coordinator` name while the steward holds the seat: the steward's next pass reports a contest and freezes the board, and ending that session clears it at the pass after.

- **The coordinator's contest reading counts claims per live roster row, so one registry entry whose `Name:` matches several rows, or a name several worktrees share, can count as several claimants (2026-09-21, from the fleet coordinator seat plan's section 2 review round 1, routed here because that plan's scoping decision fixes the per-row count and states no multi-row reading).** The roster-read paragraph of `plugins/claude-kit/skills/coordinator/SKILL.md` already resolves a key matching two or more rows to present for the prune; the contest paragraph states no equivalent. A self-named session under a `PROJECT: Role` name may now take the seat, and names of that form collide across worktrees by construction. The open question is whether an entry matching several live rows is one claimant or one per row. The ruling is the operator's, since it trades a manufactured freeze against a missed contest. Two neighbours, from the same plan's finishing reviews, ride with it. The contest's second source matches an entry's `Name:` against any live row, where the roster-read paragraph and the peer-sessions standing check scope to local rows, so a synced-in entry matching a remote or cloud row counts. And a stale `Role: Coordinator` entry whose `Name:` matches one live row that is not the seat holder has no exit: the prune leg fires only on a zero-row key, so that freeze lasts until a hand delete or the liveness plan's session-identity rekey.

- **Operator handoff from the memory database plan: drop `kit_deploy` on the host, and bring NEO-CLAUDE and ASR-CLAUDE onto the shared index (2026-09-21, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_memory-database_spec_v1.md`, Operator Verification).** The plan closed with three checks only the operator can run. The `kit_deploy` login is still on the host and was the login the version 4 installer ran under; dropping it leaves the five kit logins as the only SQL logins the installer created. On NEO-CLAUDE and ASR-CLAUDE the host certificate is imported, each VM's client config written with its own login, and `memq db-sync` run once; the doctor's `Memory database` step on each reads PASS with a clean publish once done. The embedder's VRAM reading after the Qwen context reduction is the third. A failed check reopens the plan as a new round.
- **The registry prune joins a dead predecessor to its live successor by seat name, so a re-seated name reads present forever (2026-09-21, from the abandoned `docs/archive/claude-kit_liveness-by-session-identity_spec_v1.md`, whose registry half this entry carries).** The role skill's exited verdict joins an entry's `Name:` against the live roster, and the kit's naming convention makes that name stable across re-seats, so the dead entry accretes in the fail-closed direction and nothing reports it. The archived plan's decisions 2 and 3 and its sections 1 to 3 hold the design: the roster leg becomes refusal-only, the heartbeat leg stays, and the harness transcript's modification time, located by the entry's own `Session:`, corroborates exit. Its claim-release half is gone with the claim. Promote it when a machine runs the prune, which none does today, or when an accreted entry is first seen on a board.
- **The shared memory database's `mem.Record.Author` column has no writer (2026-09-21, from the architect's re-anchor check of `docs/plans/claude-kit_memory-record-provenance_spec_v1.md` against the memory database merge).** The schema comment in `plugins/claude-kit/db/Schema/060-Record.sql` says the column stays null until the record provenance plan fills it, and that plan declares the fill out of scope. Filling it takes three changes: `collectRecords` in `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memory-database.js` reads the record's `author:` field, `mem.usp_UpsertRecords` takes the value in its JSON contract and its insert and update lists, and the installer's schema version rises. The operator then installs on the host under a deploy login. Worth doing once a fleet read surface wants to print who wrote a record another sandbox published. Until then the comment is the only thing wrong, and the value is already in the published body.
- **The `mem.usp_Health` walk over `mem.PublishRun` has no filtered index on `(SandboxId, StartedDt) WHERE ErrorText IS NULL` (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's section 8 review round 1, blind and performance lenses; deferred at the finishing pass).** The doctor is the only reader and the walk is bounded by about 90 runs a day per sandbox, so no latency requirement is missed today. Add the index when a second reader or a retention of years makes the scan measurable.
- **The decay scan's shared pairing spawns one sqlcmd per record and caps the block at 60 records (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's finishing round 1, performance lens; deferred as the spec's own shape).** Section 4's text names the per-record shape, and one batch per pairs block is a mechanism no section names. A `usp_Nearest` variant taking a table of vectors would let the block run in one spawn; the cap goes with it.
- **The memory database client pays a health probe spawn and a query spawn on every interactive read, and caches no negative reachability answer (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's finishing round 1, performance lens).** `memq find` and `recall` each spawn sqlcmd twice on a reachable host and time out twice on an unreachable one. Folding the probe into the query batch, or caching an unreachable answer for the interactive budget's span, halves the spawn count; both are outside the plan's acceptance and are recorded here.
- **The fleet block is not printed at compaction, and two publishers on one machine are not excluded from each other (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's finishing round 1, adversarial lens).** The session-start hook prints the fleet block at startup and resume and not at a compact-time restart, so a long session loses it. Two `memq db-sync` runs on one machine serialize on the host's publish lock and on SQLite's busy wait, and nothing above that refuses the second. Neither is a defect against a stated requirement.
- **`embedRecords` screens no width on the vectors the host returns, and the search procedure's CTE names `Repeats` and the caller function `CallerSandbox` read as claims they are not (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's finishing round 1, security and adversarial lenses).** A host answering a vector of the wrong width is refused by the database's `VECTOR` column, one batch later than a client screen would refuse it. The two names are house-style Minors on a shipped procedure and a shipped function.
- **The installer passes the installing password to every sqlcmd spawn through `SQLCMDPASSWORD` in that spawn's environment (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's finishing round 1, security lens).** Every child inherits the variable for its lifetime. The alternative, a per-spawn environment holding it and nothing else, is a small change to `Invoke-InstallerBatch`; the installer is run by hand on a trusted machine, which is why it was deferred.
- **The memq shim and Embedder doctor steps report INFO when node is unresolved, the reading the memory database plan's step moved to WARN (2026-09-21, from that plan's finishing round 2, adversarial lens).** A check that measured nothing has no expected absence to report. The two sibling steps sit outside that plan's scope and keep their INFO; the hook check above them FAILs the run on the same cause, so the doctor's exit is right and the two lines are not.
- **The memory-system skill's new database prose has no rationale ledger entries (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's docs curation, drift item D15).** `skills/memory-system/references/rationale-ledger.md` carries no entry mentioning `db-sync`, `db-promote`, `db-curate` or the memory database, while `docs/rationale-ledgers.md` claims one entry per claim. The section on the database layer and the three verb rows each owe entries under the skill's heading.
- **The doctor's Memory database step pays two full host timeouts on an unreachable host where one would do (2026-09-21, from the memory database plan's section 5 review round 1, performance lens; deferred because the bound it measures against is assumed rather than stated by the plan).** `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/doctor.ps1`'s step runs the host probe under `-Quick`, which spends the config's `timeoutMs` discovering a dead host, then calls `usp_Health` through the client at the same `timeoutMs` plus the two-second kill overshoot to discover the same silence, about 22 seconds at the default against 12 if the second call were floored or skipped when the probe already failed. The verdict cannot change after the probe's FAIL, and the queue count `hostHealth` also returns is computed before its host call. Remedy: when the probe exits non-zero, hand `hostHealth` the probe's own budget or skip the host call and keep the queue count. Weigh at the finishing pass beside the doctor's other interactive-path costs.
- **A comment inside the memory-recognition nudge enumerates ten of the strict agent class's eleven members (2026-09-20, from the reviewer-reranking plan's section 4 review round 3, routed here because the file sits outside that section's scope).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memory-recognition-nudge.js:78-81` names the kit's read-only judgment seats as the blind, adversarial, security and performance reviewers, the consultant, the blind reader, the prose reviewer, the council member, the design facilitator and the scope adjudicator, which is ten. The class that comment describes is the strict policy class at `kit-agent-identity-lib.js:125`, which holds eleven, and the `plan-reviewer` is the one member the comment omits. The omission predates this branch: at base commit `19e626ba` the comment named nine against a strict class of ten, and this plan's section 3 added the performance reviewer to both, so the gap was nine-against-ten before and is ten-against-eleven now. Nothing behavioural turns on it, because the comment explains why that class receives no nudge while the suppression itself reads the policy class rather than the comment. What it costs is a reader who counts the comment's list and concludes the plan reviewer is nudged. Repair shape: one edit naming the plan reviewer in that sentence, taken by the next plan that opens the file. Nothing pins it today, by that design rather than by any limit on what a gate could read. `test/memory-recognition-nudge.test.js` pins the hook's behaviour, and the suppression reads the policy class rather than the comment, so the comment's roster goes unread and the repair needs an author rather than a red test. A derived-enumeration sweep over the policy class could reach it, which is what makes this a choice about whether the comment is worth an instrument. Signal: the next edit to that hook.

- **The dropped-qualifier class reached nine instances inside one plan with no instrument catching it, and the one candidate instrument was refused on that plan's own form (2026-09-20, from the reviewer-reranking plan's section 4 review round 3).** The class is a rule stated on an owning surface and restated on another surface with a qualifier the owner carries left off, so the restatement silently retracts a condition nobody meant to retract. Nine instances landed inside that one plan. Its standing amendment 2 was extended at section 4's round-1 adjudication to name exactly this shape, rode verbatim on the two rounds after it, and prevented neither of the two that followed. The review loop's own recurrence rule carries a qualifier that bounds what it settles. It reads that an amendment is a behavior class's remedy alone, and that a second instance of a claims class, the exception above standing on the behavior side, takes a mechanical check or a deletion sweep and never an amendment. That exception is a claim on a published contract surface, and it holds such a claim to a behavior finding's bar in two cases only: where the sentence sits in the section's own delta and contradicts an acceptance bullet, Goal sentence or Intent clause the finding's trace quotes, or where it is a pointer that delta left aimed at nothing. Most instances of this class do sit on such a surface, a README, a skill, a charter or a document under `docs/`. Sitting there is necessary and not sufficient, so the surface alone puts none of them on the behavior side, and an instance whose trace quotes no such clause rates Minor whatever it arrived as. What the rule settles is that those instances owe a mechanical check rather than a third amendment. What is unsettled is the instrument's shape. The one candidate that plan could have taken, a parity pin over the `## Threat model` heading literal, which fourteen tracked files carry and nine once the two plan docs, the plans index, the rationale ledger and this file itself come out, went to the scope adjudicator and returned `REFUSE` on a form ground: that plan asks for prose at both ends, the section on the writer side and the charter's absent path on the reader side, so a pin is a mechanism its own what does not name. That refusal is bounded to that plan and says nothing about whether the kit's review loop should own such a check. Repair shape: decide whether a restatement check is worth an instrument, and if so what it keys on. A qualifier is not a heading literal, so a byte-identity pin over restated sentences is the obvious candidate and the hardest to scope, since the kit deliberately restates rules in narrowed form on lower surfaces and such a pin cannot tell a narrowing from a drop. Signal: a reviewer finding an instance outside a plan already tracking the class. The eighth and ninth instances landed inside the plan that raised this item, so an instance count is no longer the trigger.

- **A test pins five of the scope adjudicator's prose sentences byte for byte against a base ref that stops meaning anything once this plan merges (2026-09-20, from the reviewer-reranking plan's section 3 review round 1, justified-not-fixed there).** `test/review-loop-provenance.test.js` holds `BASE_REF_BUCKET_SENTENCES`, five whole lines of `plugins/claude-kit/agents/scope-adjudicator.md` read from commit `4749efe8` at authoring, and asserts each occurs exactly once at head. Its stated subject is that section 3 added the relevance shape beside the two existing question shapes without touching either, which is what the plan's own Tests line asked it to lock, so the pin is correct for the life of the plan. What it cannot do is age well. Once the plan merges, `4749efe8` names nothing a reader can place, and any later edit to the single-finding `REFUSE` bullet or to the `ACCEPT-AND-DECLARE` bullet reds a test whose message says the section did not touch them, which sends the next author hunting a change that is not there. It was left standing in section 3 because narrowing it would shrink a floor the plan set, and a Tests line is a floor an implementer may extend and never cut. Repair shape: once the plan is merged and archived, replace the five-sentence pin with a pin on the closed-at-three line and the three bucket leads, keeping the relevance-section pins and the mixed-vocabulary control, which together hold the property that matters (the two vocabularies never mix on one ruling) without pinning curated prose no identity contract covers. Signal: the reviewer-reranking plan reaching Complete and archived, or the first red from that pin on an edit nobody made to those two bullets.

- **Two intermittent reds outside any changeset showed in one whole gate on SCOTT-CLAUDE and were green solo (2026-09-19, from the hook-dispatcher plan's finishing gate).** `test/memory-session.test.js` red at `:2204` ("a spawn that never records an outcome surfaces even over a frozen ok state", asserting exactly one `Kit memory sync:` block and reading 0) in the whole gate, then red at `:1906` ("the hook's sync check runs no git fetch", same assertion shape, 0 blocks) on the first solo re-run, then 83/83 on the second; `test/session-start-goal.test.js:901` ("a short-formed cwd still matches the tree git names in long form") red in the gate and 50/50 solo. Neither file changed since `aa41f7b0`, and neither references the dispatcher. The gate ran from a linked worktree under load (22.5 min against a 7.5 min baseline) beside `memory-sync.test.js`, which spawns serial `sync-store.ps1` children for thirteen minutes; the memory-session reds both assert a sync block appeared on tests six to seven seconds long, so a timing dependence on the loaded box is the inference and nothing confirmed it. The short-formed-cwd red is plausibly a linked-worktree artifact of the same kind as the two the baseline memory already names, unconfirmed. Repair shape: the red protocol in the testing-discipline skill, run when either red repeats: isolate, repeat, capture the run's own diagnostics, and either root-cause or file the flake with its mechanism; if the memory-session pair turns on `memory-sync.test.js` running beside it, that is a contention-lane candidate. Signal: either test red again in any gate. Repeated 2026-09-20 in the same plan's final whole gate (3563 tests, 6 fail, 23 min from the linked worktree under this session's own claim, no foreign runner live): `test/memory-session.test.js:2204` red again, now 2 of 2 whole gates, joined by `:2304` ("off Windows a dirty-only store with an upstream gets the commit-clause sentence alone") and `test/session-start-plugin-view.test.js:326` ("a HEAD that is an ancestor of the install leaves the checkout behind, with no remedy"); all three green in a solo lane over their files (116 tests, 115 pass, the one fail the memq-shim standing red). `:901` was green in that gate. The under-load shape now has three members across three hooks' tests, so the contention-lane candidate is the stronger reading and the red protocol's isolate-and-repeat step is owed by the next session that holds the box alone.
- **Four comment lines inside routed hooks still say the tool matcher lives in `hooks.json`, where `dispatch-table.json` now holds it (2026-09-19, from the hook-dispatcher plan's section 2 review, routed here because that plan's Out of Scope bars any edit to a routed hook's own file).** `memory-recognition-nudge.js:103` ("would decide in `hooks.json` which memories can ever fire", on the tool registrations; its line 112 on `SubagentStart` stays true), `compact-deferral-nudge.js:70`, `chapter-boundary-nudge.js:36` ("The hooks.json matcher already scopes this") and `memory-frontmatter-guard.js:17` ("Write, Edit, MultiEdit in hooks.json"). Each states a fact about where the registration sits and nothing about behaviour, so a reader following the pointer finds the guard's matcher in the wrong file. Repair shape: one edit per line naming the table, taken by the next plan that opens any of those four files or by a one-line sweep with the four files' own test lanes as the gate. Signal: the next edit to any of the four files.
- **The design stop does not read a standing kit requirement as naming a mechanism, and the first real instance is what reopens it (2026-09-19, decided by the operator at the keyboard; from the goal-fit plan’s close-out).** The design stop fires when a fix would add a mechanism that no goal sentence, no Intent clause and no acceptance bullet asks for. A guard the kit itself requires is named by none of those three, so a needed guard trips the stop. The operator ruled no for now, on this reasoning: the goal-fit plan ran its own stop twice and the judge ruled the mechanism in scope both times, so the stop is not over-firing on the evidence so far, and there is no recorded instance of a standing-requirement guard actually being stopped. Widening a rule on zero instances is the speculative addition that plan exists to prevent. What happens meanwhile is slow rather than unsafe: an implementer needing such a guard returns NEEDS_CONTEXT, which routes to the design stop, then the judge, then the ASK bucket, then the operator, so the guard is never silently dropped. What reopens it: the first real case reaching the operator with the instance in hand, and a rate near weekly makes yes cheap to justify. What yes would cost, for whoever revisits: the four implementer charters, three places in `skills/executing-work/SKILL.md`, and the judge’s grounds output. A fix was written during that plan’s finishing pass and reverted whole. Signal: the first standing-requirement guard to hit the design stop.
- **The goal-fit plan’s 1,168-byte growth of the executing-work skill stays, with the cut routed to the operator’s corpus read (2026-09-19, decided by the operator at the keyboard).** Those bytes are the plan’s own mechanism in that file, the design stop’s trigger and the add-decision line, so cutting them removes the feature rather than trimming prose. The growth is base blob 154,049 against head blob 155,217, both read on a single line-ending basis, which is 0.75 percent of the skill. The plan’s own Intent record warns that we are growing prose again, so the question is real; the operator ruled that a byte budget is paid across a whole skill by a reader who reads all of it, not by reverting one plan’s feature in isolation. The venue is the planned corpus read, "a long, sit together session where we go through each skill, look at each paragraph". Carry one observation into it: this plan grew the very skill it was written to stop people growing. Signal: that corpus read reaching `skills/executing-work/SKILL.md`.
- **No outcome-log shape records that a session paid for an earlier session's complexity (2026-09-19, from the goal-fit plan's finishing pass; deferred to the operator's corpus read).** The operator's framing on 2026-09-18: "the model solves for the test case in front of it. It sees only the time that it takes to write the test. That's all it ever feels. What we live with past that, together, in new model sessions, is the eternal wall clock burden that test added." The kit's outcome log records what a session did and how it went, keyed to that session's own work. It has no field for a cost a session absorbed that an earlier session created: a suite that now takes longer, a guard that had to be understood before anything could be changed, a mechanism whose existence forced a workaround. So the party that pays never reports it, and the party that added it never learns. Repair shape: a field or an entry type on the outcome log naming the earlier addition and what it cost this session, which needs the memory-system skill's own shape decided before anything is written. Deferred to the operator's planned corpus read, "a long, sit together session where we go through each skill, look at each paragraph", since the log's shape is one of the surfaces that read covers. Signal: that corpus read reaching the memory-system skill.
- **A closed set named by its size can survive the sweep that was meant to catch it, and only the named spellings have been swept (2026-09-19, from the goal-fit plan's section 4, review round 3).** The operator's ruling of 2026-09-14, recorded at `skills/executing-work/references/rationale-ledger.md` entry C067 under the `plan-reviewer.md` heading, is that a closed set is named by its membership rule and never by its size, "in the charter's heading, its description, its no-findings-outside bar and every surface that restates it". That sweep missed `docs/architecture.md`, which the goal-fit plan has since corrected. What is left is the coverage question the correction does not answer. A grep over `docs/`, `README.md` and `plugins/claude-kit/` for the spellings "six-question", "seven-question", "eight-question", "six closed", "seven closed", "eight closed", "six questions", "seven questions" and "eight questions" returns nothing live: the only hits are three executing-work ledger entries carrying `superseded-by: C067`, which record what the superseded rule said and are left by design, and one journal-layer line in the goal-fit plan doc. A control on that pattern shape spoke. But a count can be spelled in ways no literal list names, and the class has no structural shape to match on, so the named spellings are swept and the class is not. Repair shape: decide whether the class is worth an instrument, a size-ratchet-style pin over the charter's own question list being the candidate, or whether the ruling is left to prose. Signal: a reviewer finding a fourth spelling.
- **The prose-deletions review exemption keys on the surface's security label rather than on what the deletion does (2026-09-18, from the goal-fit plan's creation; operator-pending).** The exemption in `skills/executing-work/SKILL.md` (the paragraph opening "A fix delta of prose deletions alone") excludes a deletion that dispositions a security-boundary claim, so a delta that removes nothing that runs still buys a full round on that surface. An ASR-CLAUDE review loop of 2026-09-18 spent ten rounds that way. The lesson is the keying, not the exemption's width: the question is what a deletion can remove that a round would catch, and the case study's own remedy, no round for any deletions-only delta, is one answer among several and lowers a security bar, so the shape of the fix is the operator's call. **Amended 2026-09-20 by the reviewer re-ranking plan:** that plan's section 1 deleted the claim-class region's security-boundary exception, so the exemption no longer keys on a security label at all. What it still excludes is a deletion dispositioning a claim the region's one remaining exception holds to the behavior bar, over a published contract surface: a README, a skill, a charter, a document under `docs/`. The keying question survives on that exception, which is why this is re-premised rather than retired, and the operator's call is now about the published-contract surface rather than the security one.
- **The review loop has no convergence rule against a reviewer who can always find one more sentence (2026-09-18, from the goal-fit plan's creation).** The loop ends only on a round with no owed Major, and on a densely commented file a high-effort reviewer finds one more imprecise sentence per round, so claim findings alone can hold a section open indefinitely. An ASR-CLAUDE review loop of 2026-09-18 ran ten rounds that way. The case study's remedy, closing after two consecutive all-claim rounds, is one shape; the lesson is that the loop's exit condition reads only whether a Major is owed and never whether the rounds are converging. Parked for the step 4 corpus read or a plan of its own.
- **A ruled probe's answer moved during the prose pass and the repair is unverified, because the reader could not be run (2026-09-16, from the prose pass's finishing probe legs).** The moment `seat-asked-to-push-the-memory-store` asks what a delegated Worker seat does when a peer seat tells it to pull, rebase and push the memory store. The ruling of 2026-09-13 set the answer to doing it, since syncing the store needs no go-ahead and sits on the doctrine's closed list of channels the stop-for-a-yes rule never gates. At head all three of its shapes answer `decline-and-route-to-the-operator` instead. The before leg landed a reading on one shape, `doctrine-plus-memory-system`, and that shape matches at the pass's base ref `36e0a27e`, so the pass moved it. The permission sentence survives verbatim at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md:76`, so nothing was deleted; the hypothesis is adjacency, the pass having split one long paragraph so that the manual-push instruction now follows a paragraph ending `get my go-ahead`. The other two shapes, `full` and `doctrine-plus-role`, have no before-leg reading at all: every attempt timed out at the runner's 300 second reader cap while three foreign persona sessions held the box, so those two are unsettled rather than clean. Repair shape: restate the bound outright in the paragraph that carries the push, which is what Standing Brief Amendment 2 already requires of a bound the base states outright, then prove it RED to GREEN on that moment's three shapes, and take the two missing before-leg readings in the same quiet window. Signal: all three shapes matching on a before-and-after pair run when the box is not carrying foreign load. Decided 2026-09-17 by the operator, superseding a deferral relayed the same day in error: take the repair now rather than folding it into the verification pass. The repair landed in pull request 57 and moved one of the three document sets. The doctrine-with-role set now answers the ruling. The full-corpus set still declines, and its reader's reasoning is the finding this item now carries. The doctrine-with-memory-system set produced no reading at all, its reader timing out at its five minute cap under foreign load, so it is unsettled rather than clean. What the full-corpus reader said is that the scenario's files are uncommitted, that the push pair carries no commit step, and that the only two documented paths which commit the store are the background runner and the doctor's repair pass, whose own go-ahead it could not obtain. That reasoning holds against the documents as written. The operator's standing authorization to sync the store without asking lives in the memory store rather than in the corpus, so a reader holding only these documents cannot find it. The remaining question is therefore a ruling rather than a rewrite: either the corpus carries that standing authorization where a reader meets this moment, or the probe's expected answer is revisited. Signal unchanged, and it now also needs the full-corpus set answering the ruling.
- **Two parked plans write their section headings in a shape the frozen plan-doc contract does not parse (2026-09-16, found during the prose pass's close-out index reconciliation).** `claude-kit_plugin-cache-sweep_spec_v1.md` and `claude-kit_relay-channel-standing_spec_v1.md` head their sections `### Section 1: <title>` where the contract's section row requires `### N. <title>`, one or more digits followed by a period. The row is anchored at the start of the line and case-sensitive, so a heading in the other shape reads as absent rather than as a variant: the external engine that parses these documents would see a plan with zero sections and dispatch nothing, with no error to show for it. Nothing local reads the row, which is why it has gone unnoticed. Both plans are Ready and unarmed, so nothing is broken yet. Repair shape: renumber the headings in both plans to the contract's shape, and check the rest of `docs/plans/` for the same spelling in the same pass, since two of twenty carrying it suggests an authoring habit rather than two slips. Signal: the triage pass over the parked plans, which reads each one before it is armed.
- **Meaning questions the prose pass raised for step 4, gathered in its Chapters and travelling with the plan into the archive (2026-09-16, from the prose pass's finishing close).** The prose pass rewrote the kit's instruction corpus into one-idea sentences under a hard bar that it may not change what any rule says. Where a rewriter or a reviewer found that the old text said the wrong thing, said two things that disagree, or left a case undecided, the pass was forbidden to fix it and recorded the place as a meaning question instead. Thirteen of the seventeen Chapters carry a `Meaning questions:` line. Twelve carry questions, and Chapter 12 records that its section raised none the base did not already hold. Three of the twelve state a count on their face: Chapter 13 sixteen, Chapter 15 eleven, Chapter 16 eleven. Every one of the twelve states that its questions are present in the base text and that none was introduced by the rewrite, which is the claim step 4 should test first, since it is what separates a defect the corpus already had from one this pass created. The questions are not copied here, because per-plan history travels with the plan rather than living in this file. Read them at `docs/archive/claude-kit_prose-pass_spec_v1.md`, on the `Meaning questions:` lines of Chapters 2, 3, 4, 5, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 16 and 17. Signal: step 4 of the post-rewrite program, the read-and-intent review, armed and Complete.
- **The corpus reads with a more uniform rhythm after the prose pass, and the operator has ruled it into step 4 rather than a pass of its own (2026-09-16, from the prose pass's finishing prose lens; decided 2026-09-17).** The prose lens returned two Majors about rhythm rather than meaning, and both were bucketed as questions for the operator rather than defects to fix. Sentence-initial `So` rose across the rewritten corpus, and the spread of sentence lengths narrowed. Neither changes what any rule says, and the pass's own bar forbade touching meaning, so neither was actionable inside it. The doctrine names uniform sentence length as a defect in its own right, which is why this is a real question rather than taste. Decided 2026-09-17 by the operator: fold it into step 4, the read-and-intent review, rather than spend a separate pass on it. The reasoning accepted was that step 4 reads the same documents anyway, so a separate rhythm pass would pay the reading cost twice. Nothing is owed before step 4 opens. Signal: step 4 of the post-rewrite program armed and Complete, with the two rhythm Majors adjudicated in it.
- **The goal library's absent-and-damaged state assertions left the suite with the recap pin (2026-09-15, from the skill-retirement plan's finishing goal read).** The retired recap skill's leash-reading pin in `test/doctrine-parity.test.js` also held the only filesystem-backed assertions on `kit-goal-lib.js`'s `readGoal` and `goalStateAbsent`, covering an absent goal state against a damaged one. `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal-lib.js` still exports `goalStateAbsent` and `test/` now carries no hit for it. The plan's Goal asked for the reference's removal and said nothing about coverage either way, so the adjudicator routed the loss here rather than into that plan: four assertions belong in a `kit-goal-lib` test, or the operator accepts the loss.
- **Two rationale ledgers state counts the tree no longer matches, which the skill-retirement plan's Out of Scope kept it from fixing (2026-09-15, from that plan's finishing goal read).** The consult ledger's document summary still names `cold` among the consult's siblings and its anchor entry C040 still calls the list four lines long, where the list now holds three; two coordinator ledger entries still say the push-moments pin has six dependents, where it holds five. Each sits in an entry for a sentence that plan did not rewrite, which its Out of Scope excludes, so the adjudicator ordered the in-flight fixes reverted and the tidy left to a pass that owns those entries.
- **Finishing-work's pull-request open names no base branch, so the pull request can target a trunk nobody chose (2026-09-14, raised by the corpus rewrite follow-up plan's finishing pass, review round 8, security lens at medium confidence).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/finishing-work/SKILL.md:93` spells the open as `gh pr create` with a title and `--body-file` and never a `--base`. Read from `gh pr create --help` on the installed 2.97.0: with no `--base` the command takes the branch's own `gh-merge-base` git config where one is set, and the repository's default branch otherwise. So the hazard has two faces rather than the one the review named. Where the integration branch is not the repository default, the pull request targets the default. And a per-branch `branch.<name>.gh-merge-base` config, which any tool or earlier session can set, silently retargets it with nothing on the command line to show it. This machine's own operator-tier memory `gh-cli-on-scott-claude` already spells the open with an explicit `--base`, so the skill is behind what the machine has recorded. Round 9's respelling bounds the blast: the arm is a mutation that cannot merge, so the worst case is auto-merge armed on an unintended base rather than a merge landing on one. Repair shape: name `--base <integration>` in the step's own spelling, with the integration branch resolved rather than assumed, and pin it. Signal: an edit to finishing-work's step 7, or the next session that opens a pull request onto a non-default integration branch.
- **The rationale ledgers carry change narrative in reason lines at a scale the new authoring rule now names as wrong (2026-09-14, measured during the corpus rewrite follow-up plan's section 5).** A ledger `reason:` states why the text reads as it does now, so a date, a ruling number or a from-X-to-Y account of the entry's own passage is a defect; Chapter 5 of that plan recorded the class as "about twenty" and left it for the close. A structural sweep over all 26 ledgers reads 114: 79 reason lines carrying a date and 35 more carrying a ruling number, spread across 18 skills, with `executing-work` the largest share. Three planted controls spoke, including one confirming the predicate does not flag a `provenance:` line, where a date belongs. Read that figure as an upper bound on a shape rather than a confirmed defect count: each line still needs reading, since a reason may cite a dated external fact legitimately. The two this plan's own section 2 had landed in that form were rewritten in section 5's close pass, so what remains predates this plan. The item matters more since section 5 landed the authoring paragraph, because the corpus now ships a rule against this form together with a large set of passages in it. Nothing in the tree checks the class, so a mechanical check over reason lines is the remedy to weigh alongside a sweep.
- **The post-rewrite program (decided 2026-09-13): six steps, each its own plan, run in this order.** (1) Finish the corpus rewrite's rulings batch, recorded on the item below, then land its edits under one follow-up plan (charter alignment, restatements brought current, the ready-and-auto-merge and close-out reap steps in finishing-work, the stop-for-a-yes reframe, the probe pins and designed marks). (2) Retire the skills the kit does not need, as its own plan, before any prose is polished. (3) The sentence-shape and paragraph-length pass over the whole corpus, doctrine and output style first. (4) A shared read-and-intent review, section by section and skill by skill, worked with the operator in the same one-ruling-at-a-time shape as the rulings batch, since some sections need the operator's word on whether they mean what they say. (5) The triage pass over the parked plans and the kaizen inbox (the parked post-rewrite-triage plan is the candidate vehicle). (6) Resume the plans that survive triage. The program doc naming each step's plan file is written at the rulings batch's close: `docs/plans/claude-kit_post-rewrite_program_v1.md`, written 2026-09-13 with step 1's spec (`claude-kit_corpus-rewrite-follow-up_spec_v1.md`) and step 2's (`claude-kit_skill-retirement_spec_v1.md`) beside it. Signal: each step's plan is Complete in order.

The operator's reason, recorded as the lesson over the whole batch: the kit went off the rails because every takeaway (kaizen, dialogue, friction) was appended as the incident's details onto existing prose, producing conflicts and unreadable notes, where the intended process was to jot the higher-level lesson, re-read the doctrine or skill passage it touches, and rework that passage with the lesson in mind, cutting and adding as the lesson warrants, rather than a hard rule against length moving. Decided 2026-09-13 as a follow-up-plan scope addition: a lesson-landing rule at the kaizen skill's disposition step, that an accepted lesson lands by re-reading the owning passage and rewriting it with the lesson in mind, the ledger entry updated in the same edit, never by appending a sentence, with the size caps read as a check on the result rather than the tool that shapes it; the rule is placed under the ownership map (kaizen owns the disposition, writing-skills the sentence bars) and its wording is the operator's to review in that PR. This closes batch 2; the rulings batch is complete. Refined 2026-09-13, after the batch closed, in the operator's words: the rework is not only cutting what the lesson makes redundant but changing what the lesson changes about what we wrote; a lesson can fully contradict earlier doctrine, reshape it, add to it or remove it, and the move is to take what we were saying, look at what the lesson taught, and rewrite the whole statement with both in mind. The follow-up plan's lesson-landing section carries this as the rule's substance. Decided 2026-09-13, ruling 27 (raised by the operator after the batch closed): the doctrine's "a PR branch is frozen the moment it goes up for merge" is broader than the mechanics warrant. On a repository whose review rule dismisses stale approvals on push and requires last-push approval (both on in `protect-main`, read from the ruleset), a push to an open pull request can never merge on an old approval; it dismisses the approval and re-requests review, showing only the new commits. The hazard that stays is a push after the merge, which with delete-branch-on-merge recreates the branch as an orphan and reports success. The rule becomes: a pull request branch is frozen once merged, not once up; before every push to a branch with an open pull request, read the pull request's merge state, and a merged one sends the change to a new branch off main; a landed push is checked by the strand-check finishing-work and branch-hygiene own. The follow-up plan amends the doctrine bullet, finishing-work's strand-check and branch-hygiene accordingly; wording is the operator's to review in that PR.
- **Two curated size caps were lowered for files this plan never cut, and the operator has not ruled on it (2026-09-11, from the test audit's finishing pass; operator-pending).** The test audit's section 8 re-baselined the ratchet to the sizes its cut produced, through a named-path `kit-size.js sync` rather than a bare one, precisely so that untouched files' caps would not move. Two untouched curated files had their caps moved anyway, on purpose: `plugins/claude-kit/skills/executing-work/SKILL.md` to 29,485 words and `plugins/claude-kit/skills/finishing-work/SKILL.md` to 19,427. The instruction is that plan's own `## Related` entry, which says the re-baseline at its section 8 is where the review-tier-decay plan's unratcheted headroom is taken back. Three separate judge dispatches flagged the move as out of scope, each of them blind to that sentence because the scope adjudicator's brief carries a plan's Goal and acceptance bullets and never its `## Related`. What is owed is a ruling. Both caps have moved again since, and `test/size-budget.json` now reads 25,400 for executing-work and 16,426 for finishing-work, so the reversal is no longer restoring those two numbers: a ruling that the headroom should have stayed raises each cap above the size its file carries today and re-runs the ratchet. Signal: the operator answering, or the next plan that needs headroom in either file discovering there is none.
- **Test comments cite their own plan's review-round findings by number, which no reader can resolve once that plan is archived (2026-09-11, from the test audit's finishing review; measured there).** The class is 80 sites across two files, and it carries two shapes. A parenthesized tag, `(M3)` or `(m8)` or `(M7/M4)`, returns 32 sites, all in `test/kit-sidecar-rollup.test.js`. A bare ordinal, `MAJOR 5's pin` or `MINOR 4 (round 4)`, returns 48 sites, all in `test/kit-sidecar-battery.test.js`. The two predicates are `grep -nE '\([Mm][0-9]+(/[Mm][0-9]+)?\)' test/*.test.js` and `grep -nE '\b(MAJOR|MINOR) [0-9]+' test/*.test.js`. Both shapes are the 2026-08-26 item's class by that item's own meaning-based definition, a comment naming a plan's own part with no document to resolve it against. This class reached no item until now, and the path it fell through is worth recording, because a deferral chain is how a measured class goes missing. The test audit measured both shapes at its section 5 and deferred the widening to sections 6 and 7. Section 6 found zero sites in its own four files and passed the decision on. Section 7 ruled only on the numbered-amendment class. Section 8 split that class out as its own item and carried only it. Each step was defensible and the class still ended up named nowhere. Both files sit in a section that is closed and cap-synced, which makes the sweep section-sized rather than a fold. Repair shape: rewrite each site to state the finding's substance rather than its ordinal, and price the rewrite against each file's line cap, because a comment stating a finding is longer than one numbering it. Signal: the corpus rewrite plan being armed, or the next edit to either sidecar file.

- **Test comments and assertion messages cite another plan's numbered amendments, which no reader can resolve once that plan is archived (2026-09-11, split out of the 2026-08-26 plan-private referent item when that item's referent half retired; measured by the test audit's sections 3, 5 and 7).** The class is 27 sites across four files. Twenty-six of them a line-based predicate returns, and one it cannot: `test/kit-sidecar-battery.test.js` wraps `Standing Brief Amendment` at the end of one line with `5 does not` at the start of the next, so no grep over single lines sees it. That shape is named here because the same gap has now cost this class twice. The test audit measured a wrap-joined reading of 16 against a flat reading of 15 in this very file at its section 5, and the split-out that followed carried the flat figure alone. The predicate for the other 26 is `grep -rniE "amendment [0-9]" test/*.test.js`, which reaches all three spellings the tree uses: `Standing Amendment <N>`, `Standing Brief Amendment <N>`, and a bare `Amendment <N>` continuing a sentence. A predicate naming only the first spelling returns 8, so a narrow grep reads this class as nearly gone. Those 26 sit 18 in `test/kit-sidecar-battery.test.js`, 19 counting the wrap-split site at `:100`, 6 in `test/memq.test.js`, and one each in `test/compact-deferral-nudge.test.js` and `test/memory-frontmatter-guard.test.js`. Twenty sit in comments and seven in assertion messages, and all seven of those are in the sidecar battery. So the fix is not uniformly a comment edit: an assertion message is behavioural surface a reader meets at a red. The test audit left the class standing on purpose, so that every cluster was swept for the same classes rather than memq for two and the rest for one. All 27 now sit in files whose sections are closed and cap-synced, which makes the sweep section-sized rather than a fold. Repair shape: rewrite each site to state the rule the amendment carries instead of its number, and price the rewrite against each file's line cap, because a comment stating a rule is longer than one citing it. Signal: the corpus rewrite plan being armed, or the next edit to the sidecar battery.

- **No test runs `checkpoint open` in a bare-repo worktree, so the refusal is pinned only as a composition of two separate cases (2026-09-11, from the test audit's section 7 review; raised by the blind lens).** The compaction checkpoint CLI refuses to open in a worktree whose goal state belongs to another checkout, and a bare-repo worktree resolves its main root differently from a linked one. After section 7 applied the verdict retiring `test/kit-goal-worktree.test.js`'s own case, two tests carry the ground between them and neither carries the conjunction: `test/kit-compact-gate.test.js:2415-2427` pins the refusal lines, exit 1 and nothing-written against a linked worktree, and `test/kit-goal-worktree.test.js:368` pins bare-repo root resolution. The objection is that a composition of two tests is not a pin of the conjunction, and it is correct: no surviving assertion runs the refusal through the bare-repo resolution path. Nothing concrete was named that the conjunction catches and the two factors miss, which is why this is a backlog item rather than an overturned verdict, and why the verdict stands: the refusal keys on the resolved root, and both the keying and the resolution are independently pinned. Fix shape, from the blind lens: extend the compact-gate case to run against `makeBareWorktree().tree`, whose fixture survives at `test/kit-goal-worktree.test.js:111` with one caller. Not folded into section 7 because the file that would carry the leg is section 4's, already closed and cap-synced, and growing a closed section's capped file is the wrong direction for a plan whose goal is subtraction.

- **Pin an upper bound on `ENTRY_FIELD_CAP`, which today has a floor and no ceiling (2026-09-11, measured during the test audit's section 5; raised by the security lens as OWASP A09).** `sidecar/spool.js:76` sets the read-side field cap to 12000, and `sidecar/spool.js:242` cuts every field to it before that text can become a prompt sent to an outside endpoint in cleartext. The only surviving assertion about the cap's value is `test/kit-sidecar-daemon.test.js:789`, `assert.ok(spool.ENTRY_FIELD_CAP > 6000)`, which is a floor. Every other assertion in that file derives its own expectation from the constant, so it tracks whatever the cap becomes and cannot see a change to it. Measured rather than argued: with the cap raised from 12000 to 60000, a five-fold widening of an outbound channel, the whole cut sidecar cluster passes at exit 0, 468 tests, 464 pass, 0 fail, and no test names the raise. The audit's section 5 removed the one test that incidentally caught a raise past 50000, and the removal was right on its own terms, which the same measurement settles: that test's expectation came from the constant too, so it pinned no magnitude, and its detection was an accident of an arbitrary `'y'.repeat(50000)` literal that catches a raise to 60000 and sleeps through one to 40000. Restoring it would reinstate a detector that is wrong about its own subject. The disclosure impact is bounded and the bound is worth stating, because it is why this is a backlog item rather than a defect: the two producers that write real spool lines are pinned against literals, at `test/kit-sidecar-capture.test.js:597` and `test/kit-sidecar-battery.test.js:2439`, and the prompt cut is pinned at `test/kit-sidecar-daemon.test.js:518`, so the read-side cap is defence in depth for a hand-written spool line rather than the channel's only bound. Fix shape, from the security lens: one assertion beside `:789` tying the read-side cap to the write-side one, such as `assert.ok(spool.ENTRY_FIELD_CAP <= 2 * hook.FIELD_CAP)`, so the defensive cap cannot drift into a channel-widening one unnoticed. Not done in section 5 because adding coverage is outside that plan's goal, which is subtraction against the earn rule, and its Out of Scope routes what a read reveals here rather than fixing it.

- **Pin that a checkpoint with no `boundSession` key at all is refused (2026-09-11, measured during the test audit's section 4).** `checkpointMatches` in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-compact-lib.js:260` refuses a mismatched session through `sameSessionId`, whose `!a` test at `:181` makes an absent key and an explicit `null` one leg. Every surviving test in `test/kit-compact-gate.test.js` plants an explicit falsy value, so nothing pins that the absent-key shape is refused, and the absent-key shape is the one an older-format record on disk actually has. Measured rather than argued: with the wrong-session leg skipped for an absent key only, all 324 surviving tests pass at exit 0, and `checkpointMatches` then answers `ok:true` for a record carrying no `boundSession` beside a valid `openedBy`. The audit's section 4 removed the one test that wrote that shape, and the removal was right on its own terms, which the same measurement settles: that test asserted only a deny, and its record carried no `openedBy` either, so it answers `ok:false` with reason `wrong-opener` under the mutation and was green too. The hole therefore predates the cut rather than being opened by it. Fix shape is one row in the falsy-planting loop at `test/kit-compact-gate.test.js` asserting the reason rather than the bare deny, since asserting the bare deny is exactly what made the removed test blind to this. Not done in section 4 because adding coverage is outside that plan's goal, which is subtraction against the earn rule, and its Out of Scope routes what a read reveals here rather than fixing it.

- **The tree exports a spawn guard for git and for nothing else, so every non-git child process restates its protections by hand (2026-09-10, from the test audit's section 1 review; named independently by the implementer and the security reviewer).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-git-lib.js` exports `gitRun`, `gitOutput` and `gitChildEnv`, so a new git caller inherits the working-directory pin, the case-insensitive `GIT_*` strip and the array argv just by calling it. Nothing equivalent exists for a node or PowerShell child: `hook-canary.js` keeps its guard inline in `runHook` and `renderLines` and exports none of it, so the next author writing a spawn reproduces the protections they can see and silently drops the ones they cannot. The properties at stake are an absolute interpreter path rather than a bare name, an argument array, a timeout delivered as `SIGKILL` so a child cannot decline the bound, an explicit output ceiling, and `windowsHide`. What it needs is one `spawnChild(exe, args, opts)` exported from a hooks-level library beside `kit-git-lib.js`, with `hook-canary.js` calling it rather than restating it. Routed here rather than into a spec because it is one export plus two call-site edits, and because no current caller crosses a trust boundary: the bare-name resolution it would close requires write access to the checkout, which is already the trust of editing the file itself.

- **Nothing in the corpus tells a session to read a rule document's rationale ledger before changing the rule (2026-09-10, from the corpus audit's docs curation; the rewrite plan's call).** The 23 ledgers at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/*/references/rationale-ledger.md` hold the why of every surviving claim, and `docs/rationale-ledgers.md` describes them, but no skill or doctrine line sends a session that is editing a rule to the ledger first, so a later edit can retire a load-bearing scar for reading like verbosity, which is the failure the ledgers exist to stop. Where such a sentence belongs (the doctrine, executing-work, the kaizen pass) was for `docs/archive/claude-kit_corpus-rewrite_spec_v1.md` to decide, and that plan closed on 2026-09-13 without deciding it, no rule document naming the ledgers, since a line added now would be one more claim for it to rewrite.

- **Give seats that wait on the operator a longer liveness cadence (2026-09-08, operator decision on the liveness plan; enhancement, after that plan lands).** Under `claude-kit_liveness-by-session-identity_spec_v1.md` an entry is pruned when its heartbeat and its transcript are both stale past twice the seat's stated cadence, so an Expert idle overnight on an operator answer loses its entry and re-creates it at its next push. The operator accepted that cost on 2026-09-08 and asked for this as the follow-up: a longer stated cadence for the seats that wait on the operator, in the peer-sessions skill's Roles table, which the coordinator runbook reads the bound from. One line per affected seat, no code. Signal: a live seat's entry pruned while it waited, read in the coordinator's board.

- **Install the plan-review-and-recap close (2026-09-08, operator-only, from that plan's close-out).** Run `claude plugin update`, then the doctor's `-Fix` so `~/.claude/claude-kit-doctrine.md` picks up the Workflow-grant bullet's one-word fix, which the installed copy at a8d46ef lacks.
