What reopens the work: a spec written `Ready` after the update whose handoff recap line lacks `plan review:`, or a close-out after it lacking the recap block. The archived plan is `archive/claude-kit_plan-review-and-recap_spec_v1.md`.


- **Name the plan review in the expert seat's banked-moment definition (2026-09-08, from the plan-review-and-recap finishing curation; deviation).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/peer-sessions/SKILL.md` defines the expert's deliverable handoff as the spec committed, the blind read adjudicated and the dispatch acked, and `docs/architecture.md` mirrors that list; brainstorming step 10 now places the plan review before `Ready`, so a seat can bank a handoff moment mid-review. One clause in the owning skill and its mirror together, since editing the mirror alone puts the two out of step.


- **Two operator verifications from the review-loop-exit plan (2026-09-08, from that plan's close-out; operator-only).** First, run `claude plugin update` so sessions started after it execute the class-keyed terminal condition and the `[claim]` rating; a session keeps the installed copy it started with. What reopens the work: a section closed after the update whose Chapter records four or more rounds with every Major in the last two rated `[claim]`. Second, rule on the proposed probe `test/probes/review-round-returning-only-claim-majors.md` in the next rulings batch; its after leg returned the proposed answer over both shapes at sections 1 and 2 and over the whole corpus at the close. What reopens the work: a ruling other than the proposed answer, which is a re-derivation of the terminal condition rather than a probe fix. The plan is `archive/claude-kit_review-loop-exit_spec_v1.md`. Signal: the update run, and the rulings batch landing.

- **The sync verb refuses a named path spelled in a different letter case on a case-insensitive checkout (2026-09-08, from the review-loop-exit plan's finishing adversarial review; confirmed by probe, routed rather than fixed because the named-path handling is a security surface that owes a review round).** `node plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-size.js sync --repo . Test/Claim-Class-Parity.test.js` exits 2 naming the path as one the budget does not measure and leaves `test/size-budget.json` byte-identical, so the failure is a loud refusal and never a wrong write. The verb resolves the named path against the repository and compares the result with the budget's keys as exact strings, which is what a case-insensitive filesystem lets diverge. Repair shape: resolve a named path to the spelling git tracks (`git ls-files` over the resolved path) before the key comparison, with a case in `test/size-ratchet.test.js` that names a file in another letter case and asserts the cap moves, watched red first. Signal: a sync refusing a path that exists.

- **The memory store's git channel pins two repo-local keys inert and leaves the rest of the class open (2026-09-07, from the store git-channel guard plan's section 1 review; operator decision).** Both git child-environment guards (`Invoke-MemorySyncGit` in `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1` and `gitChildEnv()` in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-git-lib.js`) pin `core.fsmonitor` and `core.hooksPath` through the `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` channel, which is what the plan named. Three reviewers concurred that other repo-local keys execute a command on the verbs the sync channel runs (fetch, push, commit, rebase, add), and a second round added that the same class fires on the hook channel with no ownership gate at all, on `branch-reaper-nudge.js`'s `fetch --prune` against whatever repository a session opened: `credential.helper` in its `!cmd` form, `core.sshCommand`, `core.askPass`, `core.gitProxy`, `gpg.program` under `commit.gpgSign`, and filter and merge drivers bound through `.git/info/attributes`; the claim is from git's documented semantics, not measured on this box. Pinning the pinnable ones (`core.sshCommand=ssh`, `commit.gpgSign=false`) overrides an operator's legitimate global setting on every machine that syncs, which is why it is not done in the plan: it is a risk-appetite call. Recommendation: pin `commit.gpgSign=false` (an unattended store commit has no passphrase to give, and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` already makes a signing prompt fail) and leave the transport keys alone, recording the residual in `docs/security-model.md`, which the plan's section 3 does. A yes reopens both guards and their two-direction tests as one small change; `credential.helper` and the drivers cannot be pinned to an inert value through a Windows process environment and stay a named residual either way. Two smaller calls ride with it: the `core.hooksPath` pin also disables an operator's global hooks on the store's own unattended commit and push (kept as designed, since a hook allowed on the write verbs reopens the planted route behind the forgeable ownership marker; a per-verb allowance is the alternative), and the environment-config channel needs git 2.31 or later, which nothing in the doctor checks.

- **The POSIX-shaped home pins in `test/kit-output-channel.test.js` have run on win32 only (2026-09-07, from the output-channel eliding plan's finishing round 3; operator-only).** The doubled-separator case, the run-length pin and the F2 pin stage a POSIX-shaped home through the child's environment, and this box is win32, so their POSIX reading is inferred from the pattern and the fixture by two reviewers rather than run. A run of `node --test test/kit-output-channel.test.js` on a POSIX box confirms it; a red there reopens the plan's F2 delta (`archive/claude-kit_output-channel-eliding_spec_v1.md`, Chapter 3) as a new round.

- **The scenario-probe set waits on the operator's rulings batch, and two spec amendments ride with it (2026-09-07, from the scenario-probes plan's close-out; operator-only).** Every probe under `test/probes/` ships `ruling: proposed`, so both skill hook-ins' ruled branches are dormant and `tools/probe-corpus/run.mjs` exits 0 on every run today. The batched brief on the relay (re-sent 2026-09-07 02:49Z) asks nine rulings plus two amendments: section 2's pilot-reproduction acceptance clause replaced by the three-sample branch-and-pr evidence, and Decision 2's designed-mismatch class. On the answer: set each probe's `ruling:` line to `ruled <date>`, amend the archived plan's section 2 acceptance in a new Chapter rather than in place, and run the set once so the first ruled reading lands in a Chapter. A ruling that moves an answer reopens the corpus as a new round.

- **The prose bar has no sentence-level trigger where the retire rule has an explicit one, so the two halves of the subtraction-bars mirror are unevenly discoverable (2026-09-05, from that plan's finishing docs curation; the report's stronger claim is corrected here rather than carried).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/testing-discipline/SKILL.md:3` names the retirement moment in its own description ("whether a test already in the tree still earns its keep", "a test whose subject moved or was deleted, a suite audit"), so the retire rule loads at the moment it governs. `plugins/claude-kit/skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md:3` names no sentence-level or prose-audit trigger: not sentence length, not mannered prose, not whether a sentence earns its place. The curation report read that as the bar loading only where a session already loaded the skill for another reason, which is too strong and is not what the tree shows, since that description does trigger on amending curated prose the kit ships and on correcting a claim a curated document states, which is the moment the bar mostly applies at. The real gap is the missing audit-shaped trigger, which is the moment the corpus audit arrives at. Repair shape: add the sentence-level and prose-audit triggers to that description, matched to the sibling's phrasing rather than reworded, and price the addition against that file's word cap. Signal: the corpus audit being armed, or the next edit to either skill's description.

- **A committed kaizen note names another project's internal guard file and describes a working bypass of it, in a repository that is public (2026-09-05, from the subtraction-bars plan's finishing security review; escalated to the operator rather than edited).** The note dated 2026-09-04 in `kaizen/notes-SCOTT-CLAUDE.md` names a guard file belonging to another project on this fleet and states how the guard is got around. `gh repo view` reads this repository's visibility as PUBLIC, so the line is published. The finishing pass did not edit it, on two grounds: the line is another session's, in a file several sessions append to, and rewriting another seat's inbox line is neither that seat's business nor that plan's. What the operator decides is whether the line is rewritten to keep the friction and drop the bypass, or left standing because the guard it names is not a security boundary. Repair shape once decided: rewrite the note in place, since the inbox is append-only by convention rather than by contract, and remember that git history keeps the original either way, so a rewrite reduces discoverability rather than erasing the text. Signal: the next kaizen pass over this inbox, or any review of what this repository discloses.


- **Three prose defects in the writing-skills skill, none inserted by the effort that found them (2026-09-05, from the subtraction-bars plan's finishing prose review; routed rather than fixed under that plan's Decision 3 routing rule).** Two are confirmed at source in this checkout. `plugins/claude-kit/skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md:21` cites a dated audit anecdote ("the 2026-07-14 stabilization audit found a dozen drifted copies, one in outright contradiction") inside a rule, which is journey where the surrounding text is state, and the doctrine's own delete-litmus is what convicts it. And `:42` restates the count of its own table's rows in prose ("not just the four forms above"), which is the restated-count shape Decision 3 of the subtraction-bars plan repairs by deleting the count rather than pinning it. The third is carried from that review's report and not re-derived here: the review found the skill's list of forms narrower than the set the doctrine admits, and its fuller account lived in the review's inline report rather than in a kept artifact, so the closing session re-derives the claim against the doctrine's Style section rather than looking it up. Interim board 9's ruling 83 in the subtraction-bars plan doc is the provenance for all three. Signal: the corpus audit reaching this skill, or the next edit to its form table.

- **The size-ratchet suite mutates the process environment at module scope, so its isolation is a property of load order rather than of any test (2026-09-05, from the subtraction-bars plan's finishing adversarial review; confirmed at source, not a defect in what the suite asserts).** `test/size-ratchet.test.js:148-150` assigns `process.env.HOME`, `process.env.USERPROFILE` and `process.env.XDG_CONFIG_HOME` at module scope, before any test runs, because the child processes it spawns inherit the environment and the untracked-path reading depends on it. Nothing here is wrong today: `node --test` runs each file in its own process, so the mutation cannot reach a sibling file, and the suite asserts the redirect held at `:2467-2470`. What it costs is that the guarantee sits in the runner's process model rather than in the test, so a future runner change, a concurrency mode that shares a process, or a helper imported from this file into another, silently moves the redirect's blast radius. Repair shape: move the assignment into a setup the tests call, or state the load-order dependence in a comment beside it so the next reader does not have to reconstruct why it is at module scope. Signal: any change to how the suite is invoked, or the first test file importing from this one.

- **The Haiku implementer charter is missing the settle-the-test-question sentence its three siblings carry (2026-09-05, from the subtraction-bars plan's finishing prose review; confirmed by comparing the four charters).** `plugins/claude-kit/agents/implementer-haiku.md` carries no statement of the duty to settle the test question past the brief's own `Tests:` floor, where `implementer-sonnet.md`, `implementer-opus.md` and `implementer-fable.md` each carry one. The gap matters most at exactly this tier: the brief for a Haiku section names its gate explicitly, so a charter silent on the duty leaves the cheapest implementer the one least likely to add cover the brief did not think to name. Repair shape: add the sibling sentence, matched to what the other three say rather than reworded, and check whether `test/readonly-agent-guard.test.js` or the doctrine parity suite should pin the duty across all four charters so the next charter added is held to it. Signal: the next edit to any implementer charter.

- **Two corpus-wide prose classes the subtraction-bars plan measured and deliberately did not cut (2026-09-05, from that plan's finishing prose review; the corpus audit is their home; narrowed 2026-09-16).** The finishing reviews found journey-tense clauses and sentences past the length diagnostic in curated prose that the subtraction-bars effort did not insert, and a density pattern the reviews named separately: the "X rather than Y" construction used as a default sentence shape rather than where a contrast is load-bearing. Both are class findings over the corpus rather than defects at a site, and the subtraction-bars plan cut nothing by design, its job being to write the bars the audits cut to. The corpus audit (`docs/archive/claude-kit_corpus-audit_spec_v1.md`, Complete) changed no rule text by design; `docs/archive/claude-kit_corpus-rewrite_spec_v1.md`, which the audit's Section 8 wrote, cut to the targets and closed on 2026-09-13; the sentence-shape and contrast-density classes stand as its finishing pass's M4 ask to the operator. Narrowed 2026-09-16 by the prose pass (`docs/archive/claude-kit_prose-pass_spec_v1.md`), which rewrote the doctrine, the output style, eleven skills and ten reviewer charters into sentences of one idea: the sentence-length half of this item is covered for those documents and stands for every document outside that scope. The journey-tense and contrast-density halves are covered nowhere, since the pass swept neither class as a class and left a base-inherited contrast-density note as a Minor with its reason at its section 6. Repair shape: both classes are swept by a predicate over the class rather than by a list of sites, and the sweep's own control is a planted instance withheld from its literals. Signal: the corpus audit being armed.

- **Section 2's acceptance clause in the subtraction-bars plan is satisfiable by a reading nobody performs (2026-09-05, from that plan's own finishing review; recorded at close rather than repaired, since the section shipped and the plan is archived).** That section's acceptance reads that a fresh-context read of either reviewer charter names the classes and where their definitions live. A clause whose test is a reading no gate performs is discharged by judgment rather than by evidence, which is the shape the same plan's Standing Brief Amendment 4 rules out for a closure claim: where a class can be closed mechanically, the mechanical check is the closure and the prose is not. The section did land a mechanical closure, the cross-surface agreement sweep, so the defect is the wording of the clause rather than the state of the tree. Repair shape: when a future plan writes an acceptance clause whose subject is what a reader would understand, pair it with the predicate that a gate can run, or state plainly that the clause is adjudicated by a dispatched reader and name the reader. Signal: the next plan whose acceptance turns on comprehension rather than on a check.

- **The shared-tier `glob:` refusal is restated at both `triggers` producers instead of living in the gate they both already call (2026-09-03, from the memory-recognition-reach plan's finishing security review; one Minor, confirmed at source, not exploitable).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js:10533` and `:10815` each call `triggerEntryFault(one)` for the entry grammar, and each then restates the shared-tier `glob:` refusal on its own, at `:10552` and `:10820`. Both enforce it today, so nothing reaches past either copy and there is no exposure to fix. What it costs is the second-producer drift shape the doctrine names for a sanitising guard: the next writer of a `triggers:` line, a future `--update` path or a repair verb, inherits the grammar for free and the tier rule only if someone remembers it. The two copies have already stopped being identical, `:10552` guarding on `(toType || toOperator)` where `:10820` guards a bare `glob:`, which is the drift beginning rather than merely threatened. Fix as the reviewer proposed: a `sharedTriggerEntryFault(entry)` wrapping `triggerEntryFault` plus the glob refusal, called from both sites.
- **Two operator-tier records with confirmable handles are still handle-less, and no document names which two (2026-09-03, from the memory-recognition-reach plan's section 3 security review; routed to section 5 and never applied).** Section 3's review found two records that had genuinely confirmable handles and had been put on the handle-less list on an unconfirmed-identifier ground this checkout settles, and it routed the write to section 5 rather than making it at the time. The finishing pass checked the store and found that all 52 records Chapter 3 names as handle-less still declare no handle, so the write never landed anywhere. The two cannot be recovered from the plan doc, because that document names the pair only as a count and never by name. Closing this means re-running section 3's confirmable-identifier judgment over the current 73-member handle-less list rather than looking the answer up.
- **A trigger withdrawal is inferred from absent arguments rather than asked for, so an argument loss reaches the destructive form (2026-09-03, found by the blind lens at the fifth review round of the memory-recognition-reach plan's section 5; weighed there and deliberately not fixed).** `memq triggers <name> --replace` with no `<type>:<pattern>` entries removes the record's `triggers:` line outright, which is the intended withdrawal spelling. The hazard is that it is also what a truncated command looks like: the Windows command wrapper hands its line to a shell that cuts it at the first newline and drops everything after, so a narrowing replace whose trailing patterns are lost succeeds as a silent withdrawal, exit 0 with nothing on stdout. The shared-tier consent flag does not discriminate, because a caller typing a shared-tier replace types that flag anyway. Not fixed in the section that found it, on three grounds worth keeping with the item since they are what a future reader should re-test rather than re-derive: the loss is bounded and recoverable, the record keeping its body and its applied history while the previous line survives in the backup beside it and in the store's own git history; the shape needs the wrapper path plus a multi-line invocation to arrive at all; and the repair is a new consent token, a capability the specification never scoped, landing in the verb, its usage string, the skill's rows, four curated documents and the tests at the point where five review rounds had converged. Repair shape: refuse a zero-entry `--replace` unless it carries an explicit withdrawal token, so the destructive form is asked for rather than inferred, and pin both directions. Signal: the next edit to the verb's argument parsing, or the first report of a trigger line that went missing without anyone meaning it.

- **The memory-system skill's frontmatter `description` runs well past the documented cap for that field (2026-09-03, found by the blind lens reviewing the memq triggers change in the memory-recognition-reach plan).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md`'s `description` measures around 1817 characters against a 1024-character cap Claude Code documents for skill frontmatter. The overage predates the change that surfaced it and no repo test or doctor check pins the field's length, so nothing in this tree would ever report it. Two things are unconfirmed and are why this is a backlog item rather than a fix: whether the cap is enforced at all on the harness this kit runs against, and what the failure mode is if it is, since a silently truncated description degrades the skill's discoverability rather than erroring, which is the shape nobody notices. Not fixed in the section that found it because shrinking the description is a judgment about what the skill advertises to a session choosing skills, not a mechanical trim, and the same question applies to every other skill in the tree rather than to this one alone. Repair shape: measure every shipped skill's description against the live harness once, then either pin the length in the doctor or record that the cap does not bite here. Signal: the first time a session fails to find the memory-system skill by description, or the next documented change to the frontmatter cap.

- **Six index lines in the machine-wide operator memory tier name records that do not exist (2026-09-02, found while backfilling recognition triggers in the memory-recognition-reach plan; re-dated 2026-09-21 when the memory database plan's section 5 gave it an instrument).** `~/.claude/memory-operator/MEMORY.md` carries 248 entries against 242 record files, re-counted at the close of the memory-recognition-reach plan's section 6; the six names below are unchanged, so the gap is those six and the totals have simply grown with the tier. The six with no file behind them are `untracking-a-claim-file-does-not-hold`, `ollama-wedge-shapes-and-fixes`, `sidecar-divergence-alerts-are-a-mixed-signal`, `harness-snapshots-hook-config-at-session-start`, `shared-file-list-comes-from-the-diff-not-from-recall` and `a-retirement-leaves-its-mirror-image-standing`. None sits in the tier's `archive/`, none is tracked in the store repository now, and `git log` over one of those paths returns two `kit memory sync` commits, so each existed and went. The failure is silent in both directions a reader could notice it: following such a line returns nothing, and `memq recall` counts files rather than index entries and reports 242, so the digest and the index disagree with nothing saying so. Not repaired here because the repair is a write to a shared store whose right form is itself the decision, restore the six from the store's own history or drop the stale lines, and because the store syncs between the operator's two workstations, which puts the other machine's copy inside the question. Signal: the next store sync reconciliation, or the first session that follows one of those lines and reports an empty read. The instrument is `memq db-curate --orphans`, run under the curator login: its index-lines-with-no-record list is `mem.IndexOrphan`, which every publish fills from the same index-line-versus-file comparison, and it lists exactly these six.

- **A homing round is auditable one round at a time and not in aggregate, because the journal that records it is folded away before an aggregate question is usually asked (2026-09-02, from the board-routing-and-homing plan, sections 2 and 3).** A homing round publishes a coordinator board line to the memory store or the kaizen inbox and then cuts the line, and the record of that irreversible move is a memq outcome-journal entry written at two moments. That entry is complete while it lives, so any single round can be audited. The decay pass folds entries into per-key rollups at thirty days and keeps the tally while discarding the prose, so after that window the fleet can say how many rounds ran and not what any of them moved. The plan declared this a residual rather than fixing it, because retention belongs to the memory-system skill and no deletion-shaped repair exists inside the coordinator contract. The fork is the operator's: accept the tally-only horizon, lengthen retention for this key class, or have a round write its own durable summary somewhere the rollup does not reach, each trading store growth against how far back a cut can be reconstructed. Signal: the first time someone asks what a board lost over a quarter, or the next retention change in the memory-system skill.

- **A machine-wide seat's audit record lands in a project-scoped store, so the account of an irreversible act can rest where no later reader resolves (parked 2026-09-04, from the board-routing-and-homing plan, section 6).** The coordinator seat is one per machine and has no home project. The record of a homing round, which publishes board content outward and then cuts the board line irreversibly, is two entries in the memq outcome journal, and that journal resolves per call to whichever store memq's own ladder answers with. Readers exist and are real, `memq get` by exact key, `find` by key substring, `recent` in a window, and `recall` read whole at every seat takeover, but each reads only the store its own call resolves, so a round logging from one checkout is invisible to a takeover standing in another, and `recall`'s digest is budget-bounded besides. Section 6 established that neither available fix belongs to this plan: a read-back inside the round needs a machine-stable key, and naming a home checkout resolves a store location out of a machine-local signpost. The memory-system skill owns both the journal key grammar and the store resolution, and it already carries a machine idiom for a record, so the direction is a machine-scoped journal home or a machine component for the key on that surface. Parked rather than specced because it is a change to another skill's owned contract and the appetite for one is the operator's call.

- **A branch cut for a parallel queue has no standing signal when the trunk moves the plan documents it will execute (parked 2026-09-02 by the kaizen pass; from two same-day instances across two repos on 2026-08-31).** A worktree branch ran for hours ten commits behind the trunk while three of its own queue's plan docs changed there, two amended after the cut and one authored after it and never visible in the executing tree. The instrument the machine coordinator supplied is a left-right count against the trunk plus a merge-base range filtered to `docs/plans/`, one command per branch, read at session start or at chapter boundaries. Its caveat is what parks it rather than ships it: both halves read the branch's published tip, which is a publication event and not a progress reading, so it false-alarms in the alarming direction for exactly the interval between a seat's in-tree merge and its push, observed live as an eighteen-behind reading against a tree whose refresh was already merged. Candidate homes: the session-start worktree hints, or the branch-hygiene strand check, which covers the frozen-branch direction and not this inverse. Signal: the next time a branch-executed plan is found to have run against a superseded plan doc, or a design for reading the worktree rather than the tip as the progress surface. Decision protocol: ship only with the tip-versus-tree caveat in the instrument's own words.

- **The Ollama watchdog's state logic has no test coverage, and no human drives it (2026-09-02, from the judge-partial-input finishing reviews; adjudicated a standalone rider).** `sidecar/ollama-watchdog.ps1`'s strike accounting, corrupt-strike-file degrade, and foreign-owner refusal are machine-only contracts: nothing exercises them until the host wedges, which is exactly when they must work. The repo already spawns PowerShell from node tests (the doctor tests are the sibling pattern), so the state logic is drivable without the host; the `Restart-Service` leg stays honestly host-bound. Fix shape: a small test file driving the script with a stubbed probe (the probe URL is a parameter) through strike accumulation, corrupt-file degrade, and the owner refusal. Signal: the next watchdog edit, or the first real firing on the host.

- **The judge request sets no num_ctx, so the instrument's context window is whatever the endpoint's server configuration happens to be (2026-09-01, from the judge-partial-input sections 2+3 review; declined for that changeset with the reload analysis).** `sidecar/judge.js`'s buildRequest sends num_predict and temperature only, and `sidecar/config.js` accepts url/model/timeoutMs, so a server whose default window is smaller than a worst-case judgment call (~7.5K tokens at the 6,000-char caps) silently drops the front of the prompt - SYSTEM's verdict definitions and fence rules - while the schema-constrained decode still returns a well-shaped verdict. It was declined in-changeset because the deployed server is configured at 16K (worst case fits) and a per-request num_ctx differing from the server's forces a model reload per alternation on the shared serial lane, which is its own outage shape. Fix shape: an optional config field the operator sets to match the server, sent only when present, with the mismatch-reload hazard documented beside it; size the worst case against the daemon's read-side cap (ENTRY_FIELD_CAP, 12,000 characters per judged field, so a hand-written spool line can present a ~30K-character triple), not the hook's 6,000, since on overflow the server drops the front of the prompt - SYSTEM's fence rules - while keeping the attacker-authored tail. Signal: the next endpoint resize, or a verdict log showing well-formed verdicts with reasons unmoored from the intent.

- **The commit-locally-never-push moment the ownership map lists as unowned needs the operator's ruling (2026-09-01, from the precedence-and-ownership pass).** The map is `plugins/claude-kit/skills/operating-instructions/references/ownership-map.md`, its last table; the doctrine's intake-gap rule governs it until ruled. Recommendation: no commit-locally-never-push model is added. An unpushed commit is not a durable artifact under the doctrine's recovery rule, which is why the set has no such value; a seat asked for one declares the gap and takes Review-Only or Commit-and-Push as the operator names. Signal: the next session that meets this moment.


- **The judge sends the fenced triple to the model with no invisible-character strip (2026-09-01, from the judge-partial-input section 1 review; a v2 carry-over).** `sidecar/judge.js` applies `neutralize()` to the model's answer only; the triple from `formatTriple` goes to the wire raw, so zero-width and bidi characters in a judged command or result reach the model unneutralized. There is a real design tension (the judge is supposed to see what ran, and stripping mutates evidence), which is why this is a design item rather than a fix: decide between stripping, marking, or documenting the pass-through as posture. Signal: the next judgment prompt version, or a finding that invisible characters steered a verdict.

- **The sidecar daemon's scheduled task runs production from a live git worktree, so uncommitted edits are production behavior (2026-09-01, from the judge-partial-input section 1 security review; candidate spec).** `sidecar/daemon-task.ps1` launches `node` on this checkout's files and the installer registers AtLogOn plus 15-minute repetition, so any daemon exit promotes whatever sits in the worktree, mid-edit states included, with no rollout decision. Candidate fix shapes: pin the task to a committed install (a copied payload or a tag checkout), or gate the relaunch on a clean-tree check. Deserves its own small spec rather than a drive-by. Signal: the next daemon incident traced to a half-edited worktree, or the next plan whose rollout section has to reason around this hazard.

- **recognition-v1 still opens with a blanket hedge, cites judgment-v2 as its exemplar, and its head-slices can now bisect an in-band cut marker (2026-09-01, from the judge-partial-input section 1 and sections 2+3 reviews).** The judgment prompt dropped its blanket hedge on the measured argument that a hedge on every input carries no information; recognition-v1 still carries one, and its header points at v2 as the pattern to follow, which is now a retired wording. Separately, its 800/1000-character head-slices are marker-unaware, so a field the capture pipeline cut (which now carries head, marker, tail) can reach the recognition prompt with a bisected marker fragment astride the slice point - the exact mangled-marking shape the capture side's own re-cut discipline exists to prevent, escaping through this one consumer. Bring the hedge posture in line (or record why recognition differs), repoint the exemplar, and route recognition's cuts through the shared marker-aware cutter or strip partial markers. Signal: the next recognition prompt edit, or the next harvest showing marker fragments in recognition inputs.


- **The two signpost installers create their temp file non-exclusively, where the library's own atomic writer uses an exclusive create (2026-09-01, from the durable-boundary finishing pass's security review; declined for that effort and routed here).** Section 5 of the durable-boundary plan gave `setup.sh` and `doctor.ps1` a temp-and-rename write for the kaizen signpost, so nothing is written through a link standing at the signpost path itself. The temp name is not held to the same standard: `setup.sh` writes `claude-kit.local.json.tmp-<pid>` with a plain `fs.writeFileSync` and `doctor.ps1` writes a fixed `$signpost.tmp`, so a link pre-planted at either temp name is followed, and the fixed name additionally means two concurrent doctor runs collide on one temp file. It was declined for that effort on the argument that the whole path sits inside the single-principal `~/.claude` boundary the security model places outside every control, so an actor able to plant a link there can write the signpost directly and the exclusivity buys nothing against them; `docs/security-model.md` makes no exclusivity claim about the temp path, so nothing untrue ships either way. What is worth doing is parity: both writers should take the exclusive create `atomicTmpPath` already uses, and the doctor should carry a per-process temp name, so a later reader of either writer does not conclude the weaker shape was chosen deliberately.

- **A Chapter registers one section, and this repository's plans routinely close several in one Chapter, so the machine-read section count under-reports without saying so (2026-09-01, from the instruments-not-prose finishing pass, found while archiving the plan).** The plan-doc machine contract reads the first `Completed:` line of each Chapter and matches it against one section number or one section title, so a Chapter closing three sections can register at most one of them, and a phrasing like `Completed: Section 1 (title), Section 2 (title)` registers none at all, since the value has to open with the bare number. The instruments-not-prose plan hit both halves: two of its Chapters carried the non-registering phrasing, and the first of them closed three sections. Repairing the phrasing at the close moved its registered count from four of eight to six of eight, and the remaining two cannot register from the Chapter that closed them. Nothing surfaces the shortfall: terminality rides on the `Status:` header, so a plan can read Complete while its own section count says otherwise, which is a reading instrument disagreeing with the record it reads. Fix shape is a contract question rather than a patch, and it is coordinated with the external engine rather than a drive-by: either the parser reads every `Completed:` line in a Chapter, or the kit's Chapter template stops closing more than one section at a time. Signal: the next plan whose Chapters close sections in batches, or any work on the plan-doc parser.

- **The machine-configuration epoch record is a decay candidate, and the convention that depends on it assumes it is not (2026-09-01, from the instruments-not-prose Section 2 review; routed rather than folded).** The moment-pin convention reads every measured figure against a per-machine epoch record at the canonical name `machine-configuration-epoch-<hostname>`, and the memory-system skill states the expiry rule that does the reading. The record itself carries no `pinned:` field and no write path emits or removes one, so it is an ordinary operator-tier record: a summarize candidate at 30 idle days, whose body condensation can drop the very figures the comparison needs, and an archive candidate at 60. It is read only when a comparison is actually made and stamped only on an explicit `touch --applied`, which is exactly the ambient under-stamped shape pins exist for. Once archived, the expiry rule's third case fires, a machine no epoch record covers, and the next session writes a duplicate over a shadowed name. Not folded because the honest fix is a memq change rather than a skill sentence: writing that the record is pinned would assert a mechanism the tool does not have, which is precisely the prose-wearing-a-number's-clothes defect the plan exists to remove. Fix shape: give the epoch name a decay exemption in memq's decay scan, or emit `pinned:` on the `add-operator` path for that canonical name, with the usual red-first decay tests. Signal: the first epoch record to reach 30 idle days, or the next decay pass run on a machine whose epoch was written more than a month earlier.

- **Two SessionStart hooks can each tell a session to restart over the kit's version, keyed on two different identities (2026-08-29, from the plan-lifecycle Section 3 review; routed rather than folded).** `session-start.js` now emits a session-level line when the plugin view this session loaded differs from the machine's install, naming the cache-directory sha, and `kit-version-nudge.js` has emitted its own restart nudge for longer, naming the build stamp's short git hash. Both are wired on startup, resume and compact, so one payload can carry two restart requests naming two different version strings for the same session, or one can speak while the other stays silent about the same underlying fact. Neither is wrong: they answer different questions, the first whether the installed payload moved under this session and the second whether the build stamp inside the payload changed, and the second is what catches a rebuild-in-place that leaves the cache directory name alone. What is missing is a decision about which of them owns the session-level restart claim, and single-sourcing the identity string so a reader is never handed two spellings of one version. Rated Minor by the blind lens and deliberately not folded, because picking an owner is a design call about two hooks that predate and postdate each other rather than a defect in either. Fix shape: give one hook the restart claim outright and have the other state its fact without the directive, or merge the two readings into one block that names both identities once. Signal: the next report of a restart nudge that a restart did not clear, or the next change to either hook's trigger.

- **The goal CLI's unknown-flag refusal covers `arm` alone, and `--version` still prints no version (2026-08-29, from the plan-lifecycle Section 1 review; the section shipped the `arm` half deliberately).** Section 1 of the plan-lifecycle effort made the `arm` dispatch refuse any leading-dash token that is not `--append`, with a usage error naming the token and the running build. The section body scoped the harm to `armGoal`, because that is where an unknown flag was silently read as a plan path and produced the "plan not found: --append" misdiagnosis, and the shipped code matches that scope. The plan's own Goal states the CLI unqualified, so two gaps stay open behind the fix. `clear` and `status` still accept an unknown leading-dash token in silence, which is a quieter failure than `arm`'s was rather than a safer one: nothing is misread as a path, so nothing is reported at all, and a session passing a flag a newer CLI would honour gets the unflagged behaviour with no signal that its flag was dropped. And `--version` itself, the one token a reader would reach for after seeing a version in a usage error, falls through to the bare usage line and prints no version, so the refusal teaches a spelling the CLI does not answer. Both reviewers raised the pair as one Minor and it was recorded as scope rather than widened, since widening the screen to every dispatch is a behaviour change to commands the section never tested. Fix shape: lift the leading-dash screen to the argv dispatch ahead of the command switch, with each command declaring its own known flags, and give `--version` a real handler over the same build-stamp read `arm`'s refusal already uses. Signal: the next flag added to any goal CLI command, which is the moment the silent-swallow costs something.
- **The store sync commits whatever is dirty under one identity, and that authorship consequence is priced nowhere (2026-08-29, coordinator-routed, adjudicated by the expert seat).** The sync pass stages with `git add -A` before committing (`plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1`, the sync pass's staging step), so admitted content is committed with no seat's deliberate act. The doctrine's stage-exactly-your-target rule screens by authorship so one session never commits another's in-flight work, while the sync has no authorship screen and cannot express "not mine to commit", so a peer seat's in-flight store edits are staged and shipped unattended (field instance: a coordinator declined to stage peer-modified store files and the sync's screen would have carried them regardless; reported from that seat's account). The security model handles writer-scoping deliberately elsewhere, "neither release marker is scoped to its writer" being an argued accepted risk, so an absence here reads as a decision nobody made. Fix shape: write the authorship leg in the security model's own accepted-risk form and either accept it on argument or close it with a mechanism. Signal: the next change to the sync allowlist, or the next multi-seat store incident.

- **An engineless agent fan-out is invisible to the process poll, so the box can be loaded with nothing a pre-spawn check can see (2026-08-29, from the kaizen pass).** The doctrine's box-budget bullet states the limit: the poll cannot see in-process agent fan-out, which spawns no engine and holds no distinguishable working set. A clean poll is a basis for starting, so a suite started beside a wide review fan-out runs in overlap, and the overlap costs wall clock rather than a verdict. The one measured wrong grant had this shape: a slot cleared on a poll reading zero engines with at least five review agents live. Signal: an overlap red that passes solo, filed to the kaizen inbox under the testing-discipline red protocol and traced to an undeclared fan-out; the decision then is whether a fan-out above some width declares itself.


- **An unscreened plan path reaches the goal CLI on the mid-run arm route (2026-08-29, from the review-and-record finishing security review; pre-existing).** The mid-run arm route passes a plan path into the goal CLI without the screen the token gate gives the CLI's other producer, a real second-producer gap beside that gate. Rated Minor and routed rather than fixed: the gap predates that effort, it is materially mitigated by the peer-sessions grant trace that runs before any arm, and closing it is a design change to a rule that plan does not own. Signal: the next change to the arm route or to the token gate.

- **Two prose Minors sit on surfaces the review-and-record effort did not write (2026-08-29, from that effort's finishing prose pass).** A dated audit narrative at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/writing-skills/SKILL.md:21` and a Chapter-field name at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/executing-work/SKILL.md:298` that the frozen Chapter format does not define. Both are real and both fell outside that effort's scope, which is why they landed here rather than in its fix delta. Line numbers are as of that effort's close (commit `6d2e6cc`); re-locate by content.

- **`plugins/claude-kit/skills/executing-work/SKILL.md:351` records a reduced-effort review in the Chapter alone, where the kit's other recorded review downgrades reach the close-out (2026-08-29, from the review-and-record finishing fix round).** A justifying clause in `finishing-work` was narrowed to the downgrade set the evidence supports precisely because `:351` falsifies the universal: its reduced-effort record is Chapter-only, so a close-out reader never sees it. Routing that record to the close-out is the better rule and was deliberately not taken in the pass that found it, since no finding identified `:351` as defective, the site sat outside every section's scope, and editing an unrelated rule so a justifying sentence becomes true is scope creep wearing consistency's clothes. Take it as its own small change; the receipt is that finishing pass's third-pass finding.

- **`test/memory-session.test.js:1103` asserts a property of the host, not of the code, and is a false red on any machine with a short TEMP (2026-08-27, from the seat-infrastructure Section 1 baseline).** The case `a pinned directory too long to name faithfully stands the session down` pads a fixture path with 200 characters and relies on the host's temp prefix to carry the total past the `PATH_EMIT_CAP = 260` guard at `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memory-session.js:1090`. Both lengths were computed rather than inferred: this machine's `TEMP` is `D:\Temp`, seven characters, landing the fixture at 254, so the guard is never reached and the case fails; a default Windows temp prefix lands the same fixture at 280 and it fires. The failure is silent in the dangerous direction on the other kind of box, since a machine with a long prefix gets a green that genuinely proves the guard fires while this one gets a red that proves nothing about the code at all, and a reader diffing a baseline here reads a known red as their own regression. The fix is for the test to derive its padding from the actual prefix length rather than assume a long one, which also makes the case prove the guard on every host instead of on some of them. Routed out of the seat-infrastructure plan rather than folded: it serves test soundness rather than that plan's goal, and Section 1 already carries a security review. Signal: the next session to run the suite on this machine and wonder what it broke.

- **The store-backed `.kit` redirect covers one writer family and two others reach the store directly (2026-08-28, from the seat-infrastructure Section 10 fix round).** `kitScratchDir` at `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-compact-lib.js:62-69` redirects a `.kit` path to `~/.kit/store/<rel>` when the cwd sits under the store, which is what keeps the compaction gate's journal out of the replicated tree. Two other writers do not go through it: `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal-lib.js:314` joins `.kit/goal-state.json` onto the goal root directly, and `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-statusline.js:66` joins `.kit/statusline-cache.json` onto the cwd. So a seat whose project directory is the store's own coordinator directory drops both files into the replicated tree. Neither is a sync leak: both are `.json`, which no root's leaf set admits before or after Section 10's narrowing, so they are untracked residue that the operator's one-time `.kit/` sweep removes by directory. What the item is for is the asymmetry rather than the residue, since a redirect that one writer family honors and two do not is the shape that grows a leak the moment one of those two gains a form some root admits. The candidate fix is to export the resolver and have all three writers take it. Signal: any new `.kit` writer, or a root gaining a `.json` form.

- **`Test-MemorySyncPathAllowed` leaves three hardening gaps that predate the coordinator tier (2026-08-27, from the seat-infrastructure Section 1 blind and security reviews).** All three are pre-existing across all four tier prefixes in `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1` rather than created by that section, which is why they are here instead of folded into it. First, the traversal guard is private to one caller: the predicate returns true for `coordinator/../board.md` and `coordinator/BOX/./board.md`, and the `.`/`..` refusal lives only in `sync-store.ps1`, so it protects the inbound screen and nothing else. Section 10's per-root narrowing bounded what a traversal reaches without closing the gap: the coordinator root admits `*.md` alone, so an escape can no longer land on a store-root file such as `history.jsonl`, and what remains is a traversal to any `.md` outside the root it was screened against, which is not benign residue: the store root holds `CLAUDE.md` and `claude-kit-doctrine.md`, and the predicate returns true for both by way of a memory tier's own prefix. What keeps that unreachable today is the precondition rather than the predicate: all four doctor probes are fed git-normalized output, and `sync-store.ps1` refuses any `.` or `..` segment before it calls the predicate at all. That is a property of every current caller rather than of the guard, and the doctrine's own rule is that a sanitizing guard belongs at the shared boundary rather than in the producer that first needed it. The predicate has five callers, the doctor's four probes and the inbound screen, and only the screen refuses a traversal segment, so the asymmetry already sits inside the predicate's own caller set rather than waiting on a new producer to arrive. Second, PowerShell `-match` is case-insensitive, so the predicate admits `Coordinator/x.md` while a case-sensitive peer's `.gitignore` refuses it, and the inbound screen is the one surface where the two sides are genuinely different operating systems. Third, the coordinator prefix's machine segment is host-derived and unconstrained, so an incoming tree from a POSIX peer can carry a segment legal there and illegal on NTFS, which the screen admits and the checkout then fails on, recording a conflict on every subsequent run. The first is the one worth doing on its own merits; the other two want a deliberate answer recorded rather than a silent default. Signal: a sixth caller of the predicate, or any existing caller reaching it with a path the inbound screen did not normalize first.

- **Two end-to-end observations of the compaction release paths are outstanding, and only the operator can make them (2026-08-26, from the compaction-boundaries finishing pass).** The unit-level gate behavior is pinned by tests and green, but neither release path has been watched land in a real session. First: in a long-running goalless session, run `node <plugin-root>/hooks/kit-compact-checkpoint.js boundary` at a banked moment, then let the session cross the harness's own auto-offer threshold, and confirm `.kit/compact-gate.jsonl` records the landing with reason `role-boundary` at that boundary rather than a `valve` landing at the context safety ceiling. Second: instruct a session over the relay to release its deferred compaction, and confirm the landing is journaled `operator-consent`. A failure on either reopens the effort as a new round. Signal: the next long session that actually defers.

- **`memq add-operator`'s prompt-free fleet grant was priced when the operator tier carried facts, and that tier now carries an authorization switch (2026-08-28, from the seat-infrastructure Section 8 security review).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memq-grant.js` allows `add-operator` prompt-free and agent-blind under the engine store signals, and `docs/security-model.md` accepts that risk on the reasoning that the write lands inside a store the engine chose. Section 3 of the seat-infrastructure plan changed what such a write can be: the standing-delegation opt-in is one operator-tier record whose presence flips a seat's default treatment of scoped direction from the seats above it, so a write there is an authorization change rather than a fact. The grant's stated precondition is unchanged and the reach needs the documented environment-divergence residual to fire, which is why this is a re-pricing rather than a defect: decide whether the verb stays in the prompt-free set now that the tier carries a switch, or whether the grant narrows to records that are not delegation records. The security model states the reach and points here. Signal: the next change to either the grant's allow set or the delegation model.

  Widened 2026-08-31 by the standing-grants plan's section 1: the subject is no longer one switch. The role skill now states a general standing-grant rail, of which the delegation opt-in is the first instance and the memory store's push grant the second, and the rail admits further grants whenever an owning skill states one, with no edit to the security model or to this item required for that to happen. So the question to decide is not whether the delegation switch is worth the prompt-free grant but whether a tier whose privileged content grows by an owning skill's say-so is, and the second exit above narrows accordingly: withholding the grant from delegation records alone would leave every later grant's record inside it. Signal is unchanged, with a third trigger added: a new instance being added to the rail.

- **A `superseded by <name>` label is forgeable by a record description (parked 2026-08-23).** The label rides the same line as the record's own free-text description on `find`'s lexical hits and on `recall`'s digest lines, so a description ending in `superseded by <name>` renders indistinguishably from a real label, and on an archive line, where the genuine label prints after the description, the forgery is exact. A forged label steers a reading model and an adjudicator at a decay pass; it cannot demote a rank or nominate for archive, both of which derive from the pointer map rather than from the line. This is the free-text-beside-tool-tokens class the tier label already sits in, with a token of more decision weight added to it. The fix is positional rather than a filter: give the tool-written tokens a span the description cannot enter. Surfaced by the finishing security review of `claude-kit_memory-supersedes_spec_v1.md`.

- **A `--supersedes` target named in variant case writes a pointer that goes inert off Windows (parked 2026-08-23).** `supersedesTargetRefusal` resolves the target through the filesystem, which is case-insensitive on Windows, so `--supersedes OLDER` against `older.md` passes the typo guard and stores `supersedes: OLDER` verbatim. `memoryFileKey` lowercases on win32 only, so the same record synced to a case-sensitive checkout labels nothing: the one case mismatch the write-time guard exists to catch while the author is still present. Inert is the read side's safe direction, so the cost is a silently lost label rather than corruption. The fix is to canonicalize the written value to the on-disk filename's casing at write time. The off-Windows half is inferred rather than confirmed; confirming it takes one pointer written in variant case on a case-sensitive filesystem. Surfaced by the finishing adversarial review of `claude-kit_memory-supersedes_spec_v1.md`.

- **Give the `memq-grant` `--supersedes` screen a canary probe** (parked 2026-08-23). `hooks/hook-canary.js` probes the grant's withholding direction for `--body-file`, `--rollup` and `find`, and not for `--supersedes`, a body-carrying `--update`, `--trigger`, `--type=<type>`, or `--drop-malformed`, which the kaizen batch's section 10 added to the screen on 2026-08-25. All five unprobed flag shapes have a second lock, which is the stated ground for leaving them unprobed, though `--drop-malformed`'s is of a different kind from the other four: theirs is a CLI refusal under the store signals, while its is a coupling, since the CLI answers it with a usage error unless `--rollup` accompanies it and the hook withholds `--rollup` too (`docs/security-model.md`, the memq-grant compensating-control paragraph), so on those five this is an enhancement rather than a defect. The `anchor` verb is the one that is not: it has no second lock anywhere, the verb being the operator's own, so its screen rests on the hook alone and a regression on it degrades to nothing with no probe to say so. Probe `anchor` first. Cost is one probe block per shape plus the probe-count figures in `security-model.md` and the canary's own comment. Surfaced by the section 3 review of `claude-kit_memory-supersedes_spec_v1.md`.

- **Give `memqGrantProbes` the load check its own sibling already does** (parked 2026-08-26). `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/hook-canary.js:503-517` establishes that the grant's payload is there with `fs.statSync(memq).isFile()`, while `cacheSuppliesMemq` at `:666`, one site over in the same file, answers the stronger question by loading it. A stat passes for a file that is present and unloadable, so the canary can report the grant's payload healthy on an install where every granted command would still fail at require time, and that gap widened when `scripts/memq.js` took a load-time coupling to `hooks/kit-network-lib.js`: a cache missing the sibling now stats clean and throws on require. The two probes ask about the same file and should reach the same answer. Cost is one call swap plus whatever the load's failure mode needs saying on the failure line. Routed here by Chapter 4 of `claude-kit_memory-anchors-and-frontmatter-guard_spec_v1.md`; the finishing adversarial review of that plan found the routing had never landed, which is the second half of why it is dated today rather than at Chapter 4.


- **Check the channels broker for readers of the stop-failure marker files (2026-08-23, from the dormant-feature removal).** In the `sapplefeld-channels` repo, grep for `stop-failure-latest.json` and `stop-failure-resumed.json`. The archived stop-failure spec named the broker as a possible second reader of those markers, for a status line on the Discord card it posts. No reader found means done. A reader found means either confirming it tolerates the files being absent, which is already its steady state on any machine that never logged a failure, or removing that reader on the broker side. Nothing in the kit blocks on this.

- **Take the plugin update on each fleet machine (2026-08-23, from the dormant-feature removal).** Until a machine runs `claude plugin update`, its installed cache still carries the removed payload, and its build manifest still names `stop-failure-log.js`, so the session-start canary reports that file missing on every session. The update is what quiets it. The scheduled-task precondition this item used to carry is answered: no fleet machine holds the watcher task, so the update is unconditional. The per-machine acceptance check is internal consistency plus trunk match, not a version compare alone: the installed guard's frontmatter field list agrees with the installed reader's and with the trunk's (`MEMQ_FIELDS`, seven names), because guard and reader ship in one payload and the dangerous state is a partial update rather than an old one; a stale-but-consistent payload merely lags, while a mismatched pair would read fields its own guard never checks (2026-08-30, measured on the coordinator's box: both cache payloads at six names, internally consistent, trunk at seven; activating the box's newer cache entry was checked and changes nothing, so the action is the update, not an activation).

- **Never-worse invariant, banked for the first summarizer (2026-08-22, from the Warden candidate 5 disposition).** Any future spec that adds a reduction or summarization step includes this as a tested requirement: the reduction never emits more than it consumed, and ships the original when it would. Deliberately not retrofit to the `recall`/`recent` digests, settled 2026-08-22: their coverage lines exceed a tiny store's size on purpose, because a stated empty surface is the product and a silent absence is the failure. Signal: the first spec adding a summarizer writes the invariant in; until then this item just holds it.

- **Two guards from the shared-tier delete ship with no red on this host and need provisioned environments (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's named gaps).** Both are stated rather than assumed closed, and neither is reachable from a normal run here. The hook canary's POSIX path-spelling branch cannot go red on win32, where the reversal is the state that passes, so it needs a POSIX host in the gate; both branches were verified by evaluating the constant with `path.sep` faked, which is an argument rather than a test. And the `stat` to `lstat` change on the record path needs the symlink-creation privilege, meaning Developer Mode or an elevated shell, since `fs.symlinkSync` returns EPERM on this box and a junction is directory-shaped, which the guard refuses for a different reason. Standing Brief Amendment 8 says a guard closing a defect class ships with a per-site red, so these two are the named exceptions to that inventory and should stop being exceptions when either environment is available.

- **Collapse the surviving restatements of three shared-tier facts to single owners (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's finishing prose review).** Three facts are each written out in full at three or more sites, and every one of them is a set whose membership can grow. The delete's closing-sweep rule sits at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md:14` (the owner, with two correct pointers at `:30` and `:136`) plus three independent paraphrases in `docs/security-model.md`, `docs/architecture.md` and `plugins/claude-kit/skills/finishing-work/SKILL.md`. The eleven granted verbs are enumerated in `docs/security-model.md`, `docs/fleet-integration.md` and `memory-system/SKILL.md`, plus twice more in comments inside `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memq-grant.js`, with `GRANTED_VERBS` at `memq-grant.js:249-250` the only mechanical owner. The kit doctor's `-Fix` procedure is duplicated near-verbatim across `memory-system/SKILL.md:64` and `finishing-work/SKILL.md:66`, and that duplication is confirmed to have caused a defect: a false rule about the doctor's WARN handling landed in both copies and had to be fixed in both. All six sites currently agree, which is exactly why this is a backlog item and not a bug: the cost lands on the next correction, not today. The fix is to name one owner per fact and reduce the rest to pointers, which the two correct pointers at `memory-system/SKILL.md:30` and `:136` already show working. A fourth member joined at the finishing fix round (2026-08-22, found by the fable confirmation review): the canary's grant-probe roster, count and screen names, is enumerated in full in both `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/security-model.md` on top of its mechanical owner in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/hook-canary.js`, so the next probe added must update two prose enumerations nobody owns.

- **Move `sameSessionId` from `kit-compact-lib.js` into `kit-goal-lib.js` so the two call-time requires can become load-time ones (2026-08-29, from the plan-lifecycle finishing review).** `armingSessionClaims` and `sessionHoldsLeash` in `kit-goal-lib.js` each `require('./kit-compact-lib.js')` inside the function body rather than at module load, because `kit-compact-lib.js` destructures `kit-goal-lib.js` at its own load and a top-level require back is a cycle. That is correct and documented at both sites, and `require` is cached, so the runtime cost is a map lookup. The closure is the other direction: `sameSessionId` is a session-id comparison rule with no compaction content, so it belongs in the goal library, and moving it there leaves the compaction library taking it through the load-time destructure it already performs. Parked rather than done because it rewrites every consumer in `kit-compact-lib.js` and their tests for no behavioral change, which is not work to fold into an effort's closing pass. Worth doing the next time either library is opened for its own reasons.

- **The nudge log's two security Minors ship recorded and unfixed (2026-08-30, from the memory-recognition finishing security review).** Both sit in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memory-recognition-nudge.js` and both were rated Minor and deliberately not folded. The first is a cross-session rotation gap: the log rotates to `<path>.old` past 1 MB under the emission lock, but a reader that opens only the live file after a rotation it did not observe sees a window it cannot tell from a quiet one, so `nudgeStampRate` can under-count nudges near a rotation boundary rather than refusing. The second is the state directory's ownership screen, which is a real check on POSIX and degenerate on win32, where the ownership predicate the sibling writers rely on has no equivalent, so the directory is trusted on its kind alone. Fix shape: have the rate reader take the rotated file into its window when the live file's first line postdates the window start, and state the win32 screen's actual strength where the security model describes it rather than inheriting the POSIX sentence. Signal: the first stamp-rate reading that disagrees with a hand count, or a second consumer of the state directory.

- **The nudge log's `O_NOFOLLOW` append is unexercised on the only machine the suite gates on (2026-08-30, from the memory-recognition finishing pass).** `memory-recognition-nudge.js` composes `O_NOFOLLOW` into its append open where the platform defines the constant, which refuses a final-component symlink at the open itself. Node exposes the constant only where the platform has it, and on win32 `fs.constants.O_NOFOLLOW` is `undefined`, so the branch resolves to a plain `'a'` open and the guarded path never executes here. The code is correct and the coverage is absent, which is the shape that reads green forever: no test on this box can distinguish the composed flags from the fallback. `docs/security-model.md` already states the residual in the shipped prose, so what is open is the proof rather than the disclosure. Fix shape: a POSIX runner, or a unit test over the flag-composition expression alone with the constant injected, which proves the composition without needing the syscall. Signal: the kit gaining a non-Windows gate, or a second writer reaching for the same idiom.

- **`anchor` and `triggers` are withheld from the fleet grant and probed by the canary in neither direction (2026-08-30, from the memory-recognition finishing pass).** `hooks/memq-grant.js` withholds ten verbs, and for seven of them, `find`, `anchor`, `triggers`, `forget`, `db-promote`, `db-curate` and `jev-calibration`, the grant list is the whole of what withholds them, with one qualification: `triggers` now refuses its own shared-tier and pinned-tier `--replace` under the store signals, so what rests on the grant list alone there is every other shape of the verb rather than all of it, while `find`, `anchor` and `forget` carry no such refusal at all. `hooks/hook-canary.js` probes `find` in both directions and these two in neither, so a regression on either screen degrades silently to nothing and no probe would say so. This is the same gap as the parked `--supersedes` probe item above and shares its fix shape; it is filed separately because the two verbs are the operator's own authoring verbs, which is what makes an unnoticed grant worse here than a missing probe elsewhere. Fix shape: extend `memqGrantProbes` with a withheld-direction probe per verb. Signal: the next change to `GRANTED_VERBS`, or the next verb added to either authoring family.

- **`cmdAnchor` and `cmdTriggers` both name the wrong tier in one not-found message (2026-08-30, from the memory-recognition Section 1 review).** Both verbs compute a `where` holding the tier the record actually resolved to (the run's pending tier or the project tier), and both then hardcode `'in the project tier'` in the message for a record that vanished between the pre-lock stat and the read under the lock (`memq.js`, `cmdAnchor`'s not-found write and `cmdTriggers`' clone of it). The window is racy and narrow, so nothing is lost but the accuracy of one sentence, and the reader it misleads is one already chasing a record that disappeared mid-command. Parked rather than folded into Section 1 because the defect originates in the anchor verb, and that section's whole discipline was that file anchors stay untouched: repairing it there would have put an unrelated behaviour change inside a changeset whose reviewers were told anchors were out of scope. Fix both sites together, since fixing one leaves the clone stating the same falsehood.

- **Rule the fork between a shared-file hold and a finishing pass's clean-docs gate (2026-08-30, raised by the machine coordinator seat, no durable trace found anywhere before this entry).** Two rules this kit states both apply to one situation and point opposite ways, and nothing says which wins. The doctrine's scope rule says a file another session is mid-edit on is not yours to stage even where you also changed it, so a document left dirty under that hold stays dirty. The finishing pass expects the plan doc reaching its terminal state in the same delivery as the code, and its resting state is terminal-and-staged rather than uncommitted, which a held file cannot reach. So a finishing pass that meets a held document has to either violate the hold or report itself finished with an uncommitted doc, and today the choice falls to whoever notices, unrecorded either way. What a ruling needs to settle: whether the hold outranks the gate (and if so, what the pass records so the next session can tell a held doc from a forgotten one), or the gate outranks the hold for the plan doc specifically (and if so, what makes that safe when the other session's content is in the same file). This one is the operator's rather than a seat's, because it trades a correctness risk against a bookkeeping risk and the appetite for each is theirs.

- **Frame a memory record's provenance by the pin that resolved it, not by the tier it came from (2026-08-30, declined out of the memory-recognition Section 2 security review).** The store fences a record read whose writer is someone other than its reader: the shared tiers always, and the project tier under a store pin, where the pin is what makes the writer foreign. The framing that carries that fence is written in the vocabulary of tiers, so a reader learns which tier answered and has to infer from the pin whether the text in front of them was written by this project or handed to it by an environment that chose the store. The recognition nudge sharpens the question, because it names a record without being asked and its line is the only provenance a session may ever see for that record. What a fix would state is the resolving pin itself, in the fence and on any surface that names a record, so a pinned read reads as foreign at a glance rather than after a lookup. Declined inside that section as a MINOR whose blast radius is legibility rather than safety, and because the change reaches every surface that fences a read rather than the one that prompted it.

- **Decide what the public repository may carry of the operator's own identity, and make the answer one rule rather than three conventions (2026-08-29, from the plan-lifecycle Section 4 re-review).** Three surfaces carry it today and each arrived by a different route, which is why no single sweep has ever covered them. `kaizen/notes-<hostname>.md` is machine-scoped by documented design (`plugins/claude-kit/skills/kaizen/SKILL.md`), so the hostname is in a tracked filename on purpose and any change there is a change to the kaizen model. The prose in `docs/archive/backlog-2026-Q3.md` names machines and the operator by name inside item bodies, which is incidental rather than designed: nothing reads those tokens and nothing would break if they were roles; `docs/backlog.md` itself no longer names machines, the public-surface-hygiene plan having generalized its three machine-naming entries to roles, though it still names the operator in three item bodies, which is the half of this question that plan deliberately did not settle, and that plan's disposition table is where each surviving surface's rule is now recorded. And three test files key fixtures on the real marketplace name (`claude-kit@applefeld`): `test/session-start-plugin-view.test.js`, `test/kit-statusline.test.js` and `test/kit-goal-statusline.test.js`. That is the weakest of the three surfaces, since each hook deliberately matches on the plugin half alone and a neutral marketplace name would exercise the same path. The enumeration is stated at file granularity on purpose, because the first version of this item named one of the three and a sweep run to it would have left two behind. None of it is a credential and the marketplace name is published in the repository's own install path, so this is a posture question rather than a leak: the standing rule says operator specifics are recorded by reference and never by quotation, and three surfaces answer it three ways. Decide the rule, then sweep to it in one pass rather than per-effort. The fixture keys are the cheap third to fix whatever the wider answer is.
- **Decide whether a description-only `--update` should keep its prompt-free grant on the unattended vector (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's finishing security review).** `memq-grant.js:480` withholds `--update` only when `--body` rides with it, so a bare `--update` on `add-type`/`add-operator` keeps the grant and a fleet worker can replace an existing shared-tier record's index line with no prompt. The index line is the higher-reach surface of the two: it is emitted into every declaring project's session context with no command invoked (`docs/security-model.md:39`), while the body is only read on demand. The CLI's own stated reason for refusing the body half under the store signals, that the `.bak` which would recover it is local and unsynced so a repair there is as final as a deletion (`memq.js:6942`), applies verbatim to the index rewrite, which also leaves only a never-synced `.bak`. The counterweight is blast radius: 120 characters against 64 KB. Rated Minor because nothing is exploitable as shipped and the description channel is the repair path a fleet worker most plausibly needs. Two exits: state the asymmetry in `docs/security-model.md:188`, or withhold `--update` at the hook, which is a behavior change and so ships with a per-site red under Standing Brief Amendment 8.

- **Retitle or re-harness the repair-consent test whose title outruns its assertion (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's finishing adversarial review).** `test/memq.test.js:10534` is titled as refusing a body "without its consent flag first" but runs on the `makeStore`/`run` harness, where `memq.js:6949` refuses the repair under the engine store signals before the consent gate at `:7007` is ever reached; the assertion matches `/refused under the engine store signals/` and never exercises consent. The test's own comment concedes it. The consent-first claim is pinned only by the neighbouring test at `:10579`, so nothing is currently unpinned, but a title asserting what its body does not test is the small end of the class Amendment 8 exists to close. Either retitle it to the refusal it actually pins, or move it to `makeHomeStore`/`runHome` and assert the consent refusal.

- **Break the negation-then-correction cadence in the two long prose documents (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's finishing prose review).** `references/ai-tells.md` names "it is not X, it is Y" contrast framing against a position no reader proposed. It runs at roughly one instance every two lines in `docs/security-model.md` (161 occurrences) and `docs/architecture.md` (112), with `memory-system/SKILL.md` at 46 and `docs/fleet-integration.md` at 21, alongside 23/21/9 instances of "deliberate(ly)" and 9/4/3 of "is the point". It is a frequency and uniformity finding rather than a quarrel with any line, and it is the strongest machine-prose signature in the library. Adjacent, same source: six section-closing one-line morals in `memory-system/SKILL.md`, a bolded lead-in on nearly every argument-carrying paragraph in that file's second half, and four single-sentence blocks over 100 words whose announcing count is separated from its list by an interposed clause (`architecture.md:123` at 1,258 words, `security-model.md:182` at 1,228, `finishing-work/SKILL.md:62` at 635 carrying an executable procedure, `memory-system/SKILL.md:14` at 426). Deferred rather than fixed because it is a rewrite of two documents and not a correction, and because none of it is false; the finishing pass fixed what was wrong and left what was merely mannered.

- **The shared-tier delete's ordering argument claims a permanence its checks do not establish for a node sitting at `archive/<name>.md.bak` (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's section 3, raised by the blind review lens).** `deleteSharedRecord`'s header argues that the record-copy sweeps lead because a failure there can be permanent rather than transient, and that both directories are listed before either sweep unlinks, so a permanent failure leaves the store exactly as it was. The listing step stats the two directories and not the two backup names inside them, so a directory occupying `archive/<name>.md.bak`, which the header itself names as the permanent case, is discovered only when the archive sweep tries to unlink it, by which time the live-tier sweep has already spent the record's own `.bak`, the single copy of the body a repair replaced. The shape of the fix is to lstat both `<name>.md.bak` paths during the listing step alongside the directory checks, so a permanent occupier refuses before anything is unlinked, which is what the header already claims happens. Not urgent: reaching it needs a directory hand-placed at a `.bak` name inside a tier that bars hand edits, which is the same privilege class as the effort's other named gaps, and the loss is one recovery copy rather than a record. Deliberately not taken in the effort that found it: section 3 was a documentation pass and held its code edits to comments and one message string, and a change to the delete's ordering wants a red-first test per site under Standing Brief Amendment 8 rather than a tail-of-section edit.

- **A mistyped name under `--confirm-shared` spends the shared tier's three backup generations and reports nothing (2026-08-22, from the shared-tier authoring effort's section 3, raised by the blind review lens).** Under consent `cmdDeleteType` and its operator twin deliberately stand down the no-copy-present pre-check, so a name the tier does not hold reaches the delete proper, removes nothing, sets its completion flag, and runs the closing sweep over the tier index, the archive index and the usage sidecar. Those three `.bak` files are whatever the last locked rewrite left, which for a decay pass is its only local recovery, and they go. The loss is silent twice over: the line reporting what was swept is gated on a nonzero removal count, which a typo leaves at zero, and the sweep counts no backups in the first place, so stderr says only that no memory file of that name exists. Two shapes of fix, and they compose: report each backup the sweep actually removed, so the cost is visible whatever the run did; and reconsider whether the sweep belongs on the path where no record was found, since its stated justification is that a backup holds the record being deleted, which is not true when there was none.
