A section-local re-stat would fork that single answer, which is a worse defect than the one it closes. Confirmed pre-existing: neither resolver appears in any hunk of that section's diff. Repro recipe for whoever takes it: make an existing operator-tier directory unreadable (EACCES on the directory itself, not its contents), then run `memq unstamped` from a project that declares a type tier, and observe that the operator tier is absent from the report rather than reported as unread. The EACCES-on-`statSync` behaviour is inferred from a code read rather than reproduced, because the box this surfaced on is Windows; confirming it is the first step of the work.

- **Rules in `finishing-work`, `executing-work` and `memory-system` that contradict a neighbour or the code they describe (parked 2026-08-25, from the kaizen batch's section 9 round-8 blind review).** That round briefed the blind lens to read three whole skill files rather than a diff, per project memory `skill-amendments-collide-with-neighbours`, and it returned eleven defects in text section 9 never touched. They are parked rather than folded because each needs acceptance section 9 does not carry, and they serve the kit's general correctness rather than that section's goal. Two are confirmed by this orchestrator; the rest carry the reviewer's citation and are unconfirmed, so confirming each is the first step of the work.
  - **Confirmed.** `finishing-work/SKILL.md:40`'s base-ref cross-check names a bookkeeping set of the plan doc plus `docs/README.md`, but `docs/backlog.md` is in the changeset of any healthy pass that parked a surface (executing-work step 4 writes it and step 7 commits it) and no `Files in scope:` line ever names it; a jotted `kaizen/notes-<machine>.md` is the same shape. So the check fires on the designed-normal case, which is the standing false alarm that same paragraph warns against, while `docs/README.md` cannot be dirty at that moment because the check runs before the curator. Verified against this very plan: none of its ten sections names either file. Fix: put `docs/backlog.md` and the kaizen note path in the bookkeeping set and re-time or drop `docs/README.md`.
  - **Reviewer-cited, unconfirmed.** `finishing-work:40`'s Commit-and-Push base-ref walk takes the earliest Chapter commit, which on a reopened plan reaches back into the previous round's already-reviewed changeset, and the stated cross-check cannot catch it because those files sit inside the plan's own scope union. `finishing-work:14,20,32`'s unavailability rule admits an unavailable growth reading as evidence for the never-wrote condition at :32 while :14 says a wedge failing any condition proves nothing about reachability, so a session with no control can permanently downgrade the finishing gate on evidence the rule itself disclaims. `finishing-work:52`'s cross-reference selection takes whole-token equality against repo-relative plan paths while the curator's charter writes cross-ref gaps as free-form plan names, so step 6's act-on-the-gap instruction is unreachable on the ordinary entry. `finishing-work:84` counts the decay pass as three moves where `memory-system` counts four and this step's own later prose requires the fourth (`decay-done`). `executing-work`'s Branch-and-PR bullet says the section's code commits with its Chapter and then that it commits at first green, before step 6 has written any Chapter. `executing-work`'s dispatch site states the never-park rule unqualified while the completion contract sanctions a leading `WAITING:` park when dispatched agents are the only remaining work. `memory-system:202`'s frontmatter budget arithmetic does not close against `frontmatterBlock`, which allows the closing fence at line 41. `memory-system:58`'s warning that every edit resets a file's birthtime contradicts `recentFileRecord`'s own platform gate, and one of the two is wrong. (`memory-system:114`'s closed frontmatter field set was the eleventh; it is resolved rather than parked. The memory-anchors work merged into this branch at the finishing pass, which made the omission confirmable against this branch's own code: `frontmatterAnchors` reads `anchors:` through the same `frontmatterValue` path at both placements, so the five-field enumeration was closed and false. `anchors:` is now the sixth member there and in `docs/architecture.md`'s copy of the same list.)

- **The frontmatter guard does not refuse a hand-written mutual `supersedes:` pair (2026-08-25, from Section 4's first review round in the memory-anchors plan).** memq's own CLI refuses the case where a pointer's target already supersedes the writer, on the reasoning that a pair asserts no replacement and so every reader drops both halves. The new PreToolUse guard, which is the only door in front of a hand-written project-tier pointer, does not: it checks that the target exists, is live, is not the record itself and is named in exact casing, and stops there. A hand-written pair naming each other therefore lands, and both records lose their label and their archive nomination with nothing said. Ruled out of v1 rather than folded, because it is a new deny rule the section's closed deny list did not name and it needs an acceptance criterion that section did not carry; the detection itself is cheap, since the target is a readable file in the same directory the guard already lists.

- **`memq`’s handshake-failure stderr write is bare where the goal library’s is wrapped (parked 2026-08-26, from kaizen batch 2 section 1’s blind review).** The two implementations of the worktree handshake agree on every resolution decision and are pinned against each other, but they diverge on one exotic path: `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal-lib.js` wraps its note write in a try/catch while `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js` leaves its bare, so a throwing stderr write (a closed descriptor) propagates out of memq’s resolver and is swallowed by the library’s. The fix belongs on memq’s side, which the section held read-only, and the drift pin cannot see it because the pin asserts resolution answers rather than failure behaviour.

- **The type-lock contention test can still red on a loaded box, and the cause is a two-second lock wait rather than shared machine state (parked 2026-08-26, root cause corrected 2026-08-27 from the testing-discipline effort's section 4).** The cause originally recorded here was that the test's subject is the machine-global type tier, so that a sibling Claude session running `memq touch` or `memq add-type` would be indistinguishable from the rival writer the test measures. The code refutes it. `concurrent add-type writers from two projects serialize under the type lock` in `test/memq.test.js` already spawned its writers with `KIT_MEMORY_ROOT` pointed at the test's own `fs.mkdtempSync` root, `memoryRoot()` honors that pin at `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js:412-414`, and the type tier derives from it at `:1091`, so no other session's memq can reach the store under test and no concurrent writer can reproduce the red. The live cause is a budget. `cmdAddType` calls `acquireLock` with no options at `:10546`, so `waitMs` takes its 2000 ms default at `:1290`, every writer shares that one budget, and a box slow enough pushes the tail writer past it, at which point memq exits 1 with `memq: type store locked, nothing written` and the test's exit-code assertion reds. Measured on this box by a throwaway probe script that runs the test's own spawn shape against a fresh temp store at five repetitions per writer count: the slowest of six writers finishes 294 ms after the parent spawned it, of which 249 ms is time the tail writer spent waiting rather than working (a lone writer takes 45 ms), so the wait it must fit inside 2000 ms has a factor of 8.0 to spare; four writers put the same figure at 10.9 and the two writers the test now spawns at 32.8, against the roughly 10x load factor this suite has been observed to take under full-suite contention (the sibling entry at 2026-08-04 recorded 11.1 s against 1.1 s alone). Section 4 took the writer count from six to two and made a future red self-diagnosing, so a starved writer now names the tier lock instead of dumping stderr. That buys margin and does not repair the cause. What remains is the design call the margin defers: either give `cmdAddType` a lock wait proportionate to the writers a real store can carry, or make the wait injectable so a test can ask for a generous one. Both change a production default or widen memq's environment surface, which is the most carefully gated surface in this repository, so each earns its own decision rather than a drive-by edit.

- **The doctor reports the armed queue position from the stored index, where the node-side reporters now read it from the plan docs (parked 2026-08-26, from kaizen batch 2 section 3).** `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/doctor.ps1:1255` prints `Plan N of M in the armed queue.` straight from `queueIndex`, so after section 3 it disagrees with the SessionStart advisory and with `kit-goal.js status` in exactly the stale-index state those two now correct. It is parked rather than fixed, and the reason is a real design argument rather than cost: the doctor is the surface that deliberately reads the raw state file with no hooks-side guard, so that it can report a file the hooks refuse. A doctor that silently corrects the number it is there to show you would defeat the one job that makes it the reporting control this repository credits it as. The right shape is probably to print both, the stored position and the position the evidence supports, and say when they differ. That needs its own spelling of the rule in PowerShell, since the node library is not reachable from it, which is a second implementation to keep in step and earns a decision rather than a drive-by edit.

- **Seven declared memq boundaries await the operator's adjudication (parked 2026-08-27, from the blocked-escalation effort).** Each of that effort's six section Chapters, and its finishing Chapter, declares that memq was not run at that boundary, under the operator's standing workspace constraint that a write from those sessions can flip a concurrency test in a sibling session's gate. So seven stamp adjudications were never performed, and the effort read, wrote, and stamped nothing in the memory store. They are declarations rather than omissions, which is what makes this the operator's call rather than a gap to close quietly: a sweep run later would retroactively change what those seven declarations describe. Decide whether to run the sweep against that stretch and accept the mismatch, or to let the declarations stand as the record. The plan is `docs/archive/claude-kit_blocked-escalation_spec_v1.md`, whose Chapters carry the declarations verbatim.

- **The merged-PR push guard checks only the first command-position push in a compound command (parked 2026-08-27, from the testing-discipline effort's finishing adversarial review).** `pushArgs` in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/merged-pr-push-guard.js` iterates command positions but returns on its first match, so in `git push origin safe && git push origin merged-branch` the guard judges `safe`, allows, and the second push rides through unguarded with no output. Fail-open direction and a fringe command shape. Declined during that effort's finishing fix round rather than taken, because closing it changes the function's contract from one branch to many and so changes the guard's whole decision shape, which is a redesign rather than a fix-round edit. The parser's own comment names it as a known unfixed gap.

- **The push guard's git reads, and every call on the hooks' library and the store's sync funnel, can be redirected by `HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, which no environment scrub strips (parked 2026-08-27, from the testing-discipline effort's finishing security review; widened 2026-09-07 by the store git-channel guard plan's section 3 review to the two guarded channels, whose additive pins keep reading the global config those variables select, and whose write verbs reach the open class of command-running keys the security model names).** `queryEnv` strips every `GIT_*` key, which closes `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, but git also locates `~/.gitconfig` through `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, so a repo-carried value for either redirects the global config behind the three reads the block decision rests on. Bounded: the guard only ever blocks, so a redirect yields a spurious block or the fail-open default rather than any grant, and the three commands used do not reach the config-driven command hooks that would give execution. Declined during that effort's finishing fix round because git needs `HOME` to find legitimate config, so stripping it risks breaking the guard's reads in ordinary use to close a hole whose worst outcome is a needless block. Reported rather than confirmed: the redirect was reasoned from git's lookup order, not exercised against these commands on this box.

- **The Node git guard sets `NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` by plain assignment, so a differently cased ambient spelling survives beside it (parked 2026-09-07, from the store git-channel guard plan's finishing security review).** `gitChildEnv()` in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-git-lib.js` strips `GIT_*` in every casing but assigns the current-directory setting by one spelling, so an ambient `NODEFAULTCURRENTDIRECTORYINEXEPATH` (reachable by the repo-carried terminal-profile route the security model's Principals section names) rides into the child beside the guard's own, and which of the two the child's single-slot block keeps is the runtime's ordering rather than the guard's. The PowerShell funnel has no equivalent hazard, since its setter writes the process slot. Fix: delete any key matching `/^NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath$/i` before the assignment, and have `test/git-guard-parity.test.js` plant a lower-cased spelling so the pin proves it. Defence in depth only: the spawn working directory closes the route for the git call itself.

- **The sync script's ownership test accepts the marker-bearing `.gitignore` a clone can carry before it reads the `--local` key, so it has no owner past the pins (parked 2026-09-07, from the store git-channel guard plan's finishing security review).** `Test-MemorySyncRepoIsOwn` in `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1` returns true on the committed marker before it reads `claudekit.memorysync`, where the hook's gate reads the key alone; the guard plan's Evidence names it forgeable and its security model states it as a residual, and nothing carries a fix. With the two pins in place it is the one remaining route by which a hand run of the sync script reaches a planted store root's command-running config (`credential.helper`, `core.sshCommand` on fetch and push); the precondition is write access to the store root or an operator-supplied wrong `-StoreRoot`, so it is not reachable from a cloned project alone. Recommendation: require the key whenever `.git` exists at the root, and use the `.gitignore` evidence only to repair under `-Fix` consent.

- **The absence-control rule is precisely stated and reliably violated, and the violations split into three classes with different repairs (2026-08-29, coordinator-routed, adjudicated by the expert seat).** Four instances within roughly one hour, across four seats and four unrelated instruments, every seat loading the doctrine that states the rule near-verbatim: twice a path bar that errored and printed empty on the board and its control alike; a diff filter that swallowed the index lines it was meant to inspect; and a fixed-name process scan whose true silence was reported as "the box is free" when what it established was "no declared heavy process". The adjudicated split. Class one, errored-empty (two of four): the check failed as a process and its empty output was read as a clean result; this class has a mechanical discriminator already, the check's own exit code (grep and rg distinguish no-match 1 from error 2), so its repair is an exit-code read beside every absence claim, with the armed standing-lines exit-code clause as the sibling and a shared checked-search helper as the stronger form. Class two, wrong-pattern silence (one of four): the check ran clean because its pattern could never match; no mechanism recognizes this in arbitrary output, so the control rule stands as the repair. Class three, scope over-claim (one of four): the check was correct and its silence true, and the claim shipped wider than the check's subject; the repair is a claim form that forces naming the instrument and its scope ("nothing found by scan S over scope Y"), which no control run would have caught. The finding that outranks the count: the rule fires at the claim-maker's moment of maximum confidence and demonstrably does not fire there, while three of the four were caught by a consumer reading the control or re-asking, and the fourth by the maker re-reading its own shipped claim, so the enforcement point that works is the claim's consumer, not its maker. Refined the same day by a fifth instance and a cross-seat hypothesis, recorded here so the thread's insight outlives the thread. The AI-OS worker's testable hypothesis: the four instances share one authorship shape, the check and its control written by one seat in one sitting from one mental model, so the control cannot detect the omission its author did not imagine, and the highest-risk checks are therefore the CONTROLLED ones, which carry diligence evidence while sharing the blind spot; a sweep for uncontrolled checks misses the class entirely, and a consumer who asks only whether a control existed is falsely satisfied by a same-model control. The coordinator tested it live and confirmed on its own board, the fifth instance: a nine-term enumerative bar whose control was the term list grepped against itself, incapable of failing by construction, already reported clean to the operator; a second list generated from a different model of the class produced one hit, cleared on inspection, so the verdict survived and the warrant did not. A sixth instance arrived from the worker seat while this record was being written, and it fired while verifying that the rule exists: a hand-authored character-class grep over the doctrine returned empty with the rule present in the file the whole time, and the catch was neither a control nor a consumer but redundancy, two narrower phrase greps in the same batch that both matched; so the discriminating move in every catch so far is one shape, the same question asked through a second, independently formulated instrument, whether that second instrument is a consumer's challenge, a maker's re-read, a different-model term list, or a redundant sibling query in the same batch. A seventh instance the same evening sits at a surface none of the six touched and adds a fourth class, certification moment against artifact moment: the store sync, fired by a peer seat's deliberate doctor fix pass rather than any scheduler, committed and pushed a board mid-edit under its whole-tree add, publishing bytes that failed two bars the seat had run minutes earlier, with every bar correct and every control speaking, because a bar certifies a moment and not a file while the sync commits at a different moment, so no exit-code read, control, or consumer challenge reaches the gap; the sharper reading, settled by the firing seat's own unprompted report, is that this class needs no malfunction and no timer, one seat's legitimate act carrying a blast radius neither seat could see from its own side. An eighth instance landed on the adjudicating coordinator itself inside the same pass, self-caught and self-corrected: a size figure taken partway through a rewrite was published as the finished one, with the input figure right and the arithmetic sound, because the finished size was never measured at all; nothing in the owning loop found it, and the catch was the close-out re-ground of a number about to go outward, the summary-outlives-its-source rule firing outside the loop that owned the figure. The refinement that instance adds to class four's repair, the coordinator's own reading adopted here: the repair is a measurement point as well as a claim form, since a loop that neither measures nor certifies its own output leaves both jobs to whoever happens to look later, and a moment-pin can only name the moment a measurement actually happened at. That class's repair is already armed in another costume, the instruments-not-prose moment-pin: a bar report names the commit it certifies, and the seat commits promptly after a sweep rather than editing a file it has certified; the coordinator adopted both locally the same evening. The unattended whole-tree sync staging is the adjacent item above, and this is a live instance of its unpriced consequences. The through-line of every instance above, which is this item's real subject: the gap between what an instrument measured and what the claim or the artifact actually covers, with the exit code, the scoped claim form, the different-model control, and the named commit as four faces of one coverage question. And the complement rule earned itself inside the hour it was proposed: the published board's stray soft hyphen was caught by the complement-formulated non-ASCII bar, a character no enumerative list would have thought to include. The distinction that fell out, the coordinator's own: the weakness is specific to enumerative checks, a hand-authored list of things to look for, while a complement check, defined by what it is not, has no list to be incomplete, and where a complement formulation exists it is strictly stronger than an enumeration plus a control. The kit holds independent corroboration in one narrow costume already, the recorded control-derivation lesson that a control derived from the instrument tests its mechanism and never its coverage. Fix shape: a reviewer-charter and status-round clause in the two-direction idiom, with the consumer's challenge asking the question that discriminates, whether the control could have failed at all, not merely whether one ran, and whether it exercised the direction opposite its subject's expected answer, because a control that agrees with the result it checks is one defect speaking twice (the field instance: a marker pattern truncated by one character and an absent-marker control both returned absent, two agreeing absences reading as confirmation, caught only by a positive control built to hold the marker); the exit-code helper for class one; the scoped-claim form for class three; and two write-time rules for the testing-discipline surface, prefer the complement formulation where one exists, and where a check must enumerate, its control is specified from a model other than the check author's (another seat, the consumer's challenge, or a list derived from the spec rather than from the check). Sized for the follow-on after the queue drains, or folded into the corpus-audit follow-on if its census lands first. Signal: the next errored-empty incident, or the next absence claim consumed without a challenge.

- **docs/architecture.md still describes the retired kaizen routing (2026-08-29, from the standing-capture grant; file was worker-held at edit time).** The seat-duties paragraph (around line 278) states that every non-coordinator seat routes captured friction to the coordinator by message because only that seat holds the operator's nod. The grant retired both the nod and the mandatory route: capture is standing-authorized for every session, adjudication is standing at the coordinator and the kit expert seat, and the kaizen, coordinator, role, and peer-sessions skills already say so. Fix shape: align the paragraph with role/SKILL.md's rewritten duty (append directly, public-board cap, adjudication seats named by the kaizen skill). Signal: the file leaving the worker's dirty set; any session may take it then.

- **The doctor decodes the files it reads but not the child processes it runs (parked 2026-08-30, recorded as a Section 12 exclusion rather than a miss).** Windows PowerShell 5.1 decodes a child process's stdout through the console output codepage, which is the OEM codepage rather than UTF-8, so the doctor's `git ls-files` and `git config core.hooksPath` calls can mangle a non-ASCII path before it reaches a report. Section 12's scope was every file the doctor parses, and this is the same defect class one boundary over, at a channel that section deliberately did not open. The fix shape is not the same one either: the file reads took a flag, and this takes either an explicit decode of the captured bytes or a scoped codepage change that is restored, since setting the console encoding leaks to every later process on the host. Signal: the first report that shows a mangled path from a git call, or any work that touches those call sites.
- **Absorb the discord-channels relay into the kit repo, so one repo and one doctor run own the whole machine (parked 2026-08-30, operator wish at the keyboard).** The operator standard is recorded in the operator tier (customization-converges-on-the-kit-repo-and-its-doctor): every customization comes from claude-kit and a single doctor run checks full machine health; the relay is the standing exception, installed and run from its own repo (memory stores exist for two checkouts, sapplefeld-channels and discord-channels, so a scout starts by resolving which is live). The effort when called: scout the relay repo, then a design round on what moves (the broker service, the plugin, the allowlist config), what the doctor gains (install, update, health), and what the migration costs on a machine mid-service. The judgment sidecar post-field-trial packaging lands the same direction first and is the smaller instance to learn from. Signal: the operator calls for it, or the sidecar field trial closes and its packaging round opens.

- **The memory store root is gated behind an explicit allow-data flag while the endpoint config path reads a bare home directory (parked 2026-08-30, from the judgment sidecar's Section 6 review round; deliberately outside that section's bound).** Two paths that both decide where machine-local state is read from are screened to different standards in the same tree. `memq` refuses an inherited `KIT_MEMORY_ROOT` unless `KIT_MEMORY_ROOT_ALLOW_DATA` is also set, on the reasoning that an environment variable arriving from an unknown parent must not silently move where memories are written. The endpoint configuration path, which decides which machine every judged query and every candidate set is posted to, resolves from the home directory with no comparable gate. Neither is wrong on its own terms: the store path is a write surface and the config path is a read of a file the operator authors, and no exploit follows from the asymmetry by itself. What makes it worth a decision rather than a shrug is that an auditor comparing the two will ask why one answer was reached twice, and the tree currently has no written answer. Section 6 could not close it because its stated bound excludes memq's store verbs, and changing the store gate is a behaviour change to a rule that section does not own. Fix shape: either state the difference as an argued accepted risk in `docs/security-model.md` in the form that document already uses for writer-scoping, or give the endpoint config path a screen of its own and single-source the predicate. Signal: the next change to either gate, or the sidecar's post-field-trial packaging round, which is when the endpoint config stops being one machine's hand-authored file.

- **Claude-kit gains branch protections and a release channel (queued 2026-08-30 by the operator's direction in the gate-cadence design dialog; wakes when the armed queue drains).** Direction: the repo follows the recognizable pattern rather than carrying a testing-rule exemption: PR-required protection on main, no direct pushes. Sequencing: land after the armed queue drains, because every queued plan's header reads Commit-and-Push and direct-pushes main per section, so flipping mid-queue invalidates the commit model every plan names. Phasing: protection first with self-merge allowed and no required checks; CI becomes a required check only once a runner-green suite definition exists, since the suite carries box-conditional expectations (the temp-prefix path-length test reds only under a default-length temp prefix, and zip sizes differ by PowerShell host), so a required-but-red check would freeze merges. PR-per-plan makes main the release channel, which subsumes explicit kit versioning for the update nudge unless sub-plan releases are wanted. The open cost to price at pickup: spec and index work currently direct-pushes main several times a session, so doc-only work needs either self-merge without review or batched doc PRs. The gate-cadence plan's pre-push clause is keyed on the surface, so it stops matching claude-kit the day protections land, with no rule edit.

- **The stranded-recovery path integrates a tree neither side had and no gate reaches it (2026-08-31, from the gate-cadence Section 7 carrier sweep; found and deliberately not repaired there).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/branch-hygiene/SKILL.md`'s stranded recovery cherry-picks a commit range onto a fresh base and then pushes that branch and opens a PR. A cherry-pick onto a fresh base produces a tree neither parent had, which is exactly the property the gate cadence's merge moment rests on, and yet no gate runs anywhere on that path: it sits outside the finishing pass, so the Branch-and-PR merge gate never reaches it, and the push lands on a recovery branch rather than an install-surface trunk, so the pre-push condition genuinely does not fire either. The sweep left it because closing it means extending the merge moment to name cherry-pick, which is a change to the cadence's design rather than a carrier repair, and Standing Brief Amendment 1 permits reporting over fixing on exactly that boundary. Fix shape: decide whether the whole-gate moment keys on merge-the-git-operation or on integration-produces-an-unseen-tree, and if the latter, say so in the doctrine's gate bullet and name the lane in branch-hygiene's step 4. The second reading is the one the cadence's own reason supports, since the failure it names is redness in files neither parent changed. Signal: the next stranded-recovery run, or the next change to either the merge moment or branch-hygiene's procedure.

- **A doc-only push to an install-surface trunk now prices a full gate, and nothing exempts it (2026-08-31, from the gate-cadence Section 7 carrier sweep; declined there as a change to operator-approved text).** The pre-push whole-gate condition keys on the surface a push lands on, so under Commit-and-Push every push to claude-kit's main earns the whole gate whatever the diff carries. Two rituals push doc-only changes to main under that model: executing-work's interim-board entry, and the kaizen note capture, which commits and pushes a note file alone so the inbox syncs across machines without waiting on a pass. The kaizen case is the sharper of the two, because that capture is standing-authorized in every session and is routinely executed from a session working in a different repo, which has no lane, no baseline, and often no checkout of the repo whose gate the push would owe. On the plain reading each now owes a full suite to ship a paragraph, which on a box where the gate runs twenty-five minutes is a real cost attached to the cheapest possible change, and a rule that expensive on a one-line note is one sessions will quietly skip, which is worse than no rule. The backlog-entry ride is not a member: it is committed with the Chapter and the section's code, so it travels on the section-close push, which owes its gate on its own account. One of the two is now settled and the other is not. The kaizen note push carries an exemption stated at its own step, on a narrow rule about what the push can break rather than about a file path: with the note file alone staged it appends one line to an inbox no test takes as a subject, and a commit carrying anything beside that file is a different push taking whatever lane its own surface earns. The premise was verified rather than assumed, every test touching `kaizen/` building its own temp fixture and none reading the repo's real inbox. The interim-board push is untouched and still prices a full gate, and no exemption is written for it, because the general narrowing is a change to a sentence the operator approved verbatim and is a design call rather than a carrier repair. Worth noting that this dissolves on its own the day branch protections land, since the clause stops matching claude-kit entirely; so the decision is whether to carve a doc-only exemption now or accept the cost for the window until then. Fix shape, if taken: state the exemption in terms of what the push can break rather than in terms of file paths, since a docs path is not what makes a change safe. Signal: the first time a session pays a full gate to push an interim board entry, or the branch-protections item above being picked up.

- **The kaizen routing duty lost its stated reason and now has none (2026-08-31, from the gate-cadence finishing pass's docs curation; the false clause was excised there, the design question was not answered).** `docs/architecture.md` gave every non-coordinator seat a duty to route captured kit friction to the machine's coordinator by message, and justified the message route by the authority: it claimed the kaizen capture bar puts the inbox behind the operator's nod, that only the coordinator's function holds that nod, and that there is therefore no artifact a capturing seat could write that would reach the party who acts on the note. `plugins/claude-kit/skills/kaizen/SKILL.md` now states the opposite in terms, that capture is standing-authorized for every session, seated or not, with no per-note approval and no routing through any seat, the append itself being the capture. So the premise the route rested on is gone: a capturing seat can and does write exactly the artifact the document says it cannot. The falsifying change belongs to a sibling effort rather than to the gate cadence, and the curation pass repaired only the untrue clause, replacing it with the skill's own words plus the narrow consequence that what routes to the coordinator is the dispositioning rather than the authority to capture. What is left open is whether the duty itself still earns its place: if the append is the capture and the inbox syncs across machines on its own, a routing message to the coordinator may be redundant with the artifact, or it may still be worth its cost as an interrupt that says look at the inbox now, which is the same doc-is-the-record-message-is-the-interrupt split the peer-sessions skill draws. Answering it inside the curation pass would have meant inventing a rationale to replace a false one, which is worse than the falsehood. Fix shape: decide whether the routing message survives as an interrupt, is narrowed to friction that cannot wait for a pass, or is retired in favour of the append plus the coordinator's own inbox poll; then state the surviving reason in `docs/architecture.md` rather than leaving the duty reason-free as it now stands. Signal: the next kaizen pass, or the next edit to either the capture bar or the seat-duty paragraph.
- **The suite has never run on a non-Windows platform, so every platform-conditional claim in it is argued rather than measured (parked 2026-08-31, from the judgment sidecar's section 7; recorded as a declared assumption of that section rather than a miss).** This repository has no CI configuration and this machine has no WSL, so `node --test test/*.test.js` has only ever been run on win32. Most of the suite is platform-neutral and this costs nothing; the exception is code whose whole subject is filesystem semantics. The live-store screen in `sidecar/state-screen.js` is the current instance: its Windows behaviour is measured against OS-derived spellings (UNC, extended length, volume GUID, 8.3, junctions) and its POSIX behaviour is reasoning about `path.posix` and `realpath(3)`. That module's own history is why the gap is worth a line rather than a shrug: it was found accepting a path inside the live tree six times in one section, every time through a case nobody had thought to write down, which is exactly the shape an unrun platform hides. What closes it is a runner, container or CI, that executes the suite on Linux and macOS. Note the ordering constraint the branch-protections item above already records: the suite carries box-conditional expectations (a temp-prefix path-length red, zip sizes differing by PowerShell host), so a runner-green suite definition has to exist before any of this becomes a required check. Signal: the branch-protections item is picked up, or any further work on filesystem-semantics code.
- **The judgment sidecar's transcript distiller is parked by design, waiting on a field trial and an operator call (parked 2026-08-31, from the judgment sidecar plan's section 8).** This is a designed deferral rather than work left undone, and the distinction is load-bearing: the plan reached Complete at section 7 deliberately, because section 8's own acceptance reads "deferred to the design pass", so there is no buildable acceptance a run could have reached. What it would build: end-of-session transcript extraction, chunked map-reduce distillation sized well inside the standing context, candidate memory drafts and contradiction flags written to a pending file for session or operator adjudication, with budget stated in the prompt plus validate-and-re-ask, since the model composes past schema caps and constrained decoding then truncates mid-sentence. Two things get decided at the design pass rather than now: how it interacts with the shipped memory-recognition hook, which did not exist when section 8 was written, and whether a distiller earns its keep at all once recognition is running in the field. The pass itself is specified as fable-tier. The field trial of sections 1 through 7 has run: the daemon is live against the real store on this machine and both operator-only acceptance criteria passed on 2026-09-03 (receipts in `archive/backlog-2026-Q3.md` under that date). Signal: the operator calls for the design pass.


- **The invisible-character neutralizer is shared by four consumers that each compile it with their own flags, and the fourth was found by breakage rather than by a check (2026-08-31, from the judgment sidecar's finishing fix round).** `UNSAFE_PATTERN` in `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-endpoint-lib.js` is a joined list of escape strings rather than a literal character class, deliberately, so the source file does not contain the bytes it screens for and does not drop out of the repository's own hygiene sweeps. The cost of that shape is that each consumer calls `new RegExp` itself and chooses its own flags. When the class gained a supplementary-plane range and the `u` flag with it, `sidecar/harvest.js` was still compiling the shared pattern with `g` alone and became a load-time `SyntaxError`, taking a whole suite down. That was caught because it was loud; a future consumer whose flag mismatch is merely wrong rather than fatal would not be. There is also a spelling pin asserting the hook's copy and the library's copy are byte-identical, and it is worth knowing what that pin cannot do: it pins the two equal, so when the class was too narrow it propagated the gap to both surfaces faithfully and reported green. Fix shape: export a compiled regular expression, or a `neutralize` helper, from the library so no consumer chooses flags, and keep the byte-identity pin for the hook copy that cannot require the library. Signal: a fifth consumer, or the next change to the character class.

- **Stripping the variation-selector range degrades emoji in the sidecar's advisory text (2026-08-31, from the judgment sidecar's finishing fix round; accepted rather than fixed).** The neutralizer now strips `U+FE00` through `U+FE0F`, which includes VS16, the selector that asks for the emoji rendering of a dual-presentation character. So a heart or a warning sign in captured text can arrive at the session as its monochrome text form rather than as the emoji. This is accepted because the exposure is narrow and the alternative is worse: the consumers are sidecar-internal advisory text plus the capture hook, `memq` does not consume the neutralizer, and carving VS16 out of the range would reopen a genuine steering channel to close a cosmetic one. Recorded so that a later report of "the sidecar mangles my emoji" is met with a decision rather than a bug hunt. Signal: a consumer of the neutralizer whose output is user-facing prose rather than advisory pointers.

- **`test/kit-sidecar-capture.test.js` closes the live-store class by per-call-site discipline where its two sibling suites close it by structure (2026-08-31, from the judgment sidecar's finishing fix round).** The live-store class reached ten instances in that effort, and the thing that finally held was structural rather than behavioural: one spawn helper naming the interpreter at a single site, so a later call site cannot be written without a fixture home. `test/kit-sidecar-battery.test.js`, `test/kit-sidecar-daemon.test.js` and `test/kit-sidecar-rollup.test.js` all carry that shape and a pin asserting the interpreter token appears exactly once. The capture suite does not: it has three separate `process.execPath` sites, and all three pin `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` to a fixture home correctly today. That is per-call-site discipline, which is precisely the discipline that failed eight consecutive times in the battery file, once under a file header asserting the opposite. Nothing is wrong today and this is a guard item, not a defect. Fix shape: give the capture suite the same single-site spawn helper and the same structural pin. Signal: a fourth spawn site added to that file, which is the moment discipline has to be remembered rather than enforced.

- **The sidecar inbox lock's stale-reap has a race that duplicates advisory text (2026-08-31, from the judgment sidecar's finishing adversarial review; real, inferred rather than reproduced, and deliberately shipped).** `acquireInboxLock` reaps a lock it judges stale, and two writers can judge the same lock stale in the same window and both proceed. The blast radius is bounded and was priced before the call was made: the worst outcome is a delivery item written twice, so a session sees one advisory pointer duplicated, and there is no corruption of the inbox file and no loss. It was not fixed in the finishing round because the fix is a concurrency rewrite of the acquire path rather than a pin, and a concurrency rewrite landing in a fix round whose other five items were all red-first pins is the shape that ships a worse bug than it closes. Fix shape: an atomic create-exclusive acquire with the staleness judgment made by the holder of the new lock rather than by each contender independently. Signal: the first duplicated pointer observed in the field, or the daemon gaining a second writer.

- **Three surfaces restate the coordinator board's commitment categories by hand, and nothing pins them to the ledger they summarize (2026-08-31, from the standing-lines Section 4 security and adversarial reviews; the three restatements themselves were brought current by that plan's finishing curation, which leaves the durable half open).** The categories are enumerated inside `plugins/claude-kit/skills/coordinator/SKILL.md`, and three summaries of them live outside that file: the board-holdings list and the board-carried-path classes in `docs/security-model.md`, and the count of branches read for defects and never performed in `docs/architecture.md`. All three were short before the standing-lines plan widened the set with the routed-finding category, and all three were repaired by hand during that plan's finishing pass, so none of them is wrong today. What is unresolved is that a hand-edit is the only thing keeping them right: the set has now grown once with three separate summaries needing three separate repairs, and nothing would catch the next growth. The cheap durable answer is a pin over the category set rather than a fourth round of hand-edits. Fix shape: a test that reads the category list out of the coordinator skill and asserts each restatement covers it, or a single-sourced list the three surfaces cite rather than restate. Signal: the next commitment category added to the ledger.
- **A finding routed to a seat that never answers has no terminal path, and the spec deliberately did not invent one (2026-08-31, from the standing-lines finishing pass; routed to the operator as a design decision rather than filled).** The coordinator skill now carries routed findings as a board category: the line stays open until it carries a disposition pointer or an explicit open marker, and a successor does not sweep past an open one however stale it reads. That is exactly the rule the spec asked for, and it is silent on the one state it cannot resolve by itself, a finding sent to a seat that is retired, wedged, or simply never replies, whose line then rides every successor's board forever with nothing that can ever close it. Three fix rounds during Section 4 each spawned their own defect while building machinery to close that state (a re-ask cadence, an escalation threshold, an operator disposal), and round three reverted all of it to the spec's own scope, which is why the gap is recorded here rather than filled: picking a terminal path is a design call about what a seat owes a commitment it cannot discharge, and it is the operator's. Fix shape, if wanted: a bounded number of re-asks before the line converts to an operator escalation, or an explicit expiry that retires the line with a recorded reason. Signal: a board carrying a routed-finding line nobody can answer, or any further work on the coordinator's board categories.

- **Five wording questions raised by the doctrine's withheld-control split are unanswered, because the text is operator-approved verbatim and every repair edits it (2026-08-31, from the standing-lines finishing pass; sections 3 and 5 each routed rather than took theirs).** The rule that a silent check earns its silence with a control that spoke now sits under its own bold lead, split out of "Make the test earn its green", and the split left five seams that an implementer could not repair without editing signed-off wording. (1) "The bar covers any check that proves an absence..." opens the new bullet with no antecedent inside it; in the merged bullet the phrase read "The same bar reaches past tests" and pointed back at the earn-its-green bar. Repair: "This bar" or "The control bar". (2) The bold lead states an absolute the body then qualifies: the lead says a silent check earns its silence with a withheld control, while the body's last sentence provides for the case where none can be run and directs that the silence be called unproven. Repair: a qualifier in the lead. (3) The tree-mutating-probe discipline (run one with no agent reading the tree, restore from pre-probe byte copies, verify against those copies) stayed in the retained bullet, while the act that most needs it, running a check against a state known to hold the thing, is now in the new one with no pointer to it. Repair: a pointer sentence. (4) Section 3's widened exit-code rule is unreachable from where it now binds: it sits inside a bullet whose bold lead names test lanes, while the reader it targets is authoring a silent grep and is reading the absence-check bullet. Repair: a pointer from the new bullet to the exit-code rule. (5) "A check whose subject is a class owes the coverage answer either way" now follows the sibling-subject sentence, so the two ways it names, a control drawn from the pattern's own literals versus one withheld and matched on shape, sit two sentences back. Repair: name them. All five take the same vehicle, a further sentence appended to the new bullet rather than an edit inside the approved wording, so answering them is cheap and answering none of them costs only the seams above. Signal: the operator rules on any of them, or the next edit to that bullet.

- **The standing-lines plan's Standing Amendment 3 names the doctrine's source and mirror in reverse, and the amendment is operator-approved text (2026-08-31, from the standing-lines finishing pass; surfaced rather than edited).** The amendment names `home/claude-kit-doctrine.md` as the source a doctrine edit is made in. `test/doctrine-parity.test.js` names the opposite pair: `plugins/claude-kit/skills/operating-instructions/SKILL.md` is its `SKILL` (the source) and `home/claude-kit-doctrine.md` is its `MIRROR`. The amendment also omits the operating-instructions copy entirely, so a run following it literally edits one file where the parity pin requires two and reds the suite. Both of this effort's doctrine sections resolved the edit set from the test's own constants instead and shipped green, so nothing is broken; what is unresolved is which file the kit means to call the source, since the amendment and the pin disagree and only one of them is enforced. Note the third copy is not a candidate either way: `plugins/claude-kit/claude-kit-doctrine.md` is gitignored build staging regenerated by `build.ps1:77` and is never hand-edited. Fix shape: correct the amendment's wording in whichever plan carries it forward, or rename the test's constants if the intended source is `home/`. Signal: the next plan that writes a Standing Amendment about doctrine edits.

- **A plugin build measured 4.0 KB apart from a sibling where project memory records the host-to-host gap as about 1.5 KB, and the discrepancy is unexplained (2026-08-31, noticed during the standing-lines finishing pass; not investigated, because nothing in that effort depended on it).** The memory record `build-size-differs-by-powershell-host` exists precisely so a build figure is compared only against one from the same PowerShell host, and it states the expected gap as roughly 1.5 KB between pwsh 7 and Windows PowerShell 5.1. The gap observed here is larger by enough that the recorded figure may be stale, or the comparison may have crossed something other than the host (a payload that genuinely grew, a different compression path). This is recorded rather than chased because it is a measurement question with no defect behind it yet, and because a memory contradicted by evidence gets fixed rather than routed around, so whoever confirms the real figure should correct or delete that record in the same turn. Fix shape: build once on each host from one clean tree and record both sizes. Signal: any effort that compares build sizes, or the next doctor run that reports one.

- **The memq-shim stale-detection test fails every run on the operator's primary workstation, hardened from the intermittent the baseline memory used to record, and the miss is real detection scope: content-swapped JS shims are not flagged (2026-08-31, from the sidecar staleness effort's whole gate; routed because the memq shim doctor is a different goal than the sidecar pipe).** `test/memq-shim.test.js` > "the status check reports a content-swapped shim as stale, which is what makes -Fix reach it" fails 3/3 on 2026-08-31 (one whole gate, two solo runs) with the stale list coming back `['memq.cmd', 'memq.ps1']` against an expectation that also names `kit-statusline.js` and `memq-shim.js`, so the status check sees the wrapper pair and misses the two content-swapped JS shims. Reproduced at HEAD bc57e15 in a clean worktree with no in-flight diff, and the commits since the last green measurement touch only a plan doc, so no changeset explains the flip; machine state (an installed-shim change, a build-stamp interaction) or a latent code path are the open suspects. The project memory `suite-baseline-is-not-zero-fail` is already corrected to carry it as the box's second standing fail. Fix shape: run the status check by hand against a deliberately content-swapped JS shim, read which comparison source it consults for the JS pair versus the wrappers, and either fix the check or the test's expectation. Signal: any doctor -Fix run that claims shim health, or the next baseline diff on this box.

- **Two hooks read files their own writers create through the shared bounded reader with no link refusal, where the reader now offers one (2026-08-31, from the durable-boundary plan's section 2; routed out because hardening those hooks is a different goal than this plan's).** `readFileBounded` in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-read-lib.js` follows a symlink at the final component by design and now takes an opt-in `{ refuseLink: true }`. Two callers meet the reader's own stated criterion for wanting it and do not take it: `memory-recognition-nudge.js:1295` reads `.kit/memory-recognition-nudges.jsonl`, a file that same hook creates and appends to, on a PostToolUse path where a link into a dead network mount stalls the open; and `session-start.js:708` reads `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json` on a session-blocking hook with neither a link refusal nor a network screen. A third, `memory-recognition-nudge.js:720`, hand-writes the lstat guard the option would now retire. Enumerated with a per-caller verdict by that section's fix round 4 and correctly left unedited, since editing outside a section's file list leaves the committed tree calling a symbol that exists only in an unstaged worktree. Fix shape: adopt the option at the two unguarded sites and replace the hand-written copy at the third, then run each hook's own suite. Signal: any further work on the recognition nudge or on session-start's plugin-view read.

- **The sidecar staleness-and-liveness rollout is operator-gated on two acts, and each carries its own reopen condition (parked 2026-09-01, operator-only, from that plan's finishing pass; the plan is archived with the same items in its final Chapter).** The daemon and wrapper changes run only after the live daemon (pid 25828 at close-out) is stopped and the scheduled task's next tick relaunches it from the updated source: until then the real state root has no `logs/heartbeat.json` and the real `daemon-task.log` stand-down lines read `heartbeat unreadable (no file)`, which is correct for old code and would be a section 2 defect after the restart (no stamp within a minute of a new daemon starting reopens it). The capture-hook cap split reaches sessions only after `claude plugin update` plus session restarts (the standing fleet-update item above); a delivered advisory block still breaking at one item after that reopens section 3. Signal: the operator restarts the daemon, or the next fleet plugin update lands.

- **The memory-sync state file writes timestamps in two formats, a structural defect conceded to the kit's backlog rather than any in-flight changeset (parked 2026-09-01; found by the machine coordinator, its board carries the finding and the concession dated 2026-08-31; reported from that board, not re-derived here).** `kit-sync-state.json` at the store root round-trips its timestamps through two spellings, so a reader parsing one format meets the other. Fix shape: one writer, one format, and a read that refuses rather than guesses. Signal: any work on the store's sync runner or its state file.

- **Two disagreeing copies of the sanctioned memory-sync script exist by construction, and the durable fix is a resolution rule rather than a path (parked 2026-09-01; from the machine coordinator's board finding, conceded by this seat 2026-08-31 after verifying the ancestry first-hand: the installed revision is an ancestor of the trunk, 13 commits behind at the check).** The installed plugin cache and the checkout both carry the script, the fleet update closes the gap only until the next commit reopens it, and a wrong copy fails silently (exit 0). The rule to state wherever the script is named: THE SANCTIONED COPY IS THE ONE THE UNATTENDED RUNNER ITSELF EXECUTES, so a check compares against what the runner runs, never against whichever copy is nearest. Signal: the next edit to the sync script or to any doc naming its path.

- **`logs.js` `saveState` keeps a check-then-write shape its heartbeat sibling just abandoned, out of the sidecar effort's scope and recorded rather than swept in (parked 2026-09-01, from that effort's section 2 reviews).** The heartbeat writer now runs guard, then clear, then exclusive create, precisely because a check-then-write at a guarded name can be raced by a same-user writer; `saveState` still writes its sibling without the exclusive-create step. Same-user trust makes this a robustness gap rather than a vulnerability, which is why it did not ride the section. Fix shape: mirror the heartbeat writer's order at `saveState`'s sibling write. Signal: any further hardening pass over `sidecar/logs.js`.

- **The kit's two other ancestor-climbing `.git` detectors still fail open where memq's has just learned to fail closed (parked 2026-09-01; from the memory-read-side plan's section 2 round-6 security review, which rated it Minor and named it for routing rather than as a defect that round introduced).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/docs-write-guard.js:79` and `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/readonly-agent-guard.js:718` climb ancestors testing `fs.existsSync` for a `.git` entry, and that predicate answers false both for a dangling link and for an entry that is there but cannot be examined. memq's `isRepositoryRoot` now reads only ENOENT and ENOTDIR as absence, on the reasoning that a climb continuing past a checkout's root on an unreadable entry is the nested-checkout misdirection reached with no link in the path at all. This is the amendment's named class: a rule moving in one resolver while its siblings keep the old derivation. Not reachable today, which is why it parks rather than blocking: both guards derive their root from the payload cwd, which for a launched session is the checkout root that holds `.git`, so the climb terminates at step 0 and neither overshoot (which would relocate the KIT_ONLY `.kit` write allowance to a directory above the true root) nor undershoot reaches. Fix shape: adopt memq's error-code discrimination in both, or single-source one detector that all three call. Signal: any change to either guard's root derivation, or a caller that hands one of them a working directory below the checkout root.

- **`memoryRoot` reaches `os.homedir()` unguarded, so an unresolvable home directory still silences the whole SessionStart hook (parked 2026-09-01; from the memory-read-side plan's section 2 round-7 review, where two lenses rated it Major and the security lens Minor).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js:512` ends in `path.join(os.homedir(), '.claude')` with no guard, and `os.homedir` throws on a POSIX process whose `HOME` is unset with no passwd entry for the effective uid. The hook's ordinary branch reaches it immediately, `decayNudge` to `projectMemoryDir` to `projectsRootPath` at `memq.js:1505` to `memoryRoot`, and `memory-session.js`'s tail catch turns the throw into total silence: no memory blocks at all, which is the outcome the refused-cwd guard exists to prevent while testing only the working directory's spelling. Section 2 guarded the same reach inside `sessionProjectFiling` and corrected the comment that claimed the closure was end to end, so what remains here is the reach itself. It routes rather than folding because the fix reaches every verb's contract: `memoryRoot` returns a path today and every caller assumes one, so making it decline is a contract change across the CLI rather than a change within this section's spirit. It is also invisible to the suite by construction, and that is the more useful signal: `test/memq.test.js:133-142` pins `KIT_MEMORY_ROOT` with `KIT_MEMORY_ROOT_ALLOW_DATA=1` in every child, so the override short-circuits the call and no test on the current harness can reach the unguarded branch. A pin for it needs a child that runs with the store override absent. Fix shape: give `memoryRoot`'s homedir reach a declining envelope with an explicit answer its callers can read, or hoist the resolution to one guarded site the whole module shares. Signal: any work on memq's failure envelope, any new caller of `memoryRoot`, or a harness change that stops pinning the store override in test children.


- **The recognition nudge's per-call match budget charges one op per trigger, so one charged match op covers a glob's whole inner sweep while a `cmd` op covers a single containment test (parked 2026-09-02; from the memory-read-side plan's finishing security review, Minor 2, confirmed at the code by the orchestrator before parking).** `MATCH_OPS_MAX` is 2048 at `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memory-recognition-nudge.js:507`, and `collectHits` decrements `ops.left` exactly once per trigger considered (`:1727`) and once per anchor (`:1758`). The file's own ceiling comment at `:1520` states the bound as "the matrix of triggers against subjects", which is true of the count and not of the work: a `cmd:` or `err:` op is one containment test, where a `glob:` op runs `globMatchesPath` (`:775`) over the call's touched paths, and that function itself retries `matchSegments` at every start offset of the path, bounded per path by `PATH_SEGMENTS_MAX` rather than by the op budget. So the real per-call work is not proportional to the ops charged, and the budget's unit is not uniform across trigger types. It is not a live denial of service and that is why it parks rather than blocking: both inner loops carry their own segment cap, the touched-path list on a single tool call is small, and glob triggers are admitted from the project tier alone and from no tier at all under a store pin, so the population that can reach this path is one checkout's own authored records. It routes out of the memory-read-side plan because closing it is budget accounting rather than that plan's goal, which is the memory store's read side. Fix shape: charge the glob arm per touched path rather than per trigger, or give `globMatchesPath` its own budget drawn from the same `ops` object, and correct the `:1520` ceiling comment in the same change so the stated bound and the charged unit agree. Signal: any new trigger type whose match is a sweep rather than a test, any rise in `MATCH_OPS_MAX`, or a boundary that starts handing the nudge a large touched-path list.
- **The PIANO substrate: loops on mixed timers outside the main thinking loop (direction recorded 2026-09-03 by the operator in the design dialog; note at `docs/plans/claude-kit_piano-substrate_notes_v1.md`).** Fast loops that cancel or redirect a running task and slow loops for goal-setting, measurement and memory curation, hosted outside the harness, with the judgment sidecar as the first organ and the Claude Agent SDK as the candidate host. The note carries the inventory of loops the kit already runs and the five questions to settle first, authority and SDK billing ahead of mechanics. Signal: the armed queue and the subtraction cycle (bars, test audit, corpus audit and its cut) drain; then a brainstorm, with the design council offered on the authority fork.

- **The watch ledger has no destination for a durable fact about the watched system itself (parked 2026-09-03, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2 review).** The destination rule at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` line 81 routes a plan decision to that plan's Chapters and an instrument lesson to the memory store, and names no home for a durable fact about the watched system that is neither. The flipped placement default sends more traffic through that rule, so the gap is more reachable than it was, but it is pre-existing rather than this section's and the section is not widening line 81. Likely belongs with the corpus audit.
- **The watch chassis's own kind definitions fail the gating-definition rule this plan shipped (parked 2026-09-03, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2 review).** Section 1 landed a rule requiring an enumeration followed by a trailing general clause to say which the clause is, a summary of the examples or an extension past them. The situational kind at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` line 24 is exactly that shape and says neither, and the standing kind at line 23 is the same. The kind fork at line 32 settles the reading in practice for the doubt case, so this is a compliance gap in the chassis rather than a live ambiguity, and section 2 does not touch either line. Likely belongs with the corpus audit.
- **The watch chassis places a situational member that cannot satisfy its own re-derive obligation (parked 2026-09-03, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2 review round 15).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` line 24 places recent pings on the situational list by name, line 34 requires every situational sentence to carry the command that re-derives it, and line 47 bars re-deriving from anything but the watched system's own source. A ping the loop itself sent is reproduced by no such probe, so a recent ping is a situational member no probe reproduces and has no re-derive command it can carry. Both halves are pre-existing: they are byte-identical at base commit 8a50326, and section 2's edits only state the consequence aloud. The likely resolutions are to give the class a stated substitute for the re-derive field, as the coordinator's own told-not-derived carve-out already does by carrying the time and source of the evidence instead, or to carve the class out of line 34. Likely belongs with the corpus audit.
- **The coordinator's homing round keeps a line on the board on the sole ground that the board is its only copy (parked 2026-09-03, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2 review round 17).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/coordinator/SKILL.md` line 91 runs an already-boarded line through the routing as though it were being written now, and its cut invariant cuts only a line whose content it has confirmed landed somewhere else, so a line no destination takes stays on the board and becomes a finding for the operator. Run over the founding incident's board that keeps every narrative line no destination takes, at size, which is the rescue-by-only-record shape principle 3 of that plan calls not the load-bearing half. The invariant is pre-existing, predating base commit 8a50326, and it is a deliberate bound on a destructive act rather than an oversight, so the resolution is a design question rather than a correction: whether a boarded line no destination takes should be cut with its loss accepted, as the chassis accepts at write time, or kept as the operator finding the paragraph now makes it. Likely belongs with the corpus audit.
- **The watch chassis does not say whether one pass record survives the next pass (parked 2026-09-03, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2 review round 17).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` line 79 says to write every pass in a form that survives distillation, which reads as one record per pass; line 30 names the pass record in the singular as a class the destination rule places; and line 15 says the ledger holds what is true of the watched system right now, rewritten in place. Whether pass N's record survives pass N+1 is then left to the admission test's per-line refusal, which line 28 says is asked once at placement rather than on every pass. All three passages predate base commit 8a50326 and section 2's edits do not touch the tension, which matters because an unbounded accumulation of pass records is the founding incident's own failure mode arriving through a rule rather than through drift. Likely belongs with the corpus audit.
- **The coordinator's board carries a class its own two-kinds statement excludes (parked 2026-09-03, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2 review round 19).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/coordinator/SKILL.md` line 79 states that every line on the board is situational or a commitment and that the board carries no standing section at all, while inheriting the chassis's ledger rules unchanged save its named overrides. The chassis at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` line 30 names the read protocol as a class its own rules place on the ledger, and a read protocol is neither situational nor a commitment, so the coordinator's exhaustive statement excludes a class it inherits. Both statements predate base commit 8a50326 and section 2 did not create the tension; what the section changed is that the chassis now names its placed classes explicitly, which makes the seam visible where it was latent. The resolutions are for the coordinator to name the read protocol as the one non-kind thing its board carries, or to override it away outright. Likely belongs with the corpus audit.
- **A memq test's malformed-line fixture collides with its own valid fixture in a single ~10-hour window on 2026-09-04 and on no later date in any year (found 2026-09-04 while running `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md` section 2's close gate; the product code is correct).** `test/memq.test.js:5450` hardcodes the malformed usage line as the literal `{"ts":"2026-08-25T0` and seeds a valid applied stamp at `daysAgo(10)` in the same fixture. When `daysAgo(10)` resolves to 2026-08-25 and the UTC hour begins with `0`, the surviving valid line reads `{"ts":"2026-08-25T00:...` and contains the malformed literal as a substring, so the closing assertion at `:5490`, `!includes(torn)` under the message `the malformed line left the sidecar`, fails on correct output. The drop itself works: the same run's stderr matches `dropping 1 malformed line`, its stdout reports `dropped 1 malformed line`, and the `.bak` assertion beside it passes. The collision was reproduced directly rather than inferred, and the red reproduces solo as well as under the whole gate, so it is not contention. It is a test defect and not a product defect. Its window is one-time rather than annual, because the literal hardcodes the year: `daysAgo(10)` on 2027-09-04 serializes `2027-08-25`, which cannot contain a 2026 literal, so the collision recurs on no later date in any year. That one-window reach is why nothing caught it, and it is also why the red clears itself when 2026-09-04 10:00 UTC passes rather than coming back. Fix shape: derive the malformed literal from a date the fixture's own stamps cannot produce, or assert on the parsed line set rather than on a raw substring, which is the discriminating-assertion class this plan's own Standing Brief Amendment 6 names. Fixing it still earns its keep after the window closes, because the fixture is one year-bump away from colliding again and the raw-substring assertion is the defect rather than the date. Signal: the next edit to that test.

- **The watch chassis names no author for a keeper-learned standing DO-NOT, and no destination for one either (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md`'s finishing security review).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` names two sources for a prohibition, the operator's word and a governing document behind it (lines 30 and 32), and names the keeper as author only of a do-not-reopen trap (lines 30 and 40). A DO-NOT the keeper learned from the watched system itself, of the shape "do not restart this service, the restart corrupts its cache", is unconditioned, is about the watched system rather than the keeper's reasoning, states rather than argues, and re-opens nothing, so it is a genuine standing constraint that matches no named author. Line 81's destination legs are a plan decision and a lesson about the watch's own instruments, so such a line kept off the ledger is dropped, and line 28's cost table licenses that drop on the premise that what the default keeps off is by its nature the keeper's reasoning or an episode's narrative, which is false for it. The resolution is a design question rather than a correction, because it changes the chassis's author model: either name the keeper as a third author of a standing DO-NOT, or widen the trap class to cover a keeper-learned do-not about the watched system and say so, and in either case give line 81 a leg for a durable fact about the watched system. No live consumer exercises it today, since the coordinator carries no standing section at all (`plugins/claude-kit/skills/coordinator/SKILL.md` line 79). Belongs with the corpus audit or a chassis design pass.
- **The watch chassis's admission default still drops a DO-NOT-shaped line whose membership the keeper cannot call (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md`'s finishing adversarial and security reviews).** The default's doubt branch at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` line 30 takes three shapes, and the finishing round closed only the third, a recognised member whose particular line the keeper cannot say a successor needs. The second shape, a line the keeper cannot recognise as a member at all, still sends its doubt off the ledger, so a DO-NOT-shaped line of uncertain membership is kept off and then dropped for want of a destination leg. Line 28's own cost principle points the other way for that line, since per lines 32 and 53 the off-error licenses an act no later pass takes back while the on-error only forgoes an act that remains available. The second shape's text is unchanged from base commit 8a50326, so this is pre-existing rather than that round's, and it resolves with the author-model question above rather than separately: a recognition criterion for a prohibition is what removes the doubt, and stating one is the same contract change. Belongs with the item above.
- **The watch chassis's tick order names no ledger read, so a trap's binding point is unstated (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md`'s finishing security review).** At `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` the tick order runs from line 42, its step 2 re-derives from the watched system and its step 3 presumes the standing list is already in hand, and no step names the ledger read the read protocol at line 38 describes. The self-authored wake prompt at line 53 carries the standing prohibitions and a pointer to the ledger, so it carries no trap. Line 32 states that re-deriving a trap is the re-opening the trap exists to forbid, so a pass that re-derives before it reads the ledger can trip a trap the ledger holds. Pre-existing and untouched by that plan. The resolutions are to name the ledger read as the step between 1 and 2, or to carry traps in the wake prompt beside the prohibitions. Belongs with the corpus audit.
- **Two consequence claims in the watch chassis name mechanisms its own steps do not run (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md`'s finishing reviews).** Both are in `plugins/claude-kit/skills/standing-watch/SKILL.md` and both are the Standing Brief Amendment 3 class, a consequence stated without checking the rules that would produce it. Line 48's re-measure step re-reads the governing document only for a standing DO or DO-NOT that cites a condition, so a document-backed prohibition citing no condition is obeyed after its document drops it until the operator says otherwise; the cost is over-binding, which is the recoverable direction, so this is a liveness gap rather than a safety one. And line 28 prices the loss of a confirmed mechanism as a re-derivation under the tick order's re-derive step, while that step at line 47 re-derives the board rather than re-reading the system's source, so the cost of dropping a mechanism is a source read the tick order never schedules. Line 32 carries a related over-promise, that a prohibition kept wrongly forgoes an act "which a later pass or the operator's word can still take", which the finishing round's own text narrows by putting the binding question outside the keeper's hands. Belongs with the corpus audit.
- **Confirm the blind reader's gating-definition charge actually reaches a dispatched reader (2026-09-04, operator-only, from the gating-definitions close-out, `docs/archive/claude-kit_gating-definitions_spec_v1.md`).** The charter part that asks a reader for near-miss pairs per gating definition was verified only as a labelled proxy, an agent handed the new part's text verbatim over one document, because a dispatched `blind-reader` resolves the installed plugin copy and the install trails this checkout. The check needs `claude plugin update` and a fresh session, then one `blind-reader` dispatch over a document carrying a gating definition: the report should come back with three near-miss pairs per definition and a separating feature on each. A report without them means the wiring never delivered the instruction, whatever the shipped text says, and section 1 reopens as a new round.
- **Three queued plans carry no `## Dispatch Authorization` section at all (2026-09-04, from the gating-definitions close-out).** `claude-kit_subtraction-bars_spec_v1.md`, `claude-kit_checkpoint-session-validation_spec_v1.md` and `claude-kit_test-audit_spec_v1.md` carry no such heading, confirmed by an anchored grep over each with a control that spoke on a plan whose authorization is recorded. It bears on nothing about the current arming, whose `armedBy` field reads `operator` for all fourteen queued plans, and it does bear on a peer-dispatched handoff, where the committed section is the durable grant a receiver traces. Add the section to each, above `## Sections of Work`, which is the one position the frozen v1 machine contract leaves bounding nothing.
- **The audit artifact's account of what the machine coordinator seat writes omits the seat's own artifact-production write (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_board-routing-and-homing_spec_v1.md` section 5 review round 2).** `docs/security-model.md` enumerates the write surfaces the four-kinds routing sends content to, and that enumeration is now scoped to the routing rather than to the seat. What it does not describe anywhere is the write the coordinator skill's line 63 states as a charter function, that the seat writes the spec or backlog entry in the repo that owns the work, which is a file write into an arbitrary repository on the machine including public ones, and the kaizen skill's disposition step goes further by writing a brief and then clearing the note lines it handled on a multi-writer inbox. Both were confirmed at their own sources. Nothing states whether the board's two line bars and its readership reduction reach that artifact, and a Dispatch Authorization section is designed to carry operator words. It is out of scope for the plan that found it, whose section charters the routing's writes alone. Signal: the next edit to that document's coordinator section, or the first time a seat is asked what bars reach a spec it authors.
- **The coordinator's outcome journal is a directly writable, unauthenticated account of an irreversible act, and only its first moment's landing is confirmed (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_board-routing-and-homing_spec_v1.md` section 5 review round 2).** The frontmatter guard puts sidecars out of scope on every tier and `outcomes.jsonl` sits in the sync allowlist under a union merge, so any local session with the plain file tools and any machine on the store's remote can append or rewrite an entry with nothing authenticating one.
