Separately, the homing round now confirms that its first journal moment landed and confirms nothing about the second, so a round that completes the publish and the cut while its closing entry fails to land leaves a journal showing a lone `fail` entry, which reads as a round that died partway rather than as a completed irreversible cut. Both bounds belong to the memory-system skill's surface rather than to the coordinator contract, which is why the plan that found them declared them instead of closing them. Signal: any change to the journal's integrity story, or the first time a fail-only key has to be diagnosed.
- **The homing round's new confirming read of its own journal entry runs on an unfenced channel (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_board-routing-and-homing_spec_v1.md` section 5 review round 2).** The coordinator skill now requires a round to establish that its first journal entry landed before any destination write begins, and defers how to the memory-system skill, which is what that section's acceptance requires of it. Of the two journal readers that skill states, `memq recent` is fenced and `memq get` is not: it prints the entry's fields charset-reduced but unframed, and the guard table's fence exception names recall only. The journal replicates, so a confirming read can pull store content another machine wrote into the seat's context at a privileged moment. Naming a specific reader in the coordinator skill is refused rather than pending: that section's acceptance requires the how be deferred, and restating another skill's mechanism is the class this plan's amendments 8, 9 and 10 already cut. The repair belongs to the fence's owner. Signal: the next change to the guard table's fence exceptions.
- **A homing round's cut-remedy entry has no stated verdict, so a round's record can be three entries where the contract describes two (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `claude-kit_board-routing-and-homing_spec_v1.md` section 5 review round 2).** The coordinator skill states that a round writes at two moments, the first carrying `fail` and the second `pass`, and now states that an entry that landed cut is the memory-system skill's to remedy, whose remedy is to re-log the lost tail as its own entry. Nothing states what verdict that third entry carries or how a successor auditing the journal reads a round whose record is three entries. A cut is the expected outcome for exactly the rounds that earn one, since the first moment must name every line it intends to move for a board that has already failed the one-read test while the summary caps at 120 characters, so this is the ordinary case rather than an edge. The verdict grammar is the memory-system skill's. Signal: the first homing round whose first entry lands cut.

- **Both curated indexes promise a per-plan archive list that neither carries (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by the board-routing-and-homing close-out).** `docs/README.md` and `docs/plans/README.md` each carry a sentence saying that each entry below describes what that plan delivered when it shipped, and neither has a single entry below it, while `docs/archive/` holds 97 plan documents. The repository reflects the archive by a pointer instead, which is a working convention, so the choice is to delete the two sentences or to build the lists; either way one plan's close-out cannot answer it, since the sentence is a promise about 97 other plans.

- **The journey annotation on curated index lines is that file's convention rather than one effort's addition (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by the board-routing-and-homing finishing pass).** Index entries narrate when an effort was resumed or which update wave it followed, which the house style bars from a curated document, and sibling lines across both indexes carry it. Sweeping it is a pass over both files rather than a repair inside any one plan's charter.

- **The ownership map's third column cannot distinguish a pointer from a co-owner or an enforcer (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `docs/plans/claude-kit_subtraction-bars_spec_v1.md` sections 1 through 3 review rounds).** The column names the surfaces that speak to an owner's moment, and three review rounds across three sections circled it because a row cannot say whether a listed surface points at the owner, shares ownership, or mechanically enforces the rule. Rows 40 and 93 both went stale this plan in the same way: a new doctrine pointer landed and neither row could record it as a pointer rather than a second owner. Section 5 adds the two missing entries; the column's own expressiveness is the item here.

- **`prose-reviewer.md`'s severity ladder lists no style item under Major, while its new Surplus bullet rates one Major (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `docs/plans/claude-kit_subtraction-bars_spec_v1.md` section 2, both review lenses).** The charter's ladder at `:59` and `:60` puts style deviations under Minor and names no style case under Major, and the file's other `[style]` case is Minor. Section 2's Surplus bullet rates a restatement of an owner Major and tags it `[style]`, so a reviewer reading the ladder and a reviewer reading the bullet rate the same finding differently. Either the ladder gains the case or the bullet drops to Minor; the plan's Assumption 3 records the reversal as one severity line per charter, so this is a small deliberate call rather than a defect to fix blind.

- **`writing-skills/SKILL.md:21` states which forms a mention may take, and it is narrower than the doctrine (parked 2026-09-04, surfaced by `docs/plans/claude-kit_subtraction-bars_spec_v1.md` section 3's fix round).** That line permits a pointer or an operational residue at its point of action and says never a restatement, while the doctrine at `home/claude-kit-doctrine.md:41` also permits a surface that copies the rule whole under a parity pin or a build step, and `:74` of the same skill names a deliberate restatement across covered surfaces as a live category with its own disposition. So the kit's own owner of the one-owner rule omits two forms the doctrine and its own later section allow. Section 3 left `:21` alone because it is unchanged text outside that section's delta, which Standing Amendment 3 routes here. The fix is one clause at `:21`.

- **`kit-git-lib.js` offers no way to raise its 1 MiB output ceiling for one call, so a reader of a large tracked file has no supported route to git's copy of it (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4; routed out because the fix is another file's contract).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-git-lib.js` exports `gitRun`, `gitOutput`, `gitChildEnv`, `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `MAX_OUTPUT_BYTES`, and that last constant is 1,048,576 bytes hardcoded into the `spawnSync` `maxBuffer`. The `options` object every caller passes carries `timeoutMs` alone, so the ceiling is a module constant rather than a per-call argument and a caller needing more cannot ask. It bites today: `test/memq.test.js` is 1,481,542 bytes, over the cap, and it sits inside the corpus the new size reader measures, so `kit-size.js` cannot take a HEAD reading of the single largest file in the very set it exists to bound. Section 4 worked around it rather than fixing it, reading HEAD content only for the files git reports as changed and naming a HEAD read it needs and cannot get rather than omitting one silently, because a silent omission reads exactly like a file that did not change. What the workaround cannot do is give any caller the whole-corpus HEAD reading, so the next tool that wants one rediscovers this. Repair shape: let `options` carry a `maxOutputBytes` that defaults to the constant, so the ceiling stays where it is for every existing caller and a caller with a reason can raise it, with the reason that the ceiling exists (a runaway git command should not exhaust the box) preserved by keeping the default rather than removing the bound. Signal: the next tool that wants git's copy of a file it cannot size, or the next edit to that library's signature.

- **A repo-only development tool ships inside the plugin payload every install downloads, and nothing in the layout distinguishes the two kinds (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4; the section put it there because that is where the payload's script directory is).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-size.js` reads this repository's own curated-file sizes against `test/size-budget.json` and fails a ratchet. Its subject is this repo and nothing else: an installed kit has no `test/` directory, no budget file, and none of the measured roots outside the payload, so the verb is inert wherever it lands after an install while still riding in the download. Its neighbours in that directory are genuinely shipped tools, which is why it went there and why the shape is worth deciding rather than drifting: the directory now holds two kinds of script with no marker separating them, so the next author reads the precedent rather than the intent. The cost today is bytes and a reader's confusion rather than a defect, which is why this is a backlog item. Repair shape: decide whether the payload gains a stated convention for repo-only tooling (a `dev/` sibling excluded from the build, or a header line the build reads) or whether this tool moves out of the payload entirely, and record the decision where the build can enforce it. Signal: the second repo-only tool that wants a home, or the next build-size question.

- **`test/helpers/` is the natural home for the fixture-git helper two test files now want, and the new size classifier reds any tracked file it finds there (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4).** Section 4's `test/size-ratchet.test.js` builds real git fixture repositories under the system temp directory, and the same helper steps are the kind a sibling test file would reuse rather than re-author. The obvious home is a shared module under `test/helpers/`. The obstacle is the ratchet's own coverage control: it classifies `test/*.test.js` by lines and a test count, and any other tracked file under a measured root that no shape classifies and no named exclusion covers is a hard failure, which is deliberate, since that control is what proves a ratchet cannot green on a corpus it never measured. So a helper committed at `test/helpers/fixture-git.js` reds the gate until either the classifier gains a shape for it or the exclusion list gains its name, and neither choice is obvious: a shape means deciding what a helper's budget should even measure, while an exclusion means a tracked file under a measured root that nothing bounds. Repair shape: rule on how the size budget treats test support code before the first shared helper lands, so the decision is made once rather than as a gate-unblocking edit under time pressure. Signal: the second test file that wants the fixture builder, or the first `unclassified` failure naming a path under `test/helpers/`.

- **A `Bash(node:*)` permission rule would fail both screens of the grant audit, and the shape is inviting enough to be worth writing down before someone reaches for it (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4; a grant-hygiene note rather than a defect, since no such rule exists in this tree).** The security reviewer's checklist asks two questions of any command grant: does it name a specific command rather than an interpreter, and can the argument position be steered into something else. A rule permitting `node` with any arguments fails both. It names an interpreter, so the grant is really a grant of arbitrary code execution: `node -e '<anything>'` is inside it, as is `node <any path>`, which reaches any script a repository happens to carry and any file an earlier step wrote. And the argument position is the whole payload rather than a parameter, so there is nothing narrower to bound. The reason to record it is that the temptation is structural rather than careless: this kit's own tooling is node scripts, a session runs many of them, and each one otherwise costs a prompt, so `Bash(node:*)` is exactly the rule a tired operator writes to stop being asked. Repair shape: where a grant for this tooling is wanted, name the scripts rather than the interpreter, one rule per script path, and keep the interpreter-level grant out of the settings file; and where a reviewer meets a proposed one, this is the entry that says the audit was already run on it. Signal: the first proposal of an interpreter-level grant in any settings file this kit reads.

- **The fenced-block and heading screen that keeps a printed path from forging a Chapter line lives inside its first producer, so the next tool printing repository-supplied text into that block reimplements it by not implementing it (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4; routed out because the shared home is another file's export surface).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-size.js` renders every path it prints through `safePath`, which wraps the shared `safeForAuthorization` from `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal-lib.js` and then adds two strips of its own: a backtick run, which would close the fenced block the reading sits in, and a leading hash run, which would open a markdown heading. Both strips are properties of the destination rather than of this producer, that destination being the fenced block inside a plan-doc Chapter whose body the status line's plan parser reads line by line, keying on column zero exactly as the strips do. The kit's own rule is that a sanitizing guard belongs at the shared boundary the moment a channel gains a second producer, and this channel now has one producer carrying the guard and a shared library without it, so the second author inherits the weaker half by writing nothing. Repair shape: export the destination screen from `kit-goal-lib.js` beside `safeForAuthorization`, have `kit-size.js` call it, and leave the printable-ASCII screen where it already is. Signal: the second tool that prints repository-supplied text into a Chapter's fenced block, or the next edit to that library's export list.

- **The size ratchet's own suite reaches its subject through a child process at nearly every case, where the same functions are callable in process, so the file's wall clock is set by process spawns rather than by the work under test (2026-09-11, parked 2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4; kept because the test audit has now read this file and ruled every test in it without taking the cost judgment, so the question is open and the file has grown since the measurement below).** `test/size-ratchet.test.js` stood at 2,737 lines carrying 101 call sites of its `runScript` helper, which spawns the reader as a child process, beside 155 in-process call sites of the same module's exported functions and 5 spawns of git for fixture construction, measured on the operator's primary workstation on 2026-09-04 at that section's sixth fix round. Its lane read 154 tests in 119.9 s under a named foreign contention, so the per-case process cost is the dominant term rather than the assertions. The two shapes are not interchangeable and the file is right to hold both: a case whose subject is the command-line boundary itself, argument parsing, an exit code, or which stream a refusal lands on, is only testable through a spawn, while a case whose subject is a function's own return value is testable in process and pays the spawn for nothing. What is not established is the split between them, which is the question this item asks: how many of the spawn sites are boundary cases by subject. The file now stands at 3,428 lines carrying 161 `runScript` sites, measured 2026-09-11 at the test audit's finishing pass, so the cost this item names grew rather than shrank while the item waited. Repair shape: classify each `runScript` site by what its assertion actually reads, reduce the non-boundary ones to in-process calls against the already-exported surface, and keep one spawned case per distinct boundary claim rather than per behaviour. Signal: the lane's wall clock becoming a gate concern on a slower box. The other signal this item carried, the test audit reading this file, fired at that audit's section 8 without producing the classification, so it cannot fire again.

- **`docs/security-model.md` accounts for the configuration the kit suppresses when it runs git, and not for the configuration it cannot suppress: a repository handed to a reading tool carries its own git configuration, which no environment key the kit sets can reach (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 4; routed out because the file it wants is not in that section's scope).** The kit's shared git runner strips every `GIT_*` key from the children it spawns, and the size reader's suite additionally pins `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` at absent files, so the global and system scopes are accounted for. A repository's own `.git/config` is a third scope, read by git by construction because it belongs to the repository being operated on, and the size reader operates on whatever directory its `--repo` argument names. That much is confirmed by reading the runner's strip and the reader's argument handling. What is NOT established, and is the whole question this item exists to settle rather than to assert: whether any repository-local configuration key executes a command for the specific subcommands this tool runs, which are `rev-parse`, `ls-files`, `diff` and `show`. Some git configuration does name commands (pagers, filters, external diff drivers, a filesystem monitor), and whether any of those fire on this command set is unverified here. Treat the exposure as unquantified rather than as demonstrated. Repair shape: determine which repository-local keys reach those four subcommands, then either state the residual in that document beside the mutating-CLI exposure it already names, on the same pattern, or record that the command set reaches none of them, which is equally worth writing down. Signal: the next tool that reads a repository it did not create, or the next audit of that document's exposure list.
- **The repo README's payload map lists five of the eight tracked files under the plugin's `scripts/` directory, so three ship undescribed on the surface a reader treats as the map (2026-09-04, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 5 sighted review; routed out because only the one entry the new size reader falsified was in that section's scope).** `kit-endpoint-lib.js`, `memory-index.js` and `prompts/relevance-v1.js` appear nowhere in `README.md`, while `docs/architecture.md` names all eight, so the two surfaces disagree about what the directory holds and the shorter one is the one a newcomer opens first. Section 5 added the size reader's entry because the section owned that falsified claim; completing the map is a README audit rather than a claim repair, which is why it is here. Repair shape: add the three entries in the map's own aligned form, and decide at the same time whether the map is meant to be exhaustive, since a map that is complete by accident drifts back the next time a script lands. Signal: the next script added under that directory, or the next pass over the README's payload map.

- **`test/doctrine-parity.test.js` asks git a separate question per assertion, fifteen child spawns per run where the tracked listing one of them already takes answers every membership question in the file (2026-09-05, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 5 second review round; routed out because the repair rewrites a shared helper many tests call, which is not a change to make in a section's last fix round).** The `assertTrackedInIndex` helper spawns `git ls-files --error-unmatch` once per path it is handed, and fourteen call sites use it, while the cross-surface retire-class sweep in the same file already lists the whole tracked tree once. The testing-discipline skill names the cheaper form directly: a spawn per assertion becomes a spawn per batch. Repair shape: derive the tracked set once per run and have the helper test membership in that set, keeping its contract (a throw naming the untracked path) byte-identical so no caller changes, and confirm the set is derived lazily so a file whose tests never run pays nothing. Signal: the test audit plan, which re-reads this file against the retire classes anyway, or the next time this suite's wall clock is measured under contention.

- **`test/doctrine-parity.test.js` guards the read and enumeration sites its newest sweep added and leaves every sibling site in the same file unguarded, so one file holds two contradictory standards for the same boundary (2026-09-05, from the subtraction-bars plan's section 5 third review round; routed out because the file's whole read-site population is one audit rather than one site, and auditing seventy call sites is not a change to make in a section's last fix round).** The section's new cross-surface sweep enumerates through `git ls-files`, screens each path with `containedRealPath` and reads it with `readFileBounded`. Three sibling whole-tree sweeps in the same file enumerate with `readdirSync` over the worktree instead, which includes untracked files, follows a symlinked final component out of the checkout, applies no size ceiling, and in one case puts matched line text into an assertion message; and the new sweep's own vocabulary read, which derives every class pattern the sweep then judges against, goes through the file's bare `readRepoFile` rather than through the containment it applies to every other path, so a path resolving out of the tree there empties the carrier set and reads exactly like a clean tree. Honest bound on all of it: each needs local write access to the repository, and in that scenario the assertions themselves are attacker-controlled, so the value is consistency and defence in depth rather than a live exposure. The doctrine's own rule is the shape of the repair: a guard belongs to the channel rather than to the producer that first needed it, so the boundary's guard is applied at every call site or at none. Repair shape: enumerate the file's read and directory-walk sites, route each through the exported `readFileBounded` and `containedRealPath` its newest sweep already uses, and make the file-header disclosure state the result rather than an aspiration. Signal: the test audit plan, which re-reads this file against the retire classes anyway.


- **The chat-completions translation sends a JSON schema without `strict`, so a gateway that treats a non-strict schema as advisory returns an unconstrained answer and the degrade line cannot say why (2026-09-05, from section 1 of the endpoint-dialect-key plan's review round; confirmed at source, no observed failure).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-endpoint-lib.js` builds `response_format` as `{ type: "json_schema", json_schema: { name: "answer", schema: src.format } }` in `toChatCompletionsRequest` and sets no `strict: true`. On this fleet's llama.cpp server the schema does constrain, confirmed by a live `memq find` returning a well-formed ranking that `parseJudgedAnswer` accepted, so nothing is broken today. The exposure is a gateway whose json_schema support is advisory rather than enforcing: there the ollama path's native `format` grammar constrains and the chat path's does not, the answer fails validation instead, and the reader sees `the endpoint's answer was not a ranking` with nothing naming the dialect as the cause, since only the refusal branch names it. The translator is shared, so the sidecar daemon's judge and recognition calls inherit the property. Repair shape: decide whether the translation owes `strict: true`, which is not free because some gateways reject a strict schema outright and the daemon would inherit that too, and if it is added, pin both dialects' constraint behaviour against a server that enforces; the cheaper half, independent of that call, is to name the resolved dialect beside the endpoint fingerprint note so every degrade is placeable rather than only the refusal. Not handed off as its own spec because there is no observed defect to reproduce and the answer turns on gateway behaviour this fleet cannot exercise. Signal: a fleet machine moving to a gateway other than llama.cpp, or a judged or recognition degrade reading `was not a ranking` on a healthy endpoint.

- **The endpoint config loader accepts a url carrying a path and never says so, so the OpenAI convention produces a doubled path that only fails at the first real call (2026-09-05, from section 1 of the endpoint-dialect-key plan's blind review; confirmed at source).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-endpoint-lib.js`'s `loadEndpointConfig` validates the url with `/^https?:\/\/[^\s/]+/`, which is unanchored at its end, and strips only trailing slashes, so `http://gw:8080/v1` passes as valid. Every generation call then appends the dialect's own suffix, so that config posts to `/v1/v1/chat/completions` and the endpoint answers 404. Naming a base url ending in `/v1` is the standard OpenAI convention, so this is the misconfiguration an operator is most likely to write. Section 1 made the failure legible rather than silent: the refusal detail now names the path the request actually reached, so the doubled prefix shows up in the degrade line instead of reading as a correct path. What is still absent is a reading at load time, where the cost is one 404 round trip and a puzzled reader rather than a wrong answer. Repair shape: have the loader report a url whose parsed pathname is anything but empty or `/`, and equally a url carrying a query or a fragment, which the same unanchored check admits and which swallow the appended dialect suffix into the query or the fragment while the pathname still parses as `/`, so a check reading the pathname alone stays silent on exactly the two shapes where the suffix never reaches the wire. The reading is a warning beside the existing `api ignored` one rather than a refusal, since refusing would break a deployment that genuinely serves the dialect suffix under a prefix; the warning names the pathname it found and what the call will append. Deliberately not done in the endpoint-dialect-key plan, whose Approach states that the config parser stays as it is. Signal: the next plan that touches `loadEndpointConfig`, or an operator report of a 404 from a healthy endpoint.

- **The non-2xx refusal detail carries no length cap, so the 120-character print on memq's degrade line silently drops the dialect clause for a long posted path (2026-09-05, from section 2 of the endpoint-dialect-key plan's review rounds; confirmed at source, trigger unreachable on this fleet).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-endpoint-lib.js:611` builds the detail as `HTTP ${res.status} from ${postedPath} (${api} dialect)` and applies `MAX_DETAIL_CHARS` (200) nowhere on it, while the sibling refusal at `:631` does apply it. memq prints the detail through `sanitize(sent.detail, 120)` at `memq.js:6443`, a bare slice with no truncation marker. The fixed part of that string is 31 characters, so the dialect clause starts falling off the end once `postedPath` passes 89; `postedPath` is the configured pathname joined with the dialect's own suffix, so in configured-path terms that is roughly 69 characters under `/v1/chat/completions` or 76 under `/api/generate`. The dropped clause is the one fact the change that added it exists to supply. This is not a defect in the transport's own contract, which does name all three values, so the surface is the printing one rather than the naming one, which is why it took the route out of the plan rather than an appended section. The daemon's own leg carries that same uncapped detail into a durable gap record and onto stderr with no bound at all before the rollup's 2000-character render, which is unbounded operator-authored text rather than an exposure and so rides here as a clause rather than as an item of its own. The trigger is empty on this fleet, where every machine's config names a bare host and port with no path at all. Repair shape: capping the assembled detail at the transport as its sibling does fixes nothing by itself, since 200 leaves memq's 120 the tighter bound; the two shapes that actually hold the clause are bounding the path component so the assembled detail fits inside the printing surface's own cap, or ordering the dialect ahead of the variable-length path, which section 1 of that plan pins as `HTTP <status> from <path> (<dialect> dialect)` and which would therefore be approval drift. Pin whichever shape is chosen against a long configured path, in both dialects. Signal: a fleet machine configuring an endpoint url with a path, which the sibling entry above on the loader's unanchored url check makes likelier than it reads.

- **The address screen is restated at eight sites outside the security model, and none carries the path-versus-address distinction the shipped refusal now needs (2026-09-05, from section 2 of the endpoint-dialect-key plan's security review, widened by that session's own sweep; confirmed at source).** The non-2xx refusal branch at `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-endpoint-lib.js:611` writes the configured URL's own pathname into log lines, records, reports and test assertions. The screen it narrows is claimed at `kit-endpoint-lib.js:32` ("lands in no comment, log line, record or test") and again at `:325` and inside `classifyThrow` at `:421`, which states the promise in the document's own terms, that the endpoint address reaches no log line, record or report, and which is the site the next author of the refusal branch reads, at `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js:6419`, at `sidecar/daemon.js:536` ("not printed, here or anywhere", the widest phrasing in the tree and sitting in the file that emits the line), at `sidecar/battery.js:160` and `:1530`, and on two surfaces that face outward: the printed banner at `sidecar/battery.js:1534` ("the endpoint address is never printed; its fingerprint is below") and `sidecar/batteries/README.md:431`, which ships in this public repository. Every one of them survives literally, because the address is the host and its port while a pathname is neither, and section 2 of that plan drew exactly that distinction in `docs/security-model.md` on both the memq and the daemon legs. What is left is that these are the sites the next author of a print statement in these files reads, two of them make the promise to an operator or to a stranger, and `sidecar/battery.js` wires the daemon's report dependency to its own stderr, so a dialect-mismatched or path-doubled config prints a pathname into the very report whose banner denies it. Coverage of that list, since it is a claim about a class: the predicate swept was a grep over `*.js`, `*.md` and `*.ps1` in the working tree for the phrasings that assert the address is not printed, excluding `docs/security-model.md` and `.kit/`; it found six of the eight sites above and did not find the `kit-endpoint-lib.js:32` header form or the `:421` restatement, both of which the reviews named instead, so the class of prose asserting this screen is not provably swept and a repair pass re-sweeps rather than working from this list alone. The tests that assert the screen (`test/kit-sidecar-daemon.test.js:2208` and `test/memq.test.js:26753` among them) compare against the host rather than the path, so they hold as written. Repair shape: one clause at each prose site naming the path as outside the screen and pointing at the security model's two paragraphs rather than restating them, which keeps the single-source discipline those sites already follow. Signal: the next change to any of these files that prints anything, or a reader citing one of them as authority for what a refusal discloses.

- **The architecture document credits a host-side generation-lane watchdog whose probe cannot answer against this fleet's server, so a reader counts a restart-on-wedge protection that is structurally inert here (2026-09-05, from the endpoint-dialect-key plan's finishing curation; confirmed at source, predating that plan).** `docs/architecture.md` describes, under Repo layout, a scheduled task that probes the generation lane every few minutes and restarts the service after two consecutive failed probes. `sidecar/ollama-watchdog.ps1:109` posts to `$BaseUrl/api/generate`, which is the Ollama dialect's own path and the path a llama.cpp server answers 404 on, and every machine of this fleet runs such a server. So the probe fails permanently rather than on a wedge, which is at once a protection the document credits and does not have, and a probe that would read a healthy server as one needing a restart. The endpoint-dialect-key plan named the watchdog in its Out of Scope list as host state the operator holds, and the operator-tier record `the-host-endpoint-speaks-only-the-openai-dialect` carries the endpoint's shape; what stays open is the watchdog's own path and the sentence that credits it. Repair shape: either teach the watchdog the dialect its host actually serves, reading the same `api` key the kit's own transport reads, or retire it and say so in the document. The choice is the operator's, the scheduled task being host state rather than repository state, which is why this entry does not pick one. Signal: the next plan that touches `sidecar/ollama-watchdog.ps1`, or an operator ruling on whether the watchdog stays.

- **The memory-system skill's degrade enumeration for the judged ranking carries no clause for a refusal, so the surface a session reads before running `memq find` cannot place the line this plan added (2026-09-05, from the endpoint-dialect-key plan's finishing curation; confirmed at source, deliberately out of that plan's scope).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md` states the judged block's degrades as one line for a dead or slow endpoint and silence for an absent config. A dialect mismatch is neither: the transport returns a refusal whose detail names the status, the path the request reached and the dialect it posted in (`plugins/claude-kit/scripts/kit-endpoint-lib.js:609-613`, printed at `memq.js:6439-6444`). The skill is the surface a session loads before it reaches the store, so a reader who meets that refusal line there has nothing to place it against, where `docs/architecture.md` now carries the full degrade set. The plan ruled the skill out of scope for a stated cost, that the clause would spend a size-ratchet cap raise for a sentence the architecture document already carries, and that cost is what makes this a backlog item rather than a defect the plan should have fixed. Repair shape: add one clause to the skill's degrade sentence naming the refusal, in the same edit as the next cap raise that file takes for another reason, so the raise is spent once rather than twice. Signal: the next plan that edits `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md`.

- **A queue-insert verb for the goal CLI (2026-09-10, from the review-loop-provenance plan's Out of Scope, routed at its close-out as found work serving another goal; confirmed at source).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal.js` has `arm`, `arm --append`, `clear` and `status`, and none inserts a plan at a position in an armed queue: that plan's own insertion at position 2 of the worker's queue was a direct edit of `.kit/goal-state.json` with a timestamped backup, recorded under its Operator Verification. Repair shape: an `insert <position> <plan path>` form on the CLI, all-or-nothing like `--append`, validating the path the same way and refusing a duplicate, so running a plan next never needs a hand edit of the state file. Such a verb writes the leash's queue, and only the operator's typed `/kit-goal` arms a leash, so it would take the arm's gate: it would run only where the operator typed `/kit-goal` naming the plan in that session, and a relay instruction could not drive it.

- **Dead inbound pointers to archived plans: 13 archived plans are still named by a live `docs/plans/` path (parked 2026-09-10, surfaced by the review-tier-decay finishing pass).** Curating-docs step 3 has an archival repoint the moved plan's own outbound links and says nothing about the references pointing at it, so every archival leaves its inbound pointers behind. Sweeping `docs/archive/*_v1.md` for live `plans/<basename>` references outside the archive returns 13 plans, cited from `docs/backlog.md`, two rationale ledgers, four still-active plan docs and `sidecar/batteries/README.md`. The sites split two ways and the split is the work: a navigational pointer is dead and should be repointed at `archive/`, while a provenance citation naming where a file sat at a past commit is append-only history and is correctly left alone. That per-site call is why this is parked rather than swept. The curating-docs gap itself is already in the kaizen inbox as its own note; this item is the backlog of sites that gap has already produced. Signal: the next archival, or a kaizen pass adopting the inbound-grep twin.

- **The hook canary behaviour-probes only the hooks its own probe tables name, so a hook can go inert and the canary reports nothing (2026-09-11, from the test audit's section 2 adjudication; the test records the gap itself and is kept).** `test/hook-canary.test.js` states at 726-736 that its behavioural probe covers the hooks its probe tables list, and nothing else. The consequence is concrete rather than theoretical: with `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal-lib.js` deleted, `session-start.js` becomes unloadable and `memory-session.js` and `memory-recognition-nudge.js` go silently inert, and the canary reports none of the three. The test is honest about its own scope, which is why it is kept rather than repaired, and it names the fix: one probe per unprobed hook, so the probe table stops being the boundary of what the canary can see. Routed here rather than fixed in the audit because the audit's Out of Scope bars product code, and the fix is product work on the canary rather than a verdict on a test.

- **`nudgeSubject` is not exported from the recognition nudge, so its three curated frame sentences can only be pinned as prose (2026-09-11, from the test audit's section 2 adjudication).** `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memory-recognition-nudge.js` defines `nudgeSubject` at :2047 and calls it at :2054, and `module.exports` at :2768 does not carry it. The effect is on what a test can assert: `test/memory-recognition-nudge.test.js:2095` has to pin the three frame sentences by their wording, because there is no exported function for a test to call with a boundary and compare the returned subject against. Exporting it would let the wording pin become a token pin, asserting that the prompt emission carries `nudgeSubject('UserPromptSubmit')` rather than `nudgeSubject('PreToolUse')`, which catches the wrong-branch defect with the sentences free to improve. One export plus one test edit. The audit kept 2095 as it stands and routed this here, because exporting a function is product code and the audit excludes it; the same shape holds for the wording pins the frontmatter-guard and memory-session verdicts keep on that ground, so a reader taking this item should check whether one export closes more than one pin.
- **Two rationale-ledger entries carry a controller-authored line under the `proposed:` label the ledger legend reserves for the judge's wording (2026-09-12, from the corpus rewrite plan's section 5 review, routed out at that section's close).** Under the operating-instructions ledger's heading for the output style (plugins/claude-kit/skills/operating-instructions/references/rationale-ledger.md), C004's fourth `proposed:` line and C007's second are the wording section 2 landed, written there by the controller as a record of the departure; the legend (line 5) defines `proposed:` as the target wording a judge proposed, and the plan reads several such lines as the judge's own proposals in order, so a controller-authored line misattributes the departure to the judge. The reason line is the sanctioned record and both entries' reasons already carry the landing, so the fix is to delete the two lines. That ledger is section 2's closed scope, which is why it is here rather than folded: take it in the same sweep as the docs citation item above, after the corpus rewrite closes.
- **`test/embedder-install.test.js`'s unusable-install fallback case warms a model from huggingface.co over the live network, so a rate-limit response from that host reds the whole gate (2026-09-12, from the corpus rewrite's section 21 close gate).** The case ran red in one whole-gate run on an HTTP 429 naming that host's model URL, and green when re-run alone eighteen seconds later on the same tree; the property under test (one bounded npm install repairs an unloadable package) does not need the model fetch to succeed, so a cached model fixture or a stubbed warm-up would make the case hermetic, and until one lands a 429 red in that file is read as the host's rather than the tree's, with the re-run alone as the check.
- **The memory-system skill's account of the sync script's silent exits names one of three states, and the two it leaves out are the ones that write no state file at all (2026-09-13, raised in the corpus rewrite follow-up's section 3 review and refused there as a new requirement, routed here by the KIT: Expert seat).** The skill's hand-path paragraph says a run that meets a live lock exits 0 with the state file untouched, which is true and is the least alarming of the three. `Get-SyncGateReason` (`plugins/claude-kit/doctor/sync-store.ps1:98-105`) also returns `git-missing` and `foreign`, and `Write-SyncGateState` (`:327-331`) deliberately writes nothing for those two, so the script exits 0 at `:394-398` leaving no verdict behind. A hand run against the wrong directory, or on a box with no git, therefore looks exactly like a run that deferred to a live lock, and the skill's stated check (compare the state file's timestamp against the run) tells a stale verdict from a fresh one and not one cause from another, so a run that found no git, or a store root it refused as foreign, reads as a run that deferred to a live lock, and in the `foreign` case there may be no state file at all. The same gap sits on the off-Windows path, where the doctor's memory-sync line is the stated precondition and cannot be read at all without PowerShell (`plugins/claude-kit/hooks/memory-session.js:741-744`, which returns early off Windows, and `syncFallbackText` at `:598-616`, which is the off-Windows pointer). Repair shape: one sentence in the hand-path paragraph naming the three no-state exits together, or a stop rule for a precondition that cannot be read. It was refused in section 3 because the paragraph's pre-existing sentence traces to no acceptance bullet and no Goal sentence of that plan. The operator ruled on 2026-09-14 that the off-Windows route keeps its base wording and that this gap is the recorded residual, so this entry is where the question waits rather than a sentence in the skill. A second residual rides with it, on the commit half rather than the push: off Windows that half is the doctor's `-Fix`, which from a tool shell asks for the operator's go-ahead, so on that platform the sync's commit does take a go-ahead the paragraph's own headline grant says the sync needs none of. The grant and the doctor's consent are different gates and the paragraph never says so, and closing that is the same reopening of the sync paragraph this entry already waits for. Signal: the prose pass over the memory-system skill, or any plan that reopens the sync paragraph.
- **A `Source:` line in `docs/harness-assumptions.md` carries a belief whose third clause no cited document states, at the base ref or at head (2026-09-16, from the prose pass's section 11 review round 2, routed out at that section's close).** The belief at `docs/harness-assumptions.md:168` says the harness's memory rewrite "sometimes quotes a multi-value scalar". It cited two lines of `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md`, and neither states that fact. The first states the rewrite's shape and its key variance and is still cited. The second, base line 118 and head line 134, is the hand-quoting bullet: it says a `tags: "a, b"` you type yourself matches neither tag, which is the author quoting rather than the harness quoting. So the clause was unsourced before this pass ran, and section 11 dropped the second citation rather than re-aiming it, on the disposition sections 1 and 2 took for the same shape. The clause itself is untouched and still unsourced. Repair shape: find what the clause rests on, a memory record or an observed rewrite, and cite that; or, where nothing carries it, cut the clause, since the belief's first two clauses stand on their own. This is here rather than folded because the prose pass re-aims citations and does not adjudicate the fact base's own beliefs, which is the bound sections 2 and 8 each reverted an edit on. Signal: any pass that reopens `docs/harness-assumptions.md`'s memory-system beliefs.
- **The probe corpus runner's reader timeout is a fixed constant, and the `full` shape of `seat-asked-to-push-the-memory-store` now sits past it, so that shape yields no reading at all (2026-09-16, from the prose pass's section 11 probe pair, routed out at that section's close).** `READER_TIMEOUT_MS` is `300000` at `tools/probe-corpus/run.mjs:70` and no flag raises it; `KNOWN_FLAGS` at `:74` carries `--before`, `--only`, `--shape`, `--touching`, `--claude`, `--home` and `--dry-run` and nothing else. That shape's reader completed at base ref `dec8c87` in 266.7 seconds, which is 33 seconds of margin, and exceeded the cap on three separate attempts at head, the third with no other run of this session beside it. The same moment's `doctrine-plus-role` shape exceeded it at head twice as well, while the `doctrine-plus-memory-system` shape completed twice at 295.1 and 123.6 seconds, a swing that says the wall clock moves a lot on this box. So a shape whose reader sits near the cap yields a reading or an error depending on load, and the exit code counts mismatches only, so a run reports exit 0 while telling you nothing about the pairs that errored. This matters past one section: the pair is the plan's evidence that behavior did not move, and a shape that cannot be read supplies none. Repair shapes, in rough order of cost: make the timeout a flag so a caller can buy the margin; or split the seven-file `full` shape, which is the largest prompt in the corpus; or have the runner's exit code account for errored pairs so a caller cannot read exit 0 as a clean corpus. This is here rather than folded because `tools/probe-corpus/run.mjs` sits outside section 11's Files in scope and the change is the runner's contract rather than this pass's prose. Signal: the next section whose probe pair names a large shape, or any plan that reopens the probe runner.

- **The repository carries no package lockfile, so `npm audit` cannot run against it at all (2026-09-18, from the memory database plan’s section 3 security review, routed out at that round’s adjudication).** The security lens reported `ENOLOCK` when it tried the dependency check over this tree. The kit’s own runtime code uses Node built-ins rather than third-party packages, so the check may well have nothing to find, and that is the point: nobody has established it either way, and the lens that would establish it cannot run. This is routed here rather than fixed in section 3 because the section adds no third-party dependency, so nothing in its own delta is at stake, and committing a lockfile is a repository-wide decision rather than a section’s. Repair shape: commit a `package-lock.json` so the check runs, or record in `docs/security-model.md` that the kit has no third-party runtime dependencies to audit, which turns an unrun check into a stated property. Signal: any plan that adds a runtime dependency, or the next security pass over the whole repository.

- **`acquireLock` never breaks a lock whose modification time sits ahead of the clock, so one such lock file wedges every later publish in silence (2026-09-18, from the memory database plan's section 3 round 4 review, routed out at that round's adjudication).** The staleness comparison at `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js:2404` breaks a lock once it is older than its interval. A lock file whose modification time is in the future is never older than anything, so it is never broken, and clock skew or a copy that preserved a future timestamp is enough to produce one. What that costs the publisher is specific: the spool drain reads a held lock as contention, which is the ordinary case and correctly exits zero, so every publish from then on drains nothing, reports a drained count of zero and exits clean, while the spool grows without bound. The section that found this names the silent loss of a stamp between spool and database as its own expensive failure, which is why the shape is worth writing down. It is routed here rather than fixed in that section because the lock is memq's own and pre-existing, and because that section's governing amendment removes lock-staleness arithmetic from the drain by name, so a repair inside the drain is the one shape the plan forbids. Repair shape: have `acquireLock` treat a modification time in the future as stale rather than as fresh, which is one comparison and costs nothing on a healthy clock. Signal: any plan that reopens memq's lock, or a report of a spool that stopped draining. The publisher no longer sits on this lock: the same plan's section 3 replaced the file spool with a SQLite queue whose locking is SQLite's own, and `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memory-database.js` calls `acquireLock` nowhere, so the cost paragraph above describes a drain that no longer exists. The defect in `acquireLock` itself stands for the paths that still take it: `anchor`, `triggers`, `decay-prune` and the shared-tier authoring and delete verbs.

- **The rule that a shared project record is visible but not this publisher's to write is spelled separately in each procedure that needs it (2026-09-18, from the memory database plan's section 3 round 9 review, routed out at that round's adjudication).** `mem.udf_VisibleRecords` describes itself as the one statement of what a publisher may see, and it answers exactly that. Whether a visible record is also this caller's to write is a second question, and two procedures now answer it with the same predicate written out by hand: `045-usp_ListRecords.sql` keeps another sandbox's project rows out of a publisher's inventory, and `050-usp_UpsertEmbeddings.sql` refuses a vector against one. The two are correct today and were checked against each other. The cost is the next procedure: any publisher-writable procedure that takes a caller-supplied record id has to know the rule exists before it can apply it, and the one that does not will omit it silently, which is the shape the embedding writer already failed in once. It is routed here rather than fixed in section 3 because the repair is a new inline function or a computed column on a shipped one, which is a schema change rather than a close-pass fix, and because the documents that describe the visibility model belong to section 5. Repair shape: add the owning-sandbox test as a second inline table-valued function, or as a flag column on `udf_VisibleRecords`, and call it from both procedures so the rule has one home. Signal: the next procedure that takes a record id from a caller, and section 5's pass over the visibility model in `docs/security-model.md`.
- **Finishing-work's whole fix path sits inside step 2's list item, so the pass's correctness-tier disposition reads as a clause of the advisory reviews (2026-09-20, raised by section 2's implementer on the reviewer re-ranking plan and routed out at that section's close).** Six paragraphs at `plugins/claude-kit/skills/finishing-work/SKILL.md:136-146` are three-space-indented continuations of the `2.` item, and they govern the pass's fix path whole: the owed-Major fix-or-present route, the terminal condition, the round unit, the provenance read and the capture path. They already governed findings from steps 3 and 4 before this plan touched anything, so the mismatch is pre-existing. What the plan changed is the heading above them, from `2. **Security review.**` to `2. **Advisory reviews.**`, which makes the mismatch legible: a reader scanning headings now finds the correctness tier's disposition filed under the advisory tier, the exact confusion the plan's tier-keyed-on-the-lens rule exists to prevent. Repair shapes, cheaper first. De-indent the six paragraphs to the top level between steps 2 and 3, owned by neither, which changes no step number and so touches none of the 107 `finishing-work step <n>` cross-references under `plugins/`, `test/` and `README.md` nor the 8 heading literals `test/` slices on. Or promote them to a step of their own, which renumbers steps 3 through 9 and does touch all 115 sites. The first shape is the one the evidence supports; it was not taken in section 2 because that section's acceptance names the heading, the round unit and the waiver, and says nothing about the file's step boundaries, so the edit would have been unreviewed scope. Signal: any plan that reopens finishing-work's step structure, or the corpus read reaching this skill.
- **A rationale-ledger entry's `passage:` line can stop quoting its source with nothing reading it, and six entries across the repository are adrift today (2026-09-20, from section 2's review rounds on the reviewer re-ranking plan, routed out at that section's close).** The ledger preamble requires a keep's `passage:` line to carry the source text verbatim, which is what makes a keep's re-read mechanical. Nothing read that rule until section 2 added a pin, and the class had already produced four drifts inside two of that section's own fix rounds. Measured over all 23 tracked ledgers: 69 `passage:` lines, 6 adrift. Section 2's pin covers the finishing-work ledger, and section 6 re-syncs the one adrift entry this plan caused, `executing-work` T188. The five that remain were already adrift at this branch's base commit `19e626ba`: `brainstorming` C180 and C181, `executing-work` T054 and C072, and `operating-instructions` C076. Repair shape: disposition those five, then add every ledger to the pin's `PASSAGE_PINNED_LEDGERS` list in `test/doctrine-parity.test.js`. The disposition is the decision, not the code: the preamble's closing sentence says the three format rules bind entries written after they landed and are not backfilled, so re-syncing a pre-rule entry's quote is a choice about what that exemption means rather than a repair the rule already orders. This is here rather than folded because four of the five sit in files outside this plan's scope and the fifth needs that call. One half of the class stays uncoverable either way: a `key:` line is a paraphrase by design, so no verbatim reader catches key drift, and section 2 fixed two instances of that half by hand. Signal: any plan that edits a skill body whose ledger carries `passage:` entries.
- **Two probe situations enumerate the review lenses and now under-count them, so a cold reader meets a scene naming one lens where the loop names two (2026-09-20, from section 2's probe-pair check on the reviewer re-ranking plan, routed out at that section's close).** `test/probes/branch-and-pr-pull-request-at-finishing.md:27` says the finishing pass ran "the security review", where this plan retitles finishing-work's step 2 to the advisory reviews. `test/probes/review-round-returning-only-claim-majors.md:26` recites the per-section security trigger list and states that the security reviewer was not dispatched, with no performance lens beside it, though that lens gains a trigger list of its own in section 1. Neither sentence is false: the security lens does still run at finishing, and that section's files do fail the security trigger. Both are scene-setting rather than the answer under test, and each moment's ruled answer turns on a passage this plan does not touch, the pull-request step for the first and the claim-exit terminal condition for the second. So no recorded ruling is invalidated and nothing untrue ships. Repair shape: refresh both situations to name the advisory tier and the performance trigger, then re-run each moment's pair to confirm the ruling survives the new scene. That re-run is the cost, about forty cents a pair, which is why this is a call rather than a sweep. This is here rather than folded because `test/probes/` sits outside section 2's Files in scope and in a different directory from anything it changed, and rather than an appended section because the plan's `## Out of Scope` already keeps probe fixtures out of this effort as a class. Signal: any plan that edits a probe situation, or the next effort that reopens the review roster.
- **The sixth retire class's canonical reshape example is falsified by its own first application, and the class carries no branch for the case (2026-09-20, section 3 of `claude-kit_test-requirement-axis_spec_v1`).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/testing-discipline/SKILL.md` says a pin on a choice sitting beside a requirement "is reshaped to pin the requirement alone", and names "the direction rather than the sentence" as how. Section 3 was that rule's first application and proved that exact reshape impossible for the two standing-watch paragraphs: both state both directions legitimately, only the subject separates the rule from its opposite, and every subject binder over free prose is a vocabulary pin under another name. A consult ruled it and the operator ruled the direction prose-enforced, so the retire is authorized. What the owner lacks is a branch for "the requirement has no stable form to reshape onto", under which a pin retires and the requirement is recorded as prose-enforced. Meanwhile the test comment at `test/doctrine-parity.test.js` and `standing-watch/references/rationale-ledger.md` C019, C044 and C045 each cite the sixth class as sanctioning what was done, and strictly it does not; `docs/architecture.md` describes the direction as prose-enforced and names no class. Amending the owner is a decision rather than a cleanup: it reaches a merged section's text and changes what every future retire is judged against. The operator's call on whether to add the branch, and on whether the marker-pair route the consult recommended and he declined becomes the sanctioned alternative.

- **`test/kit-goal-stop.test.js:1550`'s comment overstates the reach of the fail-open path it guards, and this plan was barred from fixing it (2026-09-20, goal read on `claude-kit_test-requirement-axis_spec_v1`).** The comment says an unparseable `KIT_GOAL_STOP_RETRY_MS` throwing would become "a silent allow on every leashed stop". Two paths never reach it, both confirmed against the code: an unusable plan has the throw caught at the call site (`kit-goal-stop.js:819`) and the stop held, and a plan whose head reads Complete returns at `kit-goal-stop.js:700` before the retry schedule is read. The accurate reach is a bound session with a readable, unfinished plan. Section 3 narrowed the sentence, and the finishing goal read refused that edit on the plan's own `## Out of Scope` entry, which names this test among four the council ruled requirements and says "They stay as written". The edit was reverted and the file is byte-identical to the base ref. So the inaccuracy stands by an exclusion rather than by a judgment that it is acceptable. It is a Minor by the finishing security lens's own rating and touches no assertion, only the explanatory comment. Whoever takes it should note that the same sentence, corrected, already ships in the hook's own comment at `kit-goal-stop.js:263`, so the test comment is the one surface left carrying the wider claim.

- **The changeset-is-data and tool-printed-claims rules reach one sighted code lens of four, and the two lenses whose own subject is a build or a spawn are not it (2026-09-20, from the reviewer-reranking plan's section 4 close pass).** The adversarial and performance charters each carry the changeset-is-data paragraph; the blind and security charters carry none. The tool-printed-claims rule, which has a reviewer settle a claim about what a tool prints against the tool rather than against documents, sits in the adversarial charter alone. So the security lens, whose findings are about spawns and grants, and the performance lens, whose findings are about what a build or a process costs, are the two most likely to want to run something and the two with the least prose telling them how. Nothing mechanical is behind any of it: the read-only guard denies write-shaped commands and command text it cannot resolve, and a bare `node <script>` invocation is outside its denylist. `docs/security-model.md` states the coverage gap as a property of the design rather than a defect, which is why this is a decision rather than a fix. Repair shape: decide whether the two paragraphs belong in every sighted charter or in one place all four point at, which is the same one-owner question the kit answers elsewhere by pointing. Signal: a reviewer running a command whose effects it could not establish, or a finding that rests on a document's claim about a tool where the tool was in reach.
- **Four test files read CRLF-checked-out sources and match bare-newline shapes with no normalisation, the same class as the memq defect just fixed (2026-09-20).** `test/kit-compact-gate.test.js` (11 shapes), `test/compact-deferral-nudge.test.js` (2), `test/hook-canary.test.js` (1) and `test/kit-output-channel.test.js` (1) each read a shipped source and match a structural shape anchored on a bare `\n`, and none of them normalises line endings at the read. Every source they read is CRLF on a Windows checkout, confirmed for `memq.js`, `kit-goal-stop.js` and `compact-deferral-nudge.js`. All four pass today, which is the reason to look rather than the reason not to: the same defect in `test/memq.test.js` took two forms, one loud and one silent. The loud one matched nothing and reported a function declared at column zero as missing. The silent one used `indexOf('\n}\n')`, got -1, and `slice(start, -1)` then handed the assertions 886,481 characters of the file instead of a 933-character function body, so the test passed while constraining nothing. A passing test in this class is therefore unverified rather than fine, and telling benign from silent means checking each of the fifteen shapes against the true span. Not done in the memq fix, which stayed scoped to the file that was failing plus the sibling defect found inside it. Whoever takes it should use the same control: remove an asserted token from the function body only, leaving it present elsewhere in the file, and require the test to red.

- **An archived plan carries the `## Standing Brief Amendments` heading twice, and the second block's five bullets exist on no other branch (2026-09-21, found by the reviewer re-ranking plan's finishing pass while it checked its base ref).** `docs/archive/claude-kit_test-requirement-axis_spec_v1.md` carries that heading at line 16 and again at line 52. The first block is byte-identical to the copy `main` holds. The second holds five amendment bullets that `main`'s copy of the same plan carries nowhere, confirmed by three distinctive strings each returning one hit here and zero there. The cause is this branch's merge `f3bdbc5a`, which reconciled two parallel landings of that plan, `1d060d51` here and `81c554e3` through `a9ad15ad` on main, and kept both sides' blocks. Nothing parses wrongly: both sit above `## Sections of Work`, so every bounded block the plan-doc machine contract defines is intact, which is why this is a record defect rather than a gate failure. It is left rather than fixed because it is another effort's archived record, serving a goal other than the reviewer re-ranking plan's, and editing an archived plan inside an unrelated pull request is the antipattern `curating-docs` names. The call it needs: whether those five bullets are amendments that plan genuinely adopted, in which case they fold under the first heading, or text its own five review rounds removed on main, in which case the second block goes.
- **The Jev client accepts an endpoint carrying a query, a fragment or userinfo, and the call then fails with a reason that points at the network instead of the config file (2026-09-21, from the Jev coverage check plan's section 1 review).** `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/jev-client.js` builds the request URL by appending `/v1/systemone` to the configured endpoint as text. So `https://host?x=1` posts to `/` with the path inside the query, a `#` truncates the path, and `https://user:pw@host` makes `fetch` throw on the credential. An endpoint written with its own path, such as `https://host/v1/systemone`, posts to `/v1/systemone/v1/systemone`. The call reads `refused` or `unreachable` rather than `config unusable`. The key still reaches only the validated host, which the section's security review confirmed, and the run still reads not checked, never a false pass. It is left because section 1's acceptance closes the `invalid` list at three shapes, and a refusal for these is a guard no clause of that plan names. Repair shape: refuse a path other than `/`, or a non-empty `url.search`, `url.hash`, `url.username` or `url.password`, in `loadJevConfig` as `invalid`, and repoint the test that pins userinfo as `unreachable`. The second Jev plan, which adds the credential screen at the client's boundary, is the natural home. Signal: an operator reading `refused HTTP 404` or `unreachable` against a vendor that is up.
- **The `ASK:` mark the doctrine now names has no row in the ownership map and no rationale-ledger entry (2026-09-22, from the ask-mark plan's section 1 review, raised by both lenses).** The doctrine now carries the doctrine bullet **On the relay thread, a reply that waits on me carries an `ASK:` line.**, telling a session to mark a relay reply whose session cannot take its next step until the operator answers or acts, in `plugins/claude-kit/skills/operating-instructions/SKILL.md` and its `home/claude-kit-doctrine.md` mirror. Two reference files under `plugins/claude-kit/skills/operating-instructions/references/` did not follow. `ownership-map.md` gains no row for the moment, and its nearest row, the prose register, names "Craft and communication" and "Write every decision ask to the client-briefing register" as the owning bullets, so a reader following the map lands on two bullets that do not carry the rule. `rationale-ledger.md` gains no entry, so the reason for the mark, its placement outside the output style's pinned register core, and its precedence under a `BLOCKED:` or `WAITING:` lead live only in the plan doc. Nothing is gated: the map pin in `test/doctrine-parity.test.js` checks only that every shipped skill owns some row, so the section's acceptance is unaffected. It is left because both files sit outside the section's three files in scope and outside the directory a fold would allow, and this plan's Goal is that a session marks its reply rather than that the map stays whole. Repair shape: add the new bullet's lead to the map's prose-register row, or give the moment its own row, and append a ledger entry under the next free id letter. Both files carry size caps that the added words move.
- **The `executing-work` skill states the shape of a `BLOCKED:` and a `WAITING:` message and does not mention the `ASK:` line the doctrine now puts under that lead (2026-09-22, from the ask-mark plan's section 1, round 2).** `plugins/claude-kit/skills/executing-work/SKILL.md` says the stop message's very first characters are `BLOCKED:` and that "the body under the prefix is a decision brief in the doctrine's client-briefing register", and it states the `WAITING:` lead the same way. The doctrine bullet **On the relay thread, a reply that waits on me carries an `ASK:` line.** now adds that where such a lead already opens the message, the `ASK:` line comes directly below it and restates the ask. Nothing contradicts: the doctrine outranks a skill under the which-text-governs rule, so a session reading both obeys the doctrine, and the mark is what the Discord broker's inbox matcher reads. The gap is a cross-reference rather than an absence: `home/CLAUDE.md` imports the doctrine mirror into every session on this machine, so the rule is in context, but a session composing a stop message from the skill's own shape paragraph meets nothing there that prompts it to add the line. It is left because the section's own acceptance closes its changed files at the three it names, and this skill is not among them. Repair shape: one clause in each of the skill's two shape paragraphs pointing at that doctrine bullet for the mark. The skill carries a size cap that the added words move.
- **A session-composed `ASK:` line reaches the Fleet: Inbox card with no board-line bar reachable from the rule that tells a session to write it (2026-09-22, from the ask-mark plan's section 1, round 4; the design stop refused the in-plan fix on a form ground and named this routing).** `docs/security-model.md` states two bars that hold "whatever the readership": no board line carries an absolute path, because a path typically embeds the OS username, and the operator's words never appear on one. Those bars are stated over the coordinator board, and the `BLOCKED:` first line carries the cap through the peer-sessions Worker seat. The doctrine's new `ASK:` bullet creates a second path for session-composed text onto a fleet surface: the broker gives a marked item an excerpt of up to 200 code points and draws it on the `Fleet: Inbox` card, which `broker/inbox/binding.ts` shows is a thread of its own rather than the session's own relay thread. So `ASK: approve the refresh at C:/Users/<name>/...` satisfies the doctrine today. It is left because the scope adjudicator refused the in-plan fix: the Goal sentence the fix would serve describes what the broker does with the line and asks nothing of its content, so a content bar is a part no Goal sentence, Intent clause or acceptance bullet names, and within the form those clauses do ask for there is no guard sentence to add. Repair shape: the bar belongs to the surface that owns it, so state in `docs/security-model.md` whether the two bars reach a session-composed `ASK:` line, and if they do, carry the cap onto that line from whichever surface the ownership map gives the mark. Signal: an inbox card excerpt carrying an absolute path or a quotation of the operator.
- **Operator verification: the registry audit exits 0 or 1 on a real coordinator directory (2026-09-23, from the guards and registry audit plan's `## Operator Verification`).** After the pull request merges and the plugin update is installed, run `node <plugin root>/hooks/kit-registry-stamp.js audit` on a machine whose coordinator directory exists. Exit 0 (clean) or 1 (findings) is the pass. Exit 2 means the audit refused to scan a directory it should read, and reopens the work as a new round. The plan is `archive/claude-kit_guards-and-registry-audit_spec_v1.md`.

- **Operator verification: the `ASK:` line on a live Discord reply (2026-09-22, from the ask-mark plan's `## Operator Verification`).** After the kit update carrying the ask-mark bullet is installed, read the next Discord reply from an interactive session that waits on you. An `ASK:` line at its top, drawn with its excerpt on the `Fleet: Inbox` card, confirms the rule reached the session. Its absence on a session started after the install reopens the plan as a new round.
- **The relay's own server instructions state a broader `ASK:` trigger than the doctrine (2026-09-22, from the ask-mark plan's finishing docs curation).** The `plugin:relay:channel-relay` server instructions a session loads, shipped from `relay/protocol.ts` in the discord-channels repository, say a reply that hands the operator a decision, a question or an act only they can perform opens with `ASK:`, with no exemption for a reply that only reports. The doctrine bullet marks only a reply that waits on the operator. A session holding both texts gets two triggers, and the broader one puts report-only replies on the inbox card where they never clear. The text is the discord-channels repository's, so the repair belongs to that repository; the operator ruled on 2026-09-22 to keep the doctrine's narrower trigger, and no discord-channels plan yet narrows the relay text to match. Signal: a `Fleet: Inbox` item from a close-out status that asks nothing.

- **The fleet-block judge shares its 1,500 ms edge with the shortlist query, so a healthy but slow host may leave it no time to answer (2026-09-23, from the Jev recollection judge plan's section 2 performance review, deferred at round 1).** The judge's edge counts from the block's start, as the plan's Approach sets it ("1,500 ms elapsed of the block's 2,000"). With a Jev config, the shortlist query embeds the first 4,000 characters of the composed situation, where the unjudged block embedded about a hundred, and the judge request that follows carries the situation whole. So on a host where the embedding and `usp_Search` together take most of a second, the judge is left a few hundred milliseconds and the block prints its stand-down line on the common path. That latency is inferred and not measured. The signal that reopens this is the stand-down rate on the operator's first judged sessions. No counter records a stand-down: it is one sentence in the session's opening context, so the rate is read by hand from those sessions' opening blocks. A block that stands down on `timeout` in most sessions means the edge needs its own clock, or stage 1 needs a shorter query still. `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/jev-judge.js` holds the edge and `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js` `fleetJudgedBlock` starts its clock.

- **Port the judgment sidecar's recognition question to the shared store and the Jev judge, deferred until the judged fleet block has a month of outcome rows (2026-09-23, from the Jev recollection judge plan).** `sidecar/daemon.js` still asks the local host model whether a project's memory index holds a record bearing on an observed call. The handoff brief proposed answering that from the shared memory database's search and the Jev judge instead, either at the daemon or on the PreToolUse and PostToolUse seams. The operator deferred that port on 2026-09-20 rather than refusing it, and the plan's `## Out of Scope` records the deferral. The reopening condition is a month of `kit.jev.pointer` outcome rows plus a measured false-pointer rate at the second floor, 0.75, read with `memq jev-calibration`. The month is the handoff brief's suggestion and the rate is the plan author's default: the operator ruled the deferral and set neither number, so the rate that would justify the port is still the operator's to name. Moving the sidecar's judgment duty is not part of this item, since the battery refused it outright. Signal: `memq jev-calibration` printing hit rates rather than its refusal line, a month after the first judged sessions.
- **Operator check: the judged fleet block's session id holds across a real session start (2026-09-23, operator-pending from the Jev recollection judge plan).** The plan's belief that a hook payload's `session_id` and the `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` of that session's tool shells name one session was checked from a tool shell, and its hook-driven half runs only once an installed kit carries the judged block. In the first session started after that install on a machine with `~/.claude/kit-jev.json`, run `memq get` on a record the session-start fleet block showed and confirm it prints no "was not keyed" line. A "was not keyed" line there falsifies the belief in `docs/harness-assumptions.md` and reopens the work as a new plan.
- **Embed the judged block's search query in chunk-sized pieces rather than as one head (2026-09-23, from the operator's review of the judge's situation caps).** The fleet block's search embeds only the first 4,000 characters of the composed situation (`QUERY_TEXT_CAP`), because the embedding server refuses an input past its 2,048-token width and one vector over a long text averages it into a vaguer query. Records are already embedded per chunk of 2,048 to 6,144 characters (`CHUNK_MIN_CHARS` and `CHUNK_MAX_CHARS` in `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memory-database.js`), so the query side is the half still embedded whole. Splitting the situation into chunk-sized pieces and taking each candidate's best match across them would let the Intent and next section steer the shortlist too. Decide on evidence: read the calibration hit rate by band (`memq jev-calibration`) once the judged block has a month of outcome rows, and act only if shown records are missing ones the operator opens.
- **The memory sync's object-id guards fail open if their shared pattern ever reads empty (2026-09-08, from the coordinator-sync-machine-scope plan's section 1 round-5 blind review; confirmed by probe, routed rather than fixed because the repair touches five git-argument sites and would owe a further review round).** That section replaced five self-contained regex literals with one `$script:MemorySyncObjectIdPattern` in `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1`, read in `Get-MemorySyncInboundForeignPaths` and `Install-MemorySyncRepo` there and in `Get-MemorySyncInboundOwnLines` in `doctor.ps1` and the inbound read in `sync-store.ps1` through dot-sourcing. PowerShell matches against an unset variable as against an empty pattern and returns a match, so `-cnotmatch $null` is False: were the constant ever renamed, moved, or read from a scope the dot-source does not reach, all five guards would admit any string, including git error text, into a git revision position, silently and with no test able to see it. Nothing reaches that state today, which is why it is Minor. Repair shape: read the pattern through a function that throws when it is not set, or assert it non-empty once at load, with a case that unsets it and watches a guard refuse rather than admit. Signal: any move of that constant, or a sixth consumer being added.
- **The memory sync loses its index restore when git prints a warning after the tree id (2026-09-08, from the coordinator-sync-machine-scope plan's section 1 round-5 blind review; routed rather than fixed because the repair changes a value that reaches a git argument position and would owe a further review round).** `Install-MemorySyncRepo` saves the pre-add index as a tree so any refusal after the add can put it back, and it reads that tree id as the last line of `git write-tree`'s output. `Invoke-MemorySyncGit` merges stdout and stderr with `2>&1`, and that merge is not chronologically ordered between the two native pipes, so a warning arriving after the tree id becomes the last line, fails the object-id match, and leaves the saved tree empty. The refusal then reports honestly that the staging could not be restored and leaves the add's staging in the index, which is fail-closed and is the state the restore closure exists to avoid, on exactly the noisy box the closure was written for. Repair shape: select the unique line matching the object-id pattern from the whole output and accept it only when exactly one line matches, rather than reading a fixed index; the same reading would serve the merge-base and upstream-sha sites. Signal: a store reporting that staged paths could not be restored on a box whose git prints warnings.
