- **The doctor's auto-compaction-window WARN collapses its two report lines into one (2026-09-08, from the coordinator-sync-machine-scope plan's section 1 round-3 sweep; confirmed by reading the literal, outside that plan's scope).** `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/doctor.ps1:2408`, in the `Auto-compaction window` branch that fires when `autoCompactWindow` holds an unusable value, builds its first array element by concatenation and does not parenthesize it: `"autoCompactWindow is set to '" + (Get-SanitizedLine $configuredWindowRaw) + "', ...",` followed by the `"Set it by hand to ..."` remedy line. In PowerShell `,` binds tighter than `+`, so the comma-separated pair becomes the right operand of the concatenation and the two notes reach `Report` as a single string, which the report then truncates: the operator is told the value is unusable and never told what to set it to. Repair shape: wrap the first element in its own parentheses, the same one-line fix applied twice inside that plan's section 1 (`install-memory-sync.ps1` at the staged-read failure and at the outbound-foreign-write notes). Not fixed there because it is a different doctor section with its own tests and outside that section's file scope. Signal: any edit to the auto-compaction-window branch, or a sweep for `+` -built array elements ending in a comma across `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/`.
- **The coordinator machine axis compares a path segment that NTFS can spell more than one way (2026-09-08, from the coordinator-sync-machine-scope plan's section 1 security review; downgraded from Major to Minor on probe evidence and routed rather than fixed).** `Test-MemorySyncCoordinatorPathIsOwn` in `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1` decides ownership by comparing a path's second segment against the hostname under a .NET case-insensitive comparison, while the filesystem the rebase writes into resolves that segment through NTFS, which holds a second spelling for the same directory: the 8.3 short name. `dir /x` over `C:\Users\LocalAdmin\.claude\coordinator` lists `SCOTT-~1` beside `SCOTT-CLAUDE` and `NEO-CL~1` beside `NEO-CLAUDE`, while a fresh directory on `D:` gets no alias, so the aliasing is per-volume and observed directly rather than read from policy (`fsutil 8dot3name query` is denied without elevation). The review rated this Major on the theory that an upstream commit carrying `coordinator/SCOTT-~1/board.md` clears the ownership check and then lands on this machine's real board at checkout. Three probes on the `C:` volume say it does not: git resolves the alias in its own working-tree reads and refuses every write that would clobber through it, aborting the merge (`error: The following untracked working tree files would be overwritten by merge`) or the checkout (`fatal: cannot create directory ... Directory not empty`), with the own board's content intact in all three variants, tracked and untracked alike. The residual is an availability defect, a store that stops rebasing, rather than a peer write landing on the board. Not fixed in that plan because the repair widens `Test-MemorySyncCoordinatorPathIsOwn`, whose comparison rule that plan's decision 3 defines precisely, on evidence that no longer supports the severity that motivated it. Repair shape if taken: refuse any coordinator machine segment outside a hostname character class (`^[A-Za-z0-9.-]+$`) as unclassifiable on both sides, which also closes the sibling case the review names, where NTFS upcases a character into a hostname letter and .NET does not equate them (U+0131 against a hostname holding `I`, U+017F against one holding `S`). One constraint on that repair, so it is not taken blind: the class as written refuses every non-ASCII hostname, and a box named outside ASCII is unverified end to end, since the funnel decodes git's output in the console code page under Windows PowerShell 5.1, though the ownership comparison is a .NET case-insensitive one and the filesystem stores the name whatever alphabet it is written in. Such a box would read its own directory as foreign on both halves of the channel by rule rather than by decode, so the class needs widening to the alphabets a hostname may actually use before it is adopted. Signal: a store gating on `inbound-foreign-write` naming a path whose machine segment nobody recognizes.
- **Two machines whose hostnames differ only by case would wedge each other's store sync permanently (2026-09-08, from the coordinator-sync-machine-scope plan's section 2 blind review; confirmed by reading the predicate, routed rather than fixed because the repair changes the comparison rule that plan's decision 3 defines and would owe a further review round).** `Test-MemorySyncCoordinatorPathIsOwn` in `plugins/claude-kit/doctor/install-memory-sync.ps1` ends with `return ($segments[1] -ieq $Machine)`, a case-insensitive comparison that decision 3 chose deliberately, because the filesystem the rebase writes into holds one directory per name whatever its spelling. The fold is right for a case variant of this machine's own name and wrong for a peer whose real name differs from this one only by case: machine `BOX` reads `coordinator/box/` as its own directory, so every legitimate upstream write machine `box` makes to its own directory refuses here with `inbound-foreign-write`, and machine `box` refuses `BOX`'s writes the same way. Both stores then stop rebasing for good, each waiting on an operator repair of a remote that is not actually wrong. No such pair exists on this fleet, whose two machines are `SCOTT-CLAUDE` and `NEO-CLAUDE`, which is why it is Minor rather than live. Repair shape: refuse the pairing at its source rather than widening the predicate, by treating two coordinator directories whose names fold to the same string as a configuration fault the doctor names, since no comparison rule can serve both readings at once; the fold cannot distinguish this machine's own variant spelling from a genuinely distinct peer, so the fix belongs upstream of the comparison. Signal: a third machine joining the store, or a store gating on `inbound-foreign-write` naming a peer's own directory.
- **Remove the leash's claim routes once no goal state armed before the arm gate remains unbound (2026-09-24, from `docs/plans/claude-kit_kit-goal-interactive-only_spec_v1.md`).** `armingSessionClaims` and the typed-command claim in `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-goal-stop.js` and `plugins/claude-kit/hooks/kit-compact-gate.js` bind an unbound goal to a session at its first stop or compaction offer. Since the arm refuses unless the calling session's transcript shows the operator typed `/kit-goal`, an arm that passes ordinarily reports bound, so what can still reach these routes is a goal state recorded before that gate or one an arm left unbound where `armGoal`'s own path screen refused the transcript path the gate had read, a home spelled as a network share or a path over 512 characters. Signal: `/kit-goal status` and the doctor's leash report show no unbound goal on any machine's projects. Then remove both claim routes and `sessionHoldsLeash`'s claim branch, with their tests.
- **Serialize publishes on one machine, so a `memq forget` spawn cannot run beside another publish (2026-09-25, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_persona-memory-port_spec_v1.md` section 5).** Every confirmed `memq forget` spawns a detached `memq db-sync`, and it deliberately skips the session-start hook's attempt marker, since that marker's interval would suppress the spawn the verb's line reports. `cmdDbSync` and `publish()` in `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memory-database.js` take no local lock. So several removals in a row start several whole-store publishes. Each one queues on the host's `mem.Publish` app lock for up to 30 s per call, and each one embeds the same unembedded rows again. There is also a stale-walk case. A publish that walked before the unlink can upsert the record after a newer publish has named it removed, and the upsert's change branch clears `DeletedDt`. The row then stands until the next publish, which names it removed again, while forget's line said it retires at the spawned one. One store-root lock taken around `publish()` answers both. A second run would wait locally for the first rather than race it call by call on the host, and every run would walk after the previous run's unlinks. Signal: a caller that removes records in bursts, such as the persona plugin's distiller retiring several at once, or a host query log showing overlapping publishes from one sandbox.
- **Close the comprehension gaps two blind reads found in the memory store's documents (2026-09-25, from `docs/archive/claude-kit_persona-memory-port_spec_v1.md` section 6).** An operator persona and a kit-session persona read `plugins/claude-kit/skills/memory-system/SKILL.md`, `plugins/claude-kit/db/README.md`, `docs/fleet-integration.md`, `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/security-model.md` blind. Each gap below predates that plan and none was in its scope. In the skill: `find`'s model-judged block and the Jev judge never say which config turns on which; `NEIGHBOUR_FLOOR`, `FLEET_NEIGHBOUR_FLOOR`, `PAIRS_SHOWN` and `NEIGHBOUR_TIMEOUT_MS` carry no values, so the pre-write overlap read cannot be performed from an installed plugin; the `MEMORY.md` contents rule omits the `Project-Type:` line the type-tier section admits; `KIT_EMBEDDER_ROOT` is never defined; `recall`'s row says it writes nothing and then lists its writes; `jev-calibration`'s default window is unstated; which stream carries `recall`'s network-share stand-down is unstated. In the db README: adding a sandbox meets the logins-file refusal with no reconciliation step; the fixed `SQLCMD.EXE` path has no override or remedy; the embedding server the config names has no setup; the `embedding.url` key reads flat where the client wants a nested object, with no example config; the host probe's invocation and `timeoutMs`'s "boundary call" are undefined. In the contract and security documents: both name `find` as a pending-tier reader on the vector that withholds it; the `memq-grant` paragraph in `docs/security-model.md` states seventeen shapes in three groupings a reader cannot reconcile, which one table would fix. Fix shape: one pass per document under the writing-skills skill, each gap answered from the code. Signal: a session or the operator stalls on one of these, or the next edit to any of the five documents.
- **Measure the thirty-candidate composition under a shared-plus-own cut before adding that scope mode (2026-09-25, parked by `docs/archive/claude-kit_persona-memory-port_spec_v1.md`).** `mem.usp_Search` now narrows its candidate population to one project segment through `@p_Segment`, and `memq judged` is its one caller. A second mode, the caller's own segment plus every shared row, was refused in that plan because no caller asked for it. Whether `memq recall`'s fleet block wants it turns on how many of the thirty candidates a shared-plus-own cut would give the working project against today's unscoped fill. Measure that over several real situations on the host, per project, before writing the mode. Signal: the operator answers the plan's open question on a `recall` default, or a session finds `recall`'s fleet block crowded out by other projects' shared rows.
- **Two narrow gaps in the description fallback and `memq forget` (2026-09-25, from the finishing review of `docs/archive/claude-kit_persona-memory-port_spec_v1.md`).** First, `frontmatterDescription` in `plugins/claude-kit/scripts/memq.js` runs `unquoteScalar` over a value `frontmatterSite` has already unquoted when it came from the harness's `metadata:` map. So a description whose own text is wholly wrapped in quotes reads back whole at the top level and loses those quotes once the harness moves the line under `metadata:`. No test covers a quoted value under the map. Repair shape: unquote only a value read off a top-level line. Second, on `forget`'s sweep path, where index lines remain but the record file is already gone, `deleteSharedRecord` exits 1 without returning true, so `forgetHostLine` never prints. A record unlinked by hand earlier may still have a host row, and the verb says nothing about it. Repair shape: print the host-row line after any confirmed run that removed an index line. Signal: a description that loses its quotes, or a sweep-path `forget` whose host row outlives it.
- **Install schema version 6 on the memory host, then check that `memq judged` returns only the calling persona's own records (2026-09-25, operator handoff from `docs/archive/claude-kit_persona-memory-port_spec_v1.md`).** The installer run is the operator's, under `sa`, with `SQLCMDPASSWORD` set in that shell: `pwsh -NoProfile -File D:/claude-kit/plugins/claude-kit/db/Install-MemoryDatabase.ps1 -Server <the server value in ~/.claude/kit-memory-db.json> -Database KitMemory -Login sa`, from a checkout holding the merged change, with forward slashes in the path. Run it twice. The second run reports `0 changed`. Then run `memq judged --situation "<any line>"` from a persona's launch directory with a Jev configuration present, and read stderr with stdout. Every stdout line should name a record of that persona's own store. A line naming another persona's private record reopens the plan's section 1. An empty stdout with a stderr line naming the judge means the configuration, not the cut. Until the install, every `memq judged` call stands down by name, and the persona plugin's companion plan cannot start. Signal: the install is done, or the companion plan is next in the queue.
